L282: ## 6.2.1.1. Design, Pool A
L283: The control arm for Pool A was from Study ATLAS-INH (EFC14768) and is described below first. The treatment arm, which used fitusiran AT-DR, was from the extension Study ATLAS-OLE (LTE15174) and is described second.
L284: ## **ATLAS-INH (EFC14768)**
L285: ATLAS-INH (EFC14768) was a multicenter, randomized, open-label study in male subjects aged ≥12 years, with hemophilia A or B and inhibitory antibodies to factor VIII (FVIII) or factor IX (FIX), who were not receiving prophylactic therapy. The study design is shown in Figure 9. An open-label study design was considered acceptable by the review team, as blinding would not be feasible given differences in treatment regimens and differences in treatment for breakthrough bleeds for each study arm. Subjects were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to either fitusiran 80 mg administered SC as prophylaxis once monthly or on-demand BPAs. Fitusiran-treated subjects
L286: could also receive on-demand BPAs for treatment of breakthrough bleeding episodes. On-demand use of BPAs was defined as the use of these agents, as needed, for episodic bleeding, and not as a regular regimen intended to prevent spontaneous bleeding. For subjects in the BPA on-demand arm (i.e., those not receiving fitusiran), bleeding management therapy with BPAs was managed based on the local standard practice for treating hemophilia subjects with inhibitors, as routinely administered by the physician. Randomization was stratified by the number of bleeding episodes in the 6 months prior to screening (\le 10 versus &gt;10). All subjects were treated for 9 months, and upon completion, eligible for participation in an open-label extension study (Study LTE15174). See a detailed description of the study design and bleed management guidelines in Section 15.1.
L287: **Figure 9. Study Design, EFC14768**
L288: Source: Applicant's Clinical Study Protocol for ATLAS-INH
L289: a On-demand local BPA as routinely prescribed by physician per local standard practice.
L290: b Fitusiran treatment arm subjects who do not enroll in the extension study: AT activity level was monitored at monthly intervals following the final fitusiran dose until activity levels return to approximately 60% (per the central laboratory) or per Investigator discretion in consultation with the study Medical Monitor.
L291: Abbreviations: BPA, bypassing agent; HA/HB, hemophilia type A / hemophilia type B; R, randomized
L292: ## Study LTE15174 (ATLAS-OLE)
L293: Study LTE15174 (ATLAS-OLE) was a multicenter, open-label extension study in male subjects aged \ge 12 years, with hemophilia A or B, with or without inhibitory antibodies to FVIII or FIX. The study included subjects who had completed any of the fitusiran phase 3 hemophilia A or B studies (Study EFC14768, Study EFC14769, and Study EFC15110, also referred to as “parent” studies) to continue to be evaluated for long-term safety and efficacy. Additionally, this study collected efficacy data for the AT-DR. Initially, subjects received a fixed dose of fitusiran 80 mg QM. Following the dosing pause for cases of thrombotic events in October 2020, subjects were switched to the AT-DR. The estimated total time on the study for a subject who started fitusiran treatment before the 2020 dosing pause is up to 90 months, and up to 62 months for a subject who started fitusiran treatment after the lifting of the pause. Bleeding episodes were treated per Bleed Management Guidelines and management during surgery is outlined in Section 15. Use of prothrombin complex concentrates and emicizumab were not permitted during the study. A detailed study design is described in Section 15.3. For Pool A, the fitusiran-treated subjects who were used from LTE15174 were those with HAwI or HBwI who were originally randomized to fitusiran in Study EFC14768 and who received AT-DR during Study LTE15174.
L294: ## 6.2.1.2. Objectives and Endpoints, Pool A
L295: ## Objectives
L296: The primary objective was to evaluate the efficacy of fitusiran treatment at the revised AT-DR compared to on-demand treatment with BPAs, as determined by the frequency of bleeding episodes.
L297: ## Endpoints
L298: The primary efficacy endpoint was annualized bleeding rate (ABR) for treated bleeds in the fitusiran primary efficacy period compared to the control period (refer to Table 14 in Section 6.2 for the definition of analysis periods). The number of bleeding episodes was annualized for each subject using the following formula: ABR for treated bleeds = "total number of bleeding episodes"/"total number of days in the respective period" \times 365.25. Treated bleeds were defined as bleeding episodes that required administration of factor concentrates or BPA infusion for management, e.g., hemarthrosis, muscle, or mucosal bleeding.
L299: The key secondary endpoints are listed below:
L300: - Annualized treated spontaneous bleeding rate (AsBR) in the primary efficacy period and the control period.
- Annualized treated joint bleeding rate (AjBR) in the primary efficacy period and the control period.
- Change in Haemophilia Quality of Life Questionnaire for Adults (Haem-A-QoL) Physical Health domain score in the primary efficacy period and the control period.
- Time to first treated bleeding episode in the primary efficacy period and control period (this endpoint was not included in the testing hierarchy of the parent Study EFC14768).
L301: ## Haem-A-QoL Physical Health Domain Score
L302: The Haem-A-QoL Physical Health domain is a 5-item domain in the 46-item Haem-A-QoL, a patient-reported outcome (PRO) instrument. The Physical Health domain is designed to assess patient-reported hemophilia-related symptoms (painful swellings and presence of joint pain) and physical functioning (pain with movement and difficulty walking far). Each item is rated on a 5-point verbal rating scale ranging from “Never” to “All the time.” The recall period is the previous 4 weeks. The Haem-A-QoL was administered by paper at baseline, and month 9.
L303: Endpoints for Study EFC14768 are described in Section 15.1.
L304: ## 6.2.1.3. Eligibility Criteria, Pool A
L305: Eligibility criteria for Study EFC14768 are described below. Study LTE15174 included subjects who completed a previous parent phase 3 clinical study of fitusiran and had central AT activity level \ge 22\% (prior to dosing).
L306: ## **Study EFC14768**
L307: ## **Key Inclusion Criteria**
L308: - Males \ge 12 years of age.
- Severe hemophilia A or B with inhibitors evidenced by:A central laboratory measurement or documented medical record evidence of FVIII &lt;1\% or FIX level \le 2\% at Screening.
- On-demand use of BPAs to manage bleeding episodes for at least the last 6 months prior to Screening, and meeting one of the following Nijmegen-modified Bethesda assay results criteria:Inhibitor titer of \ge 0.6 BU/mL at Screening, or
- Inhibitor titer of &lt;0.6 BU/mL at Screening with medical record evidence of 2 consecutive titers \ge 0.6 BU/mL, or
- Inhibitor titer of &lt;0.6 BU/mL at Screening with medical record evidence of anamnestic response.
- A minimum of 6 bleeding episodes requiring BPA treatment within the last 6 months prior to Screening.
L309: ## **Key Exclusion Criteria**
L310: - Known coexisting bleeding disorders other than hemophilia A or B, e.g., Von Willebrand's disease, additional factor deficiencies, or platelet disorders.
- Current participation in immune tolerance induction therapy.
- Current use of BPAs as regularly administered prophylaxis designed to prevent spontaneous bleeding episodes.
- AT activity &lt;60\% at Screening, as determined by central laboratory measurement, in order to exclude patients with underlying congenital AT deficiency and to decrease the risk of excessively low AT activity levels.
- Presence of clinically significant liver disease.
- Estimated glomerular filtration rate \le 45 mL/min/1.73 m^2 (using the Modification of Diet in Renal Disease formula).
- Coexisting thrombophilic disorder.
- History of arterial or venous thromboembolism.
L311: ## **6.2.1.4. Statistical Analysis Plan, Pool A**
L312: ## **Analysis of the Primary Endpoint**
L313: The primary endpoint was analyzed using a negative binomial model with the number of bleeding episodes as the response variable, and treatment arm and randomization strata of number of bleeds in the 6 months prior to study (\le 10, &gt;10) as fixed effects. The logarithm of the
L314: duration (in years) that each subject spent in each study period matching the bleeding episode data being analyzed (i.e., treatment period from Study EFC15174 and control period from Study EFC14768) were included as an offset variable to account for unequal follow-up time due to different durations of the treatment and control periods, early withdrawal, surgery, etc.
L315: ## Analysis of Key Secondary Endpoints
L316: ## **Annualized Treated Spontaneous Bleeding Rate**
L317: The same primary analysis method for the primary endpoint was used for this key secondary endpoint.
L318: ## **Annualized Treated Joint Bleeding Rate**
L319: The same primary analysis method for the primary endpoint was used for this key secondary endpoint.
L320: ## **Change in Haem-A-QoL Physical Health Domain Score**
L321: Change from baseline in Haem-A-QoL Physical Health domain score (in subjects \ge 17 years of age) in the control period and primary efficacy period were analyzed using a mixed model for repeated measure analysis (MMRM) with robust sandwich covariance matrix, with fixed effects, visit of study period (one postbaseline in control period and two postbaselines in primary efficacy period), the randomization strata of number of bleeding episodes in the 6 months prior to EFC14768 study entry, and baseline Haem-A-QoL Physical Health domain score. We show the Haem-A-QoL Physical Health results for completeness but do not consider the findings reliable for reasons discussed later in this review.
L322: ## **Time to First Treated Bleeding Episode**
L323: The risk of bleeding in the primary efficacy period and control period was compared using a Cox proportional hazards regression model. For intent-to-treat (ITT) Pool A, treatment arm and randomization strata of number of bleeding episodes (\le 10, &gt; 10) in the 6 months prior to EFC14768 study entry were fixed effects.
L324: ## **Multiple Testing Strategy**
L325: To control the familywise error rate at an alpha level of 0.05 in the testing of the primary and key secondary endpoints for each pool, a hierarchical testing approach was used. For ITT pool A, the endpoints were tested in the following order (Table 16 below):
L326: **Table 16. Multiplicity Adjustment, ITT, Pool A**
L327: Endpoints	2-Sided Alpha
1. ABR for treated bleeds in the primary efficacy period and control period	0.05
2. Annualized treated spontaneous bleeding rate in the primary efficacy period and control period	0.05
3. Annualized treated joint bleeding rate in the primary efficacy period and control period	0.05
4. Change in Haem-A-QoL Physical Health domain score in the primary efficacy period and control period	0.05
5. Time to first treated bleeding episode in the primary efficacy period and control period	0.05
L328: Source: FDA reviewer summary
L329: Abbreviations: ABR, annualized bleeding rate; Haem-A-QoL, Haemophilia Quality of Life Questionnaire for Adults; ITT, intent-to-treat
L330: If testing of any of the endpoints was not statistically significant, formal testing of subsequent endpoints would be stopped and the null hypothesis for subsequent tests would not be rejected.
L331: Note that Endpoints #1 to #4 in Table 9 were prespecified primary and key secondary endpoints in the protocol of the parent study EFC147689. However, the endpoint of the time to first treated bleeding episode (Endpoint #5) in the primary efficacy period and control period was not prespecified as a key secondary endpoint. Therefore, the review team does not consider it as a prespecified multiplicity-adjusted endpoint for the Pool A analysis. As a result, the endpoint of time to first treated bleeding episode in the primary efficacy period will not be used to support a labeling claim of fitusiran AT-DR.
L332: ## **Handling of Intercurrent Events and Missing Data**
L333: Inclusion of bleeding events during the intercurrent events, such as perioperative period, after fitusiran treatment discontinuation, or use of other medications influencing hemostasis (Table 17) could significantly bias the evaluation of the effect of fitusiran and make treatment comparisons difficult to interpret. To avoid potential selection bias, a combination of on-treatment and hypothetical strategies were used to deal with intercurrent events (Table 18).
L334: **Table 17. Intercurrent Events**
L335: Event	Description
I. Dose interruption periods	
a) If the gap between the last dose date of the parent study (EFC14768/EFC14769/EFC15110) on a new dose regimen and the first dose date of the extension study (LTE15174) was >84 days: exclude events occurring on dates starting from the date of the last dose in the parent study +29 days, up to the date of the first dose in Study LTE15174+28 days. For subjects whose last dose of the parent study/dose of the extension study was on a 50 mg Q2M schedule, the “28 days” adjustment was replaced with 56 days. The period of >84 days was prespecified to represent 2 consecutive missed doses (56 days) with one additional dosing interval (28 days). Such a pause in dosing was expected to result in antithrombin (AT) recovery to a level that would no longer reflect the efficacy of the drug.
b) Gaps from eDiary deactivation date in Study EFC15110 to eDiary reactivation date in LTE15174. This was only applicable to Pool C.
c) Permanent fitusiran treatment discontinuation during the dose adjustment period due to low AT level:
Permanent fitusiran treatment discontinuation at 50 mg Q2M.
Permanent fitusiran treatment discontinuation at 20 mg Q2M.
d) Permanent fitusiran treatment discontinuation during the primary efficacy period: Exclude events occurring on any dates starting from the date of the last dose within the primary efficacy period +29 days for on-treatment strategy. Permanent fitusiran treatment discontinuation during the dose adjustment period due to reasons other than low AT level. For subjects whose last dose was on 50 mg/20 mg Q2M schedule, the “29 days” adjustment was replaced with 57 days.
e) Gaps from eDiary deactivation date to reactivation date for subjects who had no eDiary available during the re-enrollment period from core study to low-dose cohort in LTE15174 study.
f) Gap in dosing at the original dose regimen (ODR) of 80 mg QM due to operational reasons prior to enrolling into the LTE15174 at the ODR. (Note: this is not applicable to the primary analysis).

II. Perioperative period of major surgery	The perioperative period of major surgery and major trauma was defined as the day of the surgery or trauma through the final day on which supplemental hemostatic or antithrombotic treatments were administered as part of the perioperative treatment plan.
III. Perioperative period of minor surgery	The perioperative period of minor surgery was defined like the perioperative period of major surgery (see above) or to 72 hours from the end of surgery, whichever was later.
IV. Period of Antithrombin Treatment	Defined as the first day of antithrombin treatment through the final day on which antithrombin or other anticoagulant therapies were administered +5 half-lives.
V. After treatment with emicizumab (Hemlibra®)	Prophylactic treatments with factor/BPA usage were excluded from this intercurrent event due to two considerations: factor/BPA use on any day during the perioperative period were allowed per protocol and this period was already accounted for in II and III; any prophylactic usage of factor/BPA current with fitusiran treatment outside of the perioperative period for Pool A & B or outside of the perioperative period and control period for Pool C was prohibited.
L336: Event	Description
VI. Subjects exit program	Subjects exit the program before Dose Restart Day 1 of fitusiran with a new dose regimen. This includes subjects who withdraw from the parent study or extension study before Dose Restart Day 1, and subjects who completed the parent study without any exposure to the new dose regimen and did not roll over to the extension study.
L337: Source: Summary of Clinical Efficacy Hemophilia A and B, Table 5.
L338: Abbreviations: BPA, bypassing agent; Q2M, every 2 months; QM, once monthly
L339: The primary analysis estimand was based on a combination of on treatment and hypothetical strategies.
L340: For subjects who were followed up in either the control or treatment period, the on-treatment strategy was considered of greatest relevance in assessing the primary objective. Bleeding episodes during intercurrent events I(a), I(b), I(d), I(e), and II through V were excluded from this analysis.
L341: The hypothetical strategy for subjects who received at least one dose of the AT-DR but permanently discontinued treatment during the dose adjustment period due to low AT level (intercurrent event I(c)) was used to address the scientific question concerning the outcomes if these subjects had continued the treatment and been followed until the end of the primary efficacy period. The current clinical development plan under the AT-DR was to evaluate the safety and efficacy when the AT level was between 15 and 35%. Per protocol, subjects who had two AT levels below 15% were required to de-escalate to a lower dose or permanently discontinue fitusiran treatment to mitigate vascular thrombotic risk. Available clinical data suggested that lower AT levels were associated with improved ABR. For those subjects who permanently discontinued fitusiran treatment due to AT levels below 15%, directly using bleeding data from such subjects may create a bias to artificially improve the overall fitusiran ABR. Rather, it was hypothesized that those subjects may have an ABR similar to other subjects with AT levels at 15 to 35%. Therefore, the approach of missing at random was adopted.
L342: The hypothetical strategy for subjects who exit the program before Restart Day 1 of fitusiran with the AT-DR (intercurrent event VI) was considered to address the scientific question concerning the outcomes if these subjects had remained in the program, received the new regimen, and were followed until the end of the primary efficacy period.
L343: **Table 18. Strategies to Address Intercurrent Events of the Estimand of the Primary Analysis**
L344: Intercurrent Events	Strategy
I(a), I(b), I(d), I(e), II to V	On-treatment
I(c), VI	Hypothetical
L345: Source: ISE SAP, Section 2.7.1.1
L346: Abbreviations: ISE, integrated summary of effectiveness; SAP, statistical analysis plan
L347: ## **Handling of Missing Data in the Primary Analysis**
L348: For subjects who received at least one dose of fitusiran after dose pause and permanently discontinued treatment during the dose adjustment period, if the permanent treatment discontinuation was not due to low AT level, the dose adjustment period was counted as the primary efficacy period, available bleeding episode data starting from the Dose Restart Day 1 visit to end of study was used, and intercurrent events I(a), I(b), I(d), I(e), and II to V (Table 17) would apply.
L349: No imputation of missing data in the control period was performed for subjects who withdrew in the control period.
L350: For the primary efficacy period, unobserved data due to intercurrent event I(c) and VI were addressed as follows:
L351: - For subjects who received no dose of fitusiran under the AT-DR but at least one dose of fitusiran (i.e., 80 mg QM) before dose pause, or who received no dose of fitusiran under either the initial regimen (i.e., 80 mg QM) or AT-DR, the unobserved bleeding data during the primary efficacy period (Dose Restart Day 169 to Dose Restart Day 364) were imputed via a multiple imputation (MI) method with a missing at random (MAR) assumption based on observed bleeding data from Dose Restart Day 169 to Dose Restart Day 364 for subjects who were randomized to the fitusiran arm in EFC14768/EFC14769 study or exposed to fitusiran in EFC15110 study, and also dosed after the dose adjustment period, with on-treatment strategy applied to intercurrent events I(a), I(b), I(d), I(e), II to V.
- For subjects who permanently discontinued fitusiran during the dose adjustment period due to low AT level, the unobserved bleeding data during the primary efficacy period were imputed via MI method with MAR assumption based on observed bleeding data from the primary efficacy period for subjects who were randomized to the fitusiran arm in EFC14768/EFC14769 study or exposed to fitusiran in EFC15110 study, and also dosed with 20 mg of fitusiran during the primary efficacy period, with on-treatment strategy applied to intercurrent events I(a), I(b), I(d), I(e), II to V.
L352: ## 6.2.1.5. Results of Analyses, Pool A
L353: ## Subject Disposition
L354: ITT Pool A was comprised of fitusiran-treated subjects in Study LTE15174 and on-demand BPA-treated subjects in Study EFC14768 maintaining the original randomization either to fitusiran or on-demand BPA. Thirty-five (92.1%) subjects were enrolled and entered the fitusiran prophylaxis arm in the treatment period (dose resumption Day 1 to Day 364). There were 31 (81.6%) subjects who completed the study period and 4 (10.5%) who did not. Four (10.5%) subjects discontinued the study because more than 1 AT measured &lt;15% (Table 19).

L364: The small sample size of HBwI subjects (N=12) compared to HAwI subjects (N=45) is expected as HA is more common than HB, with a prevalence of one in 5,000 male live births compared to one in 30,000, respectively (Castaman and Matino 2019).
L365: The majority of subjects enrolled in Pool A (nearly 90%) were not from the United States. This is expected, as standard of care in the United States has shifted to use of prophylaxis factor replacement for bleeding, rather than on-demand factor therapy. The efficacy outcomes in ex-U.S. patients are expected to be the same as U.S. patients, as there are no known demographic factors that should alter the response to study drug. In addition, when treating patients with hemophilia, clinical practice can vary; however, the pathophysiology of hemophilia does not differ based on country. Therefore, based on the mechanism of action of fitusiran, there is no rationale for a difference in efficacy based on race or geographic region.
L366: Additional baseline characteristics are described in Section 16.1.
L367: ## **Analysis of the Primary Endpoint**
L368: Pool A met its primary efficacy objective of superiority of fitusiran prophylaxis over BPA on demand in reducing ABR for treated bleeds. The estimated ABR was 19.1 (95% CI: 11.8, 31.0) for the BPA on demand group and 5.1 (95% CI: 2.7, 9.5) for the fitusiran prophylaxis (AT-DR) group, with a rate ratio of 0.27 (95% CI: 0.13, 0.57; p=0.0006). Results for the primary efficacy endpoint analysis are summarized in Table 21. There was no pre-planned formal comparison between the fitusiran 80-mg dose and the AT-DR. The parent study efficacy results for the 80 mg QM dose are also included in the table for reference. The Applicant's primary analysis results were confirmed by the FDA statistical review team.

The results from these sensitivity analyses showed that the primary efficacy results in Pool A were robust (Table 22). These trends of the associated rate ratio with 95% CI were consistent with the results of the primary analysis, especially with the copy reference approach, which used a conservative control-based imputation for missing data in the treatment arm.

L406: In addition to a nonsignificant p-value, the FDA review team had concerns about the interpretability for the PRO data of Haem-A-QOL Physical Health domain score given the lack of baseline values and other limitations. For details, please refer to Section 16.5. The fourth key secondary endpoint of the time to first treated bleeding episode should be considered exploratory only, since the prior endpoint in the testing hierarchy, Haem-A-QOL Physical Health domain score, failed to yield a significant p-value. Further, this endpoint was not proposed in the parent study EFC14768. In addition, while it is clearly beneficial to reduce the number of treated bleeds, it is not clear whether reducing the time to first treated bleed is a clinically meaningful endpoint.

L408: ## Subgroup Analysis of the Primary Endpoints
L409: Subgroup analyses were conducted to assess the potential for differences in the treatment effect of fitusiran prophylaxis compared to BPA on-demand across various demographic and clinical characteristic groups. Overall, the treatment effect was consistent across baseline subgroups, including hemophilia type, prior bleeding events, age group, race, and ethnicity. Of note, these subgroup analyses are limited by the small numbers of subjects, so the results should be interpreted with caution.
L410: **Figure 10. Forest Plot of Estimated Annualized Bleeding Rate Ratio (95% CI) by Subgroups, ITT, Pool A**
L411: Source: Summary of Clinical Efficacy Hemophilia A and B, Figure 15; Statistical Review team's analysis.
L412: N1 represent number of subjects in control period. N2 represent number of subjects in primary efficacy period.
L413: Abbreviations: BPA, bypassing agent; ITT, intent-to-treat; RR, rate ratio
L414: The subgroup analyses described above should be interpreted with caution given the small sample size. For example, the CI for HBwI population is wide and crosses 1; however, based on the mechanism of action of the drug (which acts independent of factors VIII and IX) and the pathophysiology of the disease, it is not expected for patients with HAwI and HBwI to respond differently to fitusiran. Likely, the difference in ABR is due to small sample size in the subgroup (N=9). With less than 10% of data from U.S. subjects (i.e., 5/57), the mean rate ratio (RR; 95% CI) was not estimable for this geographic region, although the observed mean (\pmSD) ABR for treated bleeds on fitusiran for the five U.S. subjects in the ITT Pool A was similar to the overall population: 4.8 (5.53) versus 4.4 (5.45).

L422: ## 6.2.2.1. Design, Pool B
L423: The control arm for Pool B was from Study ATLAS-A/B (EFC14769) described below. The treatment arm was from the extension Study ATLAS-OLE (LTE15174). For Pool B, the fitusiran-treated subjects who were included from LTE15174 were those with hemophilia A or B without inhibitors who were originally randomized to fitusiran in Study EFC14769 and who received AT-DR during Study LTE15174.
L424: ATLAS-A/B (EFC14769) was a multicenter, randomized, open-label study in male subjects aged ≥12 years, with hemophilia A or B without inhibitory antibodies to FVIII or FIX, who were not receiving prophylactic therapy. The study design is shown in Figure 11. Subjects were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to either fitusiran 80 mg SC as prophylaxis once monthly or to on-demand factor concentrates. The study allowed use of on-demand factor concentrates in the fitusiran-treated subjects for treatment of breakthrough bleeding episodes. On-demand use of factor concentrates is defined as the use of these agents, as needed, for episodic bleeding, and not on a regular regimen intended to prevent spontaneous bleeding. For subjects in the fitusiran treatment arm who developed breakthrough bleeding episodes, bleeding management guidelines were followed as outlined in Section 15. Randomization was stratified by the number of bleeding episodes in the 6 months prior to Screening (≤10 versus &gt;10) and hemophilia type (hemophilia A or B). All subjects were treated for 9 months and upon completion, eligible for participation in an open-label extension study. See a detailed description of the study design in Section 15.2.
L425: Figure 11. Study Design, EFC14769
L426: Source: Applicant's Clinical Study Protocol for EFC14769
L427: a On-demand factor concentrates as routinely prescribed by physician per local standard practice.
L428: b Fitusiran treatment arm subjects who do not enroll in the extension study: AT activity level was monitored at monthly intervals following the final fitusiran dose until activity levels return to approximately 60% (per the central laboratory) or per Investigator discretion in consultation with the study Medical Monitor.
L429: Abbreviations: AT, antithrombin; HA/HB, hemophilia type A / hemophilia type B; R, randomized
L430: The Study design for Study ATLAS-OLE (LTE15174) is described above in Section 6.2.1.1.
L431: ## 6.2.2.2. Objectives and Endpoints, Pool B
L432: ## Objectives
L433: The primary objective was to evaluate the efficacy of fitusiran treatment with the revised AT-DR compared to factor on demand, as determined by the frequency of bleeding episodes.
L434: The secondary objective was to evaluate the efficacy of fitusiran compared to factor on demand, as determined by the frequency of treated spontaneous bleeding episodes, the frequency of treated joint bleeding episodes, health-related quality of life in subjects \ge17 years of age, and time to first treated bleeding episode.
L435: ## Endpoints
L436: The primary efficacy endpoint was ABR of treated bleeds in the fitusiran primary efficacy period compared to the control period (Table 14).
L437: The key secondary endpoints are listed below:
L438: - Annualized treated spontaneous bleeding rate (AsBR).
- Annualized treated joint bleeding rate (AjBR).
- Change in Haemophilia Quality of Life Questionnaire for Adults (Haem-A-QoL) Physical Health domain score. This instrument is described in Section 6.2.1.2.(b) (4)
- Time to first treated bleeding episode (not proposed in parent Study EFC14769).
L439: Endpoints for Study EFC14769 are described in Section 15.2.
L440: ## 6.2.2.3. Eligibility Criteria, Pool B
L441: Eligibility criteria for Study EFC14769 are described below. Study LTE15174 included subjects who completed a previous parent phase 3 clinical study of fitusiran and had central AT activity level \ge 22\% (prior to dosing).
L442: ## ATLAS-A/B (EFC14769)
L443: ## **Key Inclusion Criteria**
L444: - Males \ge 12 years of age.
- Severe hemophilia A or B without inhibitors evidenced by:A central laboratory measurement or documented medical record evidence of FVIII &lt;1\% or FIX level \le 2\% at Screening.
- On-demand use of factor concentrate to manage bleeding episodes for at least the last 6 months prior to Screening, and meeting each of the following criterion:Nijmegen modified Bethesda assay inhibitor titer of &lt;0.6 BU/mL at Screening.
- No use of BPAs to treat bleeding episodes for at least the last 6 months prior to Screening.
- No history of immune tolerance induction therapy within the last 3 years prior to Screening.
- A minimum of 6 bleeding episodes requiring factor concentrate treatment within the last 6 months prior to Screening.
L445: ## **Key Exclusion Criteria**
L446: Key exclusion criteria are the same as Pool A as described in Section 6.2.1.3.
L447: ## 6.2.2.4. Statistical Analysis Plan, Pool B
L448: ## Analysis of the Primary Endpoint
L449: The primary endpoint was analyzed using a negative binomial model with the number of bleeding episodes as the response variable, and randomization arm, the randomization strata of number of bleeding episodes in the 6 months prior (\le 10, &gt;10), and hemophilia type as fixed effects. The logarithm of the duration (in years) that each subject spent in each study period matching their bleeding episode data (i.e., treatment period from Study EFC15174 and control period from EFC14769) was included as an offset variable to account for unequal follow-up time due to different durations of the treatment and control periods, early withdrawal, surgery, etc.
L450: ## Analysis of Key Secondary Endpoints
L451: ## **Annualized Treated Spontaneous Bleeding Rate**
L452: The same primary analysis for the primary endpoint was used for this key secondary endpoint.
L453: ## Annualized Treated Joint Bleeding Rate
L454: The same primary analysis for the primary endpoint was used for this key secondary endpoint.
L455: ## Change in Haem-A-QoL Physical Health Domain Score
L456: Change from baseline in Haem-A-QoL Physical Health domain score (in subjects \ge 17 years of age) in the control period and primary efficacy period was analyzed using an MMRM with robust sandwich covariance matrix, with fixed effects, visit of study period (one postbaseline in control period and two postbaselines in primary efficacy period), randomization strata of number of bleeding episodes in the 6 months prior to EFC14769 study entry, randomization strata of hemophilia type, and baseline Haem-A-QoL Physical Health domain score. We show the Haem-A-QoL Physical Health results for completeness but do not consider the findings reliable because of several limitations discussed in Section 16.5.
L457: ## Time to First Treated Bleeding Episode
L458: The risk of bleeding in the primary efficacy period and control period was compared using a Cox proportional hazards regression model. For ITT Pool B, treatment arm and randomization strata of number of bleeding episodes in the 6 months prior to EFC14769 study entry were fixed effects.
L459: ## Multiple Testing Strategy
L460: To control the familywise error rate at the alpha level of 0.05 in the testing of the primary and key secondary endpoints for each pool, a hierarchical testing approach was used. For ITT Pool B, the endpoints were tested in the order based on primary analysis of the endpoints as described in Table 10. If testing of any of the endpoints was not statistically significant, formal testing of subsequent endpoints would be stopped and the null hypothesis for subsequent tests would not be rejected.
L461: ## Handling of Intercurrent Events and Missing data
L462: The handling method of intercurrent events and missing data for Pool B were the same as described in Pool A. See Section 6.2.1.4 for further details.

L481: ## **Analysis of the Primary Endpoint**
L482: The study demonstrated a statistically significant reduction in ABR for treated bleeds with fitusiran prophylaxis compared to factor on-demand treatment in the primary efficacy period. The estimated ABR was 31.4 (95% CI: 20.5, 48.2) for the factor on-demand group and 9.0 (95% CI: 5.6, 14.5) for fitusiran prophylaxis with AT-DR. Results for the primary efficacy endpoint analysis are summarized in **Table 30**. There was no pre-planned formal comparison of ABR for treated bleeds between subjects who received fitusiran 80 mg prophylaxis and AT-DR fitusiran prophylaxis. The comparison results of 80 mg QM vs. factor on demand from the parent study are also presented in the table for reference. The Applicant's primary analysis results were confirmed by the FDA statistical review team.

L526: ## 6.3. Key Efficacy Review Issues
L527: ## 6.3.1. Demonstration of Benefit of Fitusiran:
## Antithrombin-Based Dose Regimen Efficacy Data
## From the Long-Term Extension Study Compared
## to Control Data From Randomized Parent Studies
L528: ## Issue
L529: The parent randomized controlled phase 3 studies were nearly completed when the Applicant switched to the AT-DR, the to-be-marketed dose of fitusiran. To assess the efficacy of the AT-DR, the Applicant compared the efficacy data from the long-term extension study (LTE15174), which implemented the AT-DR, to the control arms of the parent studies (EFC14768 and
L530: EFC14769). The review team examined whether this approach is adequate to establish the effectiveness of the AT-DR.
L531: ## **Background**
L532: Dosing in the phase 3 studies, EFC14768, EFC14769, and EFC15110 (referred to as parent studies in the rest of the section), was initiated with fitusiran at 80 mg QM. To mitigate the risk of thrombotic events, the AT-DR targeting an AT activity range of 15 to 35% was implemented when the parent studies were nearly completed. The AT-DR was fully implemented in the extension study, LTE15174.
L533: The efficacy of fitusiran AT-DR treatment was assessed by comparing the fitusiran AT-DR treatment data from the long-term extension study LTE15174 to the control data from the parent studies (see Figure 7). This approach was discussed during the IND stage of development. At the Type C Guidance Meeting on September 18, 2023, and in a May 15, 2024, information request, the Agency raised the concern whether the time lag (approximately 2 years) between the parent studies and the extension study LTE15174 introduces additional confounding factors and ultimately reduces the confidence that this approach can establish effectiveness for the proposed AT-DR for fitusiran.
L534: ## **Assessment**
L535: The Applicant and the FDA clinical and statistical review team conducted thorough evaluations to assess the comparability between the control arm data from the parent studies and the fitusiran AT-DR data from the extension study.
L536: ## **Pathophysiology**
L537: The pathophysiology of severe hemophilia allows for a comparison between control and intervention groups across time. Congenital severe hemophilia typically presents in infancy and throughout the lifespan of a given patient. The clinical hallmark of severe hemophilia is spontaneous bleeding that requires lifelong treatment, including both prophylaxis and on-demand therapies for breakthrough bleeding events. In the control on-demand group, episodic treatment with CFC or BPA does not alter the course of the disease, and for that reason, the individual bleeding phenotype is not anticipated to change in the absence of prophylactic intervention over the study duration. Therefore, the use of the control group in the parent studies is adequate and not impacted by the time lag.
L538: ## **Baseline Characteristics**
L539: The parent studies' baseline characteristics, including medical and surgical history at time of enrollment, concomitant medications, and prior hemophilia-directed therapies (e.g., CFC/BPA, prohibited medications, bleed management, etc.), were comparable between the active and control arms. No significant changes were observed in reported medical history or concomitant medications within the relatively short timeframe in which subjects were enrolled in the fitusiran parent and extension clinical studies. Bleed management guidelines were followed in all studies.
L540: ## Carryover Benefit From Prior Treatment With Fitusiran (i.e., Fitusiran Original Dosing Regimen of 80 mg QM)
L541: ## Influence From Prior Exposure to Fitusiran
L542: In the extension study LTE15174, among 11 participants who were never exposed to the original dose regimen, the estimated mean (95% CI) annualized bleed rate (ABR) for treated bleeds was lower than the overall AT-DR participants [1.49 (0.58, 3.85) vs. 6.41 (5.31, 7.74)] with nonoverlapped CI, suggesting that there was no positive influence from prior exposure to the original dose regimen for the estimated efficacy for AT-DR. In addition, the primary efficacy assessment occurred after a 6-month period in which study participants restarted fitusiran, targeting AT levels of 15-35%. By the end of the 6-month period, the majority of study participants reached AT-steady state and were on a stable dose. Therefore, as participants were on a steady state of fitusiran, it is unlikely that the prior exposure to fitusiran at the 80mg fixed dose had an influence on efficacy outcomes.
L543: ## Imbalanced Dropout Rate Between Treatment Arms
L544: The dropout rate is 15% to 22% higher in the fitusiran AT-DR treatment arm from the extension study LTE15174 in subjects originally randomized to fitusiran in the parent studies compared to the control arm from parent studies. The time lag between the extension study and the parent studies may have a considerable contribution to the imbalanced dropout rate between arms (please refer to the disposition tables for Pools A, B and C in Section 6.2 for further information). Per the FDA statistical review team's recommendation, the Applicant conducted several sensitivity analyses for ABR in Pools A and B to impute the missing data due to different reasons, including a copy reference approach, which is a conservative control-based imputation that imputes unobserved bleeding data during the primary efficacy period (Dose Restart Day 169 to Dose Restart Day 364) using the control period data distribution in the corresponding parent study. These analyses were consistent with the primary analysis (for details, see the sensitivity analysis results for the primary endpoint in Section 6.2.1.5 and 6.2.2.5), showing that the primary efficacy results for the primary efficacy endpoint ABR for treated bleeds are robust. The missing data and dropouts did not affect the final efficacy conclusion. Additional reassurance is provided by the large treatment effect size in which the p-value of the primary efficacy endpoint (ABR for treated bleeds) was &lt;0.001, for both subjects with and without inhibitors.
L545: ## Impact of Unknown and Known Confounders
L546: ## Randomization
L547: Although known confounders appear to be generally balanced between the two treatment arms in the pooled data (please refer to the baseline demographics and clinical characteristics tables in Section 6.2), unknown confounders can only be balanced through randomization. To balance the treatment and control groups for the unknown confounders, the Applicant followed the FDA statistical review team's recommendation to maintain the original randomization used in the parent studies for the pooled analyses, i.e., the fitusiran new dose regimen arm should only include subjects who were randomized to the fitusiran arm in the parent study. The efficacy analyses were conducted according to the intent-to-treat (ITT) principle, preserving the randomization in the parent studies.
L548: ## Intrasubject Comparison
L549: Unknown confounders were further evaluated in a supplementary analysis using intrasubject comparison where the individual serves as their own control. For subjects who were randomized to the control arm in the parent study EFC14768 or EFC14769 and received at least one dose of fitusiran under the revised AT-DR in LTE15174, their fitusiran AT-DR treatment data from the extension study LTE15174 were compared with their own control data from the parent studies (Table 36). The results of the analysis reached a conclusion consistent with the primary analysis.

L564: ## Conclusion
L565: In summary, the review team determined that the approach of comparing efficacy data from the long-term extension study which used the AT-DR, to the control arms in the parent studies was acceptable for establishing the effectiveness of the AT-DR.
L566: ## 7. Safety (Risk and Risk Management)
L567: ## 7.1. Potential Risks or Safety Concerns Based on Nonclinical Data
L568: The safety of fitusiran was assessed in good laboratory practice repeat-dose toxicology studies in healthy animals (rats and monkeys) and in animal models of hemophilia (mice, dogs, and monkeys). Animal studies were conducted using the subcutaneous route of administration, which is consistent with the intended clinical route. To ensure continuous exposure and account for differences in tissue exposure half-lives, animals were generally dosed more often (once weekly) than the clinical dosing schedule (once per month or once every other month). Small interfering RNA drugs, including fitusiran, show prolonged tissue residence times (e.g., weeks to months), but have short intermittent plasma exposure (e.g., hours). Because of this, the relationship of fitusiran blood exposures (i.e., AUC) to tissue levels remains uncertain. To account for this, animal-to-human safety margins described herein are based on dose comparisons (instead of plasma exposures), which have been corrected to reflect the total fitusiran dose over time (i.e., mg/kg/month) and further adjusted for body surface area (BSA) differences between species.
L569: Fitusiran was pharmacologically active in the common laboratory strains of rats and monkeys that were utilized in standard toxicology studies. Because these species exhibit normal clotting ability at baseline, toxicity studies with fitusiran in healthy animals were anticipated to overestimate clotting risk to hemophilia patients. However, such studies are still anticipated to provide valuable information, especially for off-target safety assessment. To address the limitations of studies in healthy animals, the Applicant conducted extensive studies in animal models of hemophilia, which provided a more relevant physiological system with which to assess on-target risk for patients with hemophilia. Off-target risk was assessed in both healthy animals and hemophilia animal models. Key nonclinical safety findings and their potential clinical relevance are discussed below (and we refer to the detailed nonclinical study summaries in Section 13.1 of this review).
L570: ## Exaggerated Pharmacology/Thrombosis in Healthy Animals
L571: A major risk of fitusiran is anticipated to be excessive clotting related to exaggerated pharmacology. To begin to characterize this risk, healthy rats were subcutaneously administered fitusiran at up to 1 mg/kg/week for 26 weeks (representing doses below the maximum recommended human dose [MRHD] based on BSA extrapolation). Deaths due to systemic thrombosis occurred at 1 mg/kg/week, a dose that decreased AT protein levels by up to 80% (AT activity not measured). Lower doses resulting in AT protein reductions up to 70% were tolerated. In a shorter, 7-week study, rats were given up to 3 mg/kg/week (representing 2 times the MRHD based on BSA comparisons). The 3 mg/kg/week dose was not tolerated due to systemic clotting, where AT activity and protein levels were decreased up to 91% and 83%, respectively; lower doses that reduced AT activity by up to 88% and AT protein up to 82% were tolerated.
L572: In monkeys with normal coagulation at baseline, fitusiran administration at up to 0.5 mg/kg/week (representing a dose below the MRHD based on BSA comparisons) for 39 weeks decreased AT protein and activity up to 74% and 71%, respectively, and all doses were well-tolerated. Fitusiran administered to monkeys for 7 weeks at up to 1 mg/kg/week (representing approximately 2 times the MRHD based on BSA comparisons) decreased AT activity and protein by up to 83% and 84%, respectively, and deaths occurred that were attributed to systemic clotting. Lower doses resulting in AT protein and AT activity reductions up to 60% were tolerated.
L573: Overall, in healthy animals with normal clotting at baseline, fitusiran was generally tolerated so long as AT protein levels remained &gt;10% and activity levels remained &gt;20%. These data highlight the lower limits of AT protein and activity reductions in healthy animals and underscore the potential limitations of safety assessments with fitusiran in animals with normal clotting at baseline.
L574: ## Exaggerated Pharmacology/Thrombosis in Hemophilia Animal Models
L575: An important question was whether the hemophilia condition might prevent excessive clotting with fitusiran, compared to animals with normal clotting at baseline. To address this, fitusiran was tested in various hemophilia animal models.
L576: Transgenic hemophilia A (HA) mice were administered fitusiran at up to 30 mg/kg/week (approximately 12 times the MRHD based on BSA comparisons) for 26 weeks. AT protein levels were reduced up to 90% (AT activity not measured), but deaths were only observed in
L577: ## NDA 219019
## QFITLIA (fitusiran)
L578: untreated control HA mice (caused by hemophilia-related bleeding). Fitusiran administration reduced deaths significantly; only 6% of HA mice given fitusiran died, compared to 40% of untreated control HA mice.
L579: A 4-week pharmacodynamic study was conducted using HA dogs (a genetic model) administered fitusiran by subcutaneous injection of up to 10 mg/kg/week (a dose representing 26 times the MRHD based on BSA comparisons). At 90% AT activity reduction (AT protein levels not measured), a dog was euthanized with evidence of systemic clotting, but AT activity reductions up to 70% were well-tolerated at 8 times the MRHD (based on BSA comparisons).
L580: In a monkey study, fitusiran was administered to healthy animals at up to 0.5 mg/kg/week (representing a dose below the MRHD based on BSA comparisons) for 6 weeks, which was followed on Day 43 by induction of an HA phenotype using an antihuman Factor VIII antibody. Fitusiran was well tolerated in monkeys prior to the induction of HA, where AT activity was decreased up to 80% with no evidence of thrombosis. Induction of HA in monkeys on Day 43 resulted in a normalization of thrombin generation at the 0.5 mg/kg/week dose (representing a dose below the MRHD based on BSA comparisons). A disadvantage of this model was that the fitusiran dose was limited (to below the clinical dose) to prevent thrombosis prior to administration of the FVIII antibody.
L581: Taken together, studies in animal models of hemophilia underscored the utility of employing nontraditional species/strains in both pharmacodynamic and toxicology studies, both of which contributed important safety information. Overall, these data strongly suggested that animal models of hemophilia were less sensitive to exaggerated pharmacology with fitusiran compared to animals with normal clotting at baseline. In contrast to the induction of clotting seen in healthy animals, fitusiran was generally found to be protective (of bleeding) in animal models of hemophilia along with low risk of excessive clotting at clinically relevant exposures.
L582: ## Hepatobiliary Toxicity
L583: Subcutaneous administration of fitusiran in animals was characterized by rapid hepatic deposition and slow hepatic clearance, which is typical of GalNAc-conjugated liver-targeted siRNA drugs. Indeed, liver fitusiran concentrations were in vast excess of those measured in other tissues, and liver tissue half-life (~60 hours) greatly exceeded that measured in the plasma (1 to 3 hours). The liver was also a clear target organ of toxicity across the fitusiran nonclinical program. Given that the liver is both the primary site of fitusiran distribution and pharmacodynamic activity, it was important to assess whether fitusiran was directly hepatotoxic. Animal studies consistently showed minimal to moderate hepatic necrosis and bile duct dilatation at the highest doses in the presence of extensive systemic clotting. It is unclear how these biliary tract findings in rats may translate to the human gallbladder. Residual uncertainty regarding a clinical risk of hepatobiliary toxicity should be based on clinical trial data.
L584: ## Hypersensitivity Reactions
L585: Fitusiran increased the incidence of hypersensitivity reactions (skin redness/dryness/scab/discharge at the injection site) in rats and monkeys at all doses tested. However, given potential differences between animals and humans, any potential labeling of a risk of hypersensitivity reactions should be based on clinical trial data.
L586: ## **Reproductive and Developmental Effects**
L587: Fitusiran was not tested in reproduction studies in female animals because hemophilia A and B occur almost exclusively in males. In the absence of such data, the use of effective contraceptive measures by female patients with hemophilia should be encouraged during treatment with fitusiran. Fitusiran did not adversely impact male fertility and did not negatively impact early embryonic development in a 26-week repeat-dose chronic toxicity study in rats where male rats were dosed at up to 1 mg/kg/week (a dose below the MRHD based on BSA comparisons) for 16 weeks and then mated with untreated females.
L588: ## **Clinical Safety Margins**
L589: Fitusiran, like other GalNAc conjugated siRNAs, is quickly cleared from the plasma and taken up by the liver and to a lesser extent other tissues. In this context, plasma exposure levels may inadequately reflect total tissue exposure levels over time. Therefore, clinical safety margins shown in Table 38 below reflect total fitusiran doses over time, with additional corrections for body surface area differences between species.
L590: **Table 38. Fitusiran Safety Margins**

L596: ## **Risk Summary and Recommendation**
L597: Studies of fitusiran in animal models of hemophilia suggested reduced risk of excessive clotting at clinically relevant doses compared to healthy animals. Hepatotoxicity in animal studies was observed with fitusiran at high doses in the presence of severe systemic clotting. Adverse skin reactions were observed at the subcutaneous injection site with fitusiran in animals, but clinical risk should be based on clinical trial data. No developmental or reproduction studies were conducted in female animals administered fitusiran because hemophilia A and B occur almost exclusively in males. Fitusiran did not adversely impact male fertility or reproductive performance, and conceptuses from dosed males were normal.
L598: Overall, the nonclinical data support the marketing approval of fitusiran for the treatment of hemophilia. Regarding specific risks identified, and for product labeling, while animal models helped characterize the risks of excessive thrombosis, hepatobiliary toxicity, and injection site hypersensitivity reactions, the product label should emphasize clinical trial data. Data obtained from fertility and early embryonic development studies conducted using male animals given fitusiran should be described in Section 13.1 of the product label (refer also to Section 8.4 of this review). No animal data are recommended for inclusion in Section 13.2 (Animal Toxicology) of the product label. Finally, based on extensive review of the nonclinical program for fitusiran, the
L599: Pharmacology/Toxicology review team did not identify deficiencies that preclude approval of fitusiran for the treatment of hemophilia A and B with or without inhibitors.
L600: ## 7.2. Potential Risks or Safety Concerns Based on Drug Class or Other Drug-Specific Factors
L601: Potential risks or safety concerns were reviewed based on known information from approved non-factor-replacement therapies emicizumab, marstacimab and concizumab. Approved non-factor-replacement products increase thrombin generation by enhancing procoagulants (anti-FIXa/FX bispecific antibody in the case of emicizumab), or by inhibiting anticoagulant pathways (antithrombin siRNA therapy in the case of fitusiran, and anti-TFPI antibodies in the case of concizumab and marstacimab). Increases in thrombin generation are associated with thrombotic risk, and this risk is included in the USPI Warnings and Precautions sections of HEMLIBRA (emicizumab) (Genentech 2024), HYMPAVZI (marstacimab) (Pfizer Laboratories Div Pfizer Inc 2025), and ALHEMO (concizumab) (Novo Nordisk 2024) labels. A box warning in the HEMLIBRA USPI relates the risk of thrombotic microangiopathy and thrombotic events when a cumulative amount of &gt;100 U/kg/24 hours of aPCC was administered for 24 hours or more to patients receiving HEMLIBRA prophylaxis (Genentech 2024). Section 7.1 discusses the risk of thromboembolic events in the fitusiran development program.
L602: Fitusiran is an siRNA, conjugated on the sense strand with triantennary N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) to facilitate uptake by hepatocytes. In hepatocytes, fitusiran utilizes the RNA interference mechanism and directs catalytic breakdown of mRNA for antithrombin. Potential risks or safety concerns were reviewed based on known information from approved hepatic targeted siRNA therapies ONPATTRO (patisiran) (Alnylam Pharmaceuticals 2023a), GIVLAARI (givosiran) (Alnylam Pharmaceuticals 2024b), OXLUMO (lumasiran) (Alnylam Pharmaceuticals 2023b), LEQVIO (inclisiran) (Novartis Pharmaceuticals Corporation 2024), and AMVUTTRA (vutrisiran) (Alnylam Pharmaceuticals 2024a). Transaminase elevations (alanine aminotransferase [ALT]) of at least 3 times the upper limit of normal (ULN) were observed in 15% of patients treated with GIVLAARI, and hepatic toxicity is listed in the Warnings and Precautions section of the USPI. Section 7.3 discusses the risk of hepatotoxicity observed in the fitusiran development program.
L603: ## 7.3. Potential Risks or Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L604: Fitusiran is not currently approved or marketed in the United States or in any country.
L605: ## 7.4. FDA Approach to the Safety Review
L606: The primary safety assessment for the intended population of subjects with HA or HB with or without inhibitors was based on a pooled analysis of 286 subjects who received fitusiran AT-DR during the fitusiran developmental program. These individuals include subjects who received the AT-DR during studies EFC15110 (N=2), LTE14762 (N=18), and LTE15174 (N=269). Study EFC15110 was an open-label switching study in subjects with HA or HB with or without
L607: inhibitors, previously on BPA/factor prophylaxis. Study LTE14762 was an open-label long-term extension study in subjects with HA or HB with or without inhibitors who were previously enrolled in a phase 1 study (TDR14767). Study LTE15174 was an open-label extension study in subjects with HA or HB with or without inhibitors who were previously enrolled in a phase 3 parent study (see Table 5 in Section 3.3 for further details).
L608: To support the analysis of safety, pooled safety assessments was based on data derived from a total of 335 unique subjects who received at least one dose of fitusiran (whether on the non-AT-DR dose or AT-DR dose) while enrolled in studies LTE14762, EFC14768, EFC14769, EFC15110, and LTE15174. Safety results from these studies were only described when applicable. Figure 13 shows the subject flow and fitusiran dose in the safety population.
L609: A supportive assessment of safety was also conducted for the randomized controlled studies EFC14768 and EFC14769, separately, in order to understand the safety profile of fitusiran compared to a concurrent control (on-demand therapy). The safety results should be interpreted with the understanding that this is based on the 80 mg QM dose regimen, which is a higher dose than the AT-DR. These results are presented in Section 17.
L610: Further, an exploratory assessment of safety was conducted comparing safety results of subjects who received the AT-DR in LTE15174 who were originally randomized to the fitusiran 80 mg QM dose in studies EFC14768 and EFC14769 to the control on-demand arm from studies EFC14768 and EFC14769. Results are provided in Section 17.
L611: Adverse events of special interest identified by the Agency were thrombosis, hepatotoxicity, and gallbladder disease. The Drug-Induced Liver Injury (DILI) Team was consulted to examine safety signals of gallbladder disease and hepatotoxicity.
L612: Figure 13. Subject Flow and Fitusiran Regimen in Clinical Studies
L613: Source: Applicant Summary of Clinical Safety
L614: Abbreviations: AT-DR, antithrombin-based dose regimen; fitu, fitusiran; hem, hemophilia; RCT, randomized controlled trial
L615: Clinical study data were independently analyzed by the Agency. Clinical data scientists (CDSs) from the Division of Biomedical Informatics generated the safety tables and figures. These include safety tables and figures for subject disposition, exposure data, treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs), and serious adverse events (SAEs). Additional exploration and analysis of TEAEs were performed by the clinical reviewer using JMP, JMP Clinical, and the Office of Computational Science Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA)-based Adverse Event Diagnostics tool.
L616: The Applicant's translations of verbatim terms to the MedDRA (version 25.1) preferred terms (PTs) for TEAEs were reviewed and found to be acceptable. The review team did not identify any major data quality or integrity issues that precluded performing a thorough safety review.
L617: TEAEs were defined as any adverse event after receiving a dose of the study drug. TEAEs were collected after the last administration of fitusiran, until AT activity levels returned to at least 60%. All adverse events (AEs) in the reviewed trials were graded by investigators using the terms mild (asymptomatic or mild symptoms, intervention not indicated), moderate (minimal, local or noninvasive intervention indicated; limiting age-appropriate instrumental activities of daily living), and severe (severe or medically significant but not immediately life-threatening; hospitalization or prolongation of hospitalization indicated; disabling; limiting self-care activities of daily living; life-threatening; or death).
L618: TEAEs were analyzed at all levels of the MedDRA hierarchy and using FDA Medical Queries (FMQ) narrow. FMQ (narrow) are a collection of approximately 110 standardized groups, where each group comprises adverse event terms that reflect a specific medical concept. Each FMQ term is composed of one or more PTs. FMQs are not mutually exclusive; that is, an individual preferred term (PT) may be counted in more than one FMQ. When reporting FMQ analyses, all FMQs, including overlapping terms, are included in the presentation of the data in this review. In addition, not all PTs are included in an FMQ. To assure that all potential safety signals are identified, analysis of all reported TEAEs not included in at least one FMQ were identified and analyzed.
L619: Review of safety was based upon:
L620: - Clinical Study Reports for studies
- Protocols for studies
- Data sets for the populations from clinical studies described above (data cutoff June 14, 2023)
- Summary of clinical safety
- 120-day safety update (data cutoff March 1, 2024)
- Subject narratives
- Case report forms
- Case report forms and narratives were provided and reviewed for SAEs, and adverse events of special interest
L621: ## 7.5. Adequacy of the Clinical Safety Database
L622: The exposure for the primary safety population AT-DR period is shown in Table 39. The median duration of exposure to AT-DR was 96 (range 72.7 to 117.9) weeks. Approximately 84% of subjects were exposed to fitusiran AT-DR for 50 weeks or longer, with a total exposure to the AT-DR of 486 subject-years.
L623: The size of the safety database is considered adequate for a comprehensive safety assessment of fitusiran for the proposed indication, subject population, dosage regimen, and duration, given the rarity of hemophilia, and is consistent with other drug development programs for severe hemophilia.

L629: ## 7.5.1. Safety Results
L630: Fitusiran has a serious risk of thromboembolism, similar to other approved drugs for patients with hemophilia; however, fitusiran also has serious risks of gallbladder disease and hepatotoxicity. Overall, few subjects had serious adverse reactions or required drug discontinuation or dose interruption on the AT-DR. Safety analysis showed fitusiran to have an acceptable safety profile in subjects with either HA or HB, with or without inhibitors. No new safety concerns were identified in the 120-day safety update. As the AT-DR was implemented in
L631: mainly long-term extension studies, the study design did not allow for randomized controlled safety data.
L632: Key safety findings of adverse event analyses of the AT-DR are:
L633: - There were no fatal adverse events on the AT-DR.
- Serious adverse events (SAEs) occurred in 14.3% in subjects who received the AT-DR. The most common SAEs were hemorrhage (8.7%), which is most likely related to the underlying disease and unlikely to be related to fitusiran based on its mechanism of action and disease of study. Serious adverse reactions considered related to fitusiran occurred in 1.4% of subjects and included pancreatitis, cholecystitis, cholangitis, alanine aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase increased, and embolic stroke.
- Rates of drug interruption and drug discontinuation were low. In total, two subjects (0.7%) experienced TEAEs that led to drug interruption. Both events leading to drug interruption were due to elevated liver enzymes and were determined to be related to fitusiran. Five subjects (1.7%) experienced TEAEs that led to permanent discontinuation of fitusiran. Adverse reactions leading to drug discontinuation included liver injury, postoperative deep vein thrombosis, cerebral infarction, and pruritus.
- A total of 83.2% of subjects experienced a TEAE. The most common adverse reactions (&gt;10%) were viral infection (29%), nasopharyngitis (26%), and bacterial infection (11%). Less common adverse reactions were hepatic injury, arthralgia, prothrombin fragment 1.2 increased, injection site reaction, headache, cough, dyspepsia and abdominal pain.
L634: ## 7.5.2. Safety Results, Pooled Trials LTE15174, LTE14762, and EFC15110, AT-DR Period
L635: ## 7.5.2.1. Overview of Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events Summary, Pooled Trials LTE15174, LTE14762, and EFC15110, AT-DR Period
L636: Table 40 summarizes the overall safety results in the pooled trials LTE15174, LTE14762 and EFC15110, AT-DR period, based on frequency analysis. Most TEAEs were reported as mild or moderate in severity, and 9.8% of subjects had a reported severe TEAE. There were no TEAEs leading to death. SAEs were reported in 14.3% of subjects; however, many of these were related to hemorrhagic events, due to underlying hemophilia. There were five TEAEs that led to permanent discontinuation of the study drug, and two TEAEs leading to drug interruption. Overall, there did not appear to be a difference in overall TEAEs based on hemophilia A or B nor inhibitor or noninhibitor subtypes.

L644: ## 7.5.2.2. Deaths, Pooled Analyses, Pooled Trials LTE15174, LTE14762, and EFC15110, AT-DR Period
L645: No deaths were reported in Pooled Trials LTE15174, LTE14762, and EFC15110, AT-DR Period.
L646: In supportive safety analyses, two subjects died while receiving the original fitusiran dosing regimen (80 mg every month). Brief subject narratives are provided below.
L647: - Subject (b) (6) was a 48-year-old white male with hemophilia A with inhibitors and a complicated medical history including hepatitis C and HIV. The subject completed parent study EFC14769 and developed a TESAE of “metastases to peritoneum” while participating in extension study LTE15174. The subject was diagnosed with metastatic adenocarcinoma (primary unknown). The subject’s death due to metastatic adenocarcinoma occurred approximately 6 months after enrolling into extension Study LTE15174. This event
L648: is not considered related to fitusiran, as there is no evidence that fitusiran increases the risk of malignancy. Hepatitis C can increase the risk of hepatocellular carcinoma, although it is unclear whether the liver was the source of the subject's cancer.
L649: 2. Subject (b) (6) was a 25-year-old white male with hemophilia A without inhibitors who completed Study TDR14767 and enrolled in extension Study LTE14762. The subject experienced a TEAE of cerebral venous sinus thrombosis (CVST) that resulted in a fatal outcome. On Day 452, the study subject received his last dose of fitusiran before the start of the event of cerebral venous sinus thrombosis. During the long-term extension study (date not specified), the subject received three doses of factor VIII for hip pain, with doses of 31 IU/kg to 46 IU/kg. About 1 week later, the study subject presented with severe headache, vomiting, and fever, and was subsequently admitted to the hospital (40 days after the last dose of fitusiran). While initially suspected to have viral meningitis, a computed tomography scan performed to evaluate the headache was read as a subarachnoid hemorrhage, and the study subject was treated with replacement factor therapy two to three times daily. The subject subsequently deteriorated and died. Postmortem independent neuro-radiology consultants determined that the initial neuroimaging revealed it was a CVST (not subarachnoid hemorrhage) that led to the subject's initial presentation and subsequent cerebral edema and death.
L650: While the CVST event basis is multifactorial, related to recent factor administration and potential infection, the event is also likely related to fitusiran, given its long pharmacodynamic half-life. Following this event, the Applicant implemented Bleed Management Guidelines which include delineation of the use and dose of FVIII when treating breakthrough bleeds. See Section 7.1 for further discussion on thrombosis risk.
L651: ## 7.5.2.3. Serious Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events, Pooled Trials LTE15174, LTE14762, and EFC15110, AT-DR Period.
L652: The frequency of SAEs was 14.3% in subjects who received the AT-DR. Table 41 provides a frequency analysis of serious TEAEs by preferred terms and Table 42 provides a frequency analysis by FMQs that occurred during the AT-DR period. The most common SAE was hemorrhage, which occurred in 8.7% of subjects, most likely related to hemophilia and unlikely to be related to treatment with fitusiran based on its procoagulant mechanism of action.
L653: Serious adverse reactions related to fitusiran occurred in 1.4% of subjects and included pancreatitis, cholecystitis, cholangitis, alanine aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase increased, and embolic stroke. Subject narratives are summarized below. See Section 7.2 for a discussion of acute gallbladder disease (including cholecystitis, cholelithiasis, and related complications), Section 7.3 for a discussion of hepatotoxicity, and Section 7.1 for a discussion of embolic stroke. Of note, adverse events that occurred perioperatively were not considered SAEs per the Applicant.
L654: There was one event of pneumonitis. This was not considered related to fitusiran as it occurred on day 606 of the study and the subject recovered after receiving antiviral and antibiotics. Fitusiran was not interrupted or discontinued.
