L673: ## **Selected SAE Narratives Considered To Be Serious Adverse Reactions**
L674: - Subject (b) (6) was a 50-year-old male with HA with inhibitors and a history of diabetes mellitus, hypertension and hepatitis C who experienced an SAE of **cholangitis**. On Day 777 of Study LTE15174, he presented with symptoms of pancreatitis. Imaging showed obstruction due to a common bile duct stone. The subject was hospitalized and diagnosed with obstructive cholangitis, and 4 days later underwent pancreatic sphincterotomy. The subject was treated with recombinant factor VIIa at the time of surgery. Fitusiran dose had been interrupted before the SAE of cholangitis due to a prior event of aspartate aminotransferase (AST) increase on Day 677 and was resumed 2 days after the sphincterotomy, and the subject remains on fitusiran. The review team assessed this SAE as likely to be related to fitusiran based on the association of fitusiran and development of gallbladder disease (see Section 7.6.2).
L675: - Subject (b) (6) was a 27-year-old male with hemophilia who experienced a SAE of **cholecystitis**. On Day 52 of LTE15174, the subject developed biliary colic and was admitted to hospital. A CT scan with contrast showed no visible gallstones. Magnetic resonance cholangiopancreatography showed dilated intrahepatic bile ducts, but no gallstones. On Day 56, the study subject recovered from the event of biliary colic and was discharged from the hospital on the same day. Fitusiran was permanently discontinued due to the event of biliary colic and the last dose was administered on Day 114. On Day 230 (116 days after the last dose of fitusiran), the study subject recovered from the event of biliary colic. The review team assessed this SAE as likely to be related to fitusiran based on the association of fitusiran and development of gallbladder disease (see Section 7.6.2).
- Subject (b) (6) was a 27-year-old male who experienced the SAEs of **cholecystitis**, **alanine aminotransferase increased**, **aspartate aminotransferase increased**, and **pancreatitis**. On Day 136 of LTE15174, he experienced symptoms of cholecystitis, and an ultrasound showed gallbladder stones. On Day 151, AEs of ALT increased (ALT &gt;3\timesULN at 411 IU/L (normal range: 0 to 41 IU/L, baseline value: 12 IU/L [normal range: 10 to 40 IU/L]), aspartate aminotransferase increased (AST &gt;3\timesULN at 467 IU/L (normal range: 0 to 40 IU/L, baseline value: 13 IU/L [normal range: 10 to 43 IU/L]), pancreatitis, and cholecystitis were reported and the subject was hospitalized. The study subject was discharged from the hospital on Day 156. On Day 217, the subject was readmitted to hospital with symptoms of abdominal pain, and an ultrasound of the gallbladder showed multiple subcentimetric stones in the gallbladder lumen. The subject recovered from the SAEs of alanine aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase increased, and pancreatitis. Fitusiran was continued without interruption. The review team considers that fitusiran is related to the SAEs of cholecystitis, alanine aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase increased, and pancreatitis as acute gallbladder disease has been identified as serious adverse reactions with fitusiran (see Section 7.6.2).
- Subject (b) (6) is a 54-year-old male with hemophilia who experienced an SAE of **embolic stroke**. The subject had a history of hypertension, hyperlipidemia, and diabetes. The subject was initially on fitusiran 80 mg QM during Study EFC14769, and then transitioned to the AT-DR in Study LTE15174. On Day 327, the fitusiran dose was reduced to 50 mg Q2M based on AT levels. On Day 508, fitusiran was permanently discontinued due to persistently low AT levels. On Day 673 (165 days after the last dose of fitusiran), the subject developed acute symptoms of lightheadedness and dizziness in the setting of elevated blood pressure, and subsequent slurring of speech and loss of strength in the right arm and leg. He was hospitalized. MRI showed acute nonhemorrhagic infarcts in the left corona radiata and ganglio-capsular region, chronic ischemic changes in the bilateral fronto-parietal region, and chronic lacunar infarcts in bilateral ganglio-capsular region. On Day 687 (179 days after the last dose of fitusiran), the study subject recovered from the event embolic stroke and was discharged from the hospital. Given the multiple risk factors for stroke, it is difficult to discern if fitusiran contributed to the event. Due to the mechanism of action of fitusiran and fitusiran's long pharmacodynamic half-life, it is possible that fitusiran increased the thrombotic risk. While the subject had discontinued the drug 165 days prior to the event, the subject had discontinued fitusiran due to AT &lt;15\%, a strong risk factor for thrombosis. At the time of the event, the AT level was 36%.
L676: ## 7.5.2.4. Adverse Events Leading to Treatment Discontinuation, Pooled Trials LTE15174, LTE14762, and EFC15110, AT-DR Period.
L677: During the AT-DR period, there were two subjects (0.7%) who experienced TEAEs that led to drug interruption. Both events leading to drug interruption were due to elevated liver enzymes and were determined to be related to fitusiran (see Section 7.6.3 for further discussion on hepatotoxicity). In total, five subjects (1.7%) experienced TEAEs that led to permanent discontinuation of fitusiran. Adverse reactions leading to drug discontinuation included liver injury, postoperative deep vein thrombosis, cerebral infarction, and pruritus. Subject narratives are summarized below. Risk of thrombosis with fitusiran is further discussed in Section 7.6.1.
L678: Subject narratives for TEAEs leading to drug interruption:
L679: - Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6) experienced transaminases increased (PT). The study subject was a 35-year-old male with HA and a history of hepatitis C. On Day 1515 of LTE14762, ALT was 531 IU/L (normal range: 0 to 50 IU/L) and AST was 288 IU/L (normal range: 0 to 50 IU/L). These elevations were associated with an episode of alcohol consumption as reported by the study subject. Fitusiran was interrupted with the last dose given on Day 1480. The subject was instructed to abstain from alcohol; no other corrective treatment was given. The transaminases returned to the normal range by Day 1564, and fitusiran was restarted on Day 1564. The liver enzymes remained normal until Day 1955 when ALT was &gt;3\timesULN, with ALT at 309 IU/L (normal range: 10 to 40 IU/L) and AST at 139 IU/L (normal range: 10 to 43 IU/L). No further details regarding the event were provided. The subject interrupted fitusiran and subsequently withdrew from the study.
- Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6) experienced aspartate aminotransferase increased (PT) which led to drug interruption. See Section 7.6.3 for further details.
L680: Subject narratives of TEAEs leading to permanent drug discontinuation:
L681: - Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6) was a 20-year-old male with HA who permanently discontinued fitusiran after a TEAE of liver injury on Day 56 of LTE15174, after his first dose of fitusiran (50 mg Q2M). On Day 56, LFTs showed AST at 164 IU/L (normal range: 15 to 40 IU/L, baseline value: 24 IU/L), ALT at 624 IU/L (normal range: 9 to 50 IU/L, baseline value: 35 IU/L [normal range: 10 to 40 IU/L]). Alkaline phosphatase (ALP), bilirubin, and gamma glutamyl transferase were within the normal range. On Day 135, the study subject recovered from the event of liver injury with AST at 28 IU/L and ALT at 44 IU/L. This event is considered related to fitusiran.
- Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6) permanently discontinued fitusiran after a TEAE of cerebral infarction. The subject was a 26-year-old male with HB with inhibitors and a history of type 2 diabetes, dyslipidemia, hepatic steatosis, obstructive sleep apnea, and nephrotic syndrome. The subject was evaluated on Day 219 of LTE15174 for vertigo and an MRI revealed evidence of a cerebral infarction in the right temporal lobe and cerebellum, though acuity could not be determined. The subject received the last dose of fitusiran (50 mg Q2M) on Day 226. The review team considers that while the subject had cardiovascular risk factors for stroke, given the mechanism of action of fitusiran to increase thrombotic risk, its role in the causation of the event cannot be ruled out.
L682: 3. Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) discontinued fitusiran after a TEAE of hepatocellular carcinoma. The subject was a 61-year-old male with HA and a history of hepatitis C. On Day 2340 of study LTE14762, the subject experienced a TEAE of hepatocellular carcinoma after investigation of elevated alkaline phosphatase and bilirubin. The last dose of fitusiran was Day 2309. The review team considers that fitusiran is unrelated to the event of hepatocellular carcinoma, as there is no mechanistic reason for fitusiran to cause malignancies and hepatitis C is a strong risk factor for hepatocellular carcinoma.

4. Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) permanently discontinued fitusiran after a TEAE of pruritus. The subject was a 47-year-old with HA and a history of pruritus, who on Day 627 of LTE15174, while receiving the AT-DR (dose 50 mg Q2M), developed worsening pruritus. Fitusiran was discontinued on Day 624. At the time of the cutoff date, the study subject had not recovered from the event of pruritus. As the pruritus worsened with fitusiran, it is possible fitusiran contributed to this adverse event.

5. Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) was a 40-year-old male with HA who permanently discontinued fitusiran due to an AE of deep vein thrombosis postoperative. On Day 1140 of LTE15174, the subject underwent a left total knee replacement. On Day 1148, he had a peripherally inserted central venous catheter. On Day 1155, he developed pain and swelling of the left leg and venous doppler ultrasound showed extensive thrombosis of the left common femoral, popliteal, and calf veins. The subject received anticoagulation with apixaban, and fitusiran was discontinued, with the last dose (50 mg QM) administered on Day 1148. The event of thrombosis is considered related to fitusiran based on the mechanism of action of the drug. Recent orthopedic surgery and the central line likely further increased the risk of thrombosis.
L683: ## 7.5.2.5. Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events, Pooled Trials LTE15174, LTE14762, and EFC15110, AT-DR Period
L684: Table 43 lists the adverse events by PT and Table 44 lists adverse events by FMQ from the Pooled Trials LTE15174, LTE14762, and EFC15110, AT-DR Period. A total of 83.2% of subjects experienced a TEAE. The most common TEAEs were viral infection (29%), nasopharyngitis (26%), and bacterial infection (11%). While it is not expected based on the mechanism of action of fitusiran for there to be an increased risk of infections, on further analyses of the randomized controlled studies (Studies EFC14768 and EFC14769), the rates of viral infection, nasopharyngitis, and bacterial infections were significantly higher in the fitusiran arm compared to the on-demand arm (see Section 17.1 of the review). Therefore, the clinical team considered these TEAEs as likely related to fitusiran. The next most common TEAEs were hemorrhage (10%), hepatic injury (9%), arthralgia (8%), pyrexia (7%), diarrhea (6%), pain in extremity (6%), prothrombin fragment 1.2 increased (6%), injection site reaction (5%), cough (5%), and headache (5%). Elevated prothrombin 1.2 levels can indicate an increased risk for thrombosis (Ota et al. 2008). Adverse events of pyrexia, diarrhea and pain in extremity did not occur at higher rates in the fitusiran arms compared to on-demand arms in randomized controlled trials, therefore, these events were not considered adverse reactions. Urticaria was reported in two subjects (0.7%). It is unclear if the events of urticaria were related to fitusiran due to limited details regarding the events. However, both subjects continued fitusiran without interruption, and similar rates of urticaria were seen in control and fitusiran arms in randomized controlled trials (see Section 17.1 for further details). The review team considered hemorrhage unrelated to
L685: fitusiran as it is related to the underlying disease. Of note, there was a single event of mild angioedema (lip swelling). This was not considered related to fitusiran as it occurred on day 971 of the study and the fitusiran dose was not interrupted.

L699: Treatment-emergent adverse events defined as any AE with onset or ongoing after fitusiran dose restart and before the data cut off for NDA. However, AEs were not considered treatment-emergent if they occurred during the follow-up after a subject's AT value was 60% or greater.

L704: ## 7.5.2.6. Laboratory Findings, Pooled Trials LTE15174, LTE14762, and EFC15110, AT-DR Period
L705: Routine monitoring of blood and urine, including complete blood count, clinical chemistries, and hepatic and renal evaluations, were obtained regularly at prespecified points throughout the studies. This section presents a frequency-based analysis of laboratory assessments.
L706: ## Chemistries
L707: Table 45 presents a frequency-based analysis of laboratory assessments using objective laboratory data. There were no significant changes in laboratory assessments for sodium, potassium, chloride, bicarbonate, calcium, magnesium, phosphate, or albumin.
L708: Low glucose levels occurred in 16.1% of study subjects. Most low glucose levels were level 1 and not associated with a continuous trend. High random glucose levels occurred in 6% of study subjects. Sustained elevated glucoses were in study subjects who had a concurrent diagnosis of diabetes mellitus. Of note, glucose levels were not fasting. There was no pattern of either low or high glucose based on hemophilia type or inhibitor status.
L709: Increases in creatinine \ge 2\times baseline occurred in 4.2% of subjects; however, these were not sustained increases. Reductions in eGFR of \ge 50\% from baseline occurred in 3 individuals. These cases were reviewed, and the reductions were not sustained and recovered to baseline.
L710: Overall, no clinically relevant chemistry laboratory findings were identified. Most abnormal laboratory values were not sustained and were associated with underlying comorbidities. In addition, while there appear to be outliers for hypokalemia and hypophosphatemia in this analysis, no imbalance was seen for fitusiran vs. on demand treatment in the randomized, controlled parent studies.

L722: ## Hematology Laboratory Findings
L723: Frequency of abnormal laboratory values for hematology assessments are shown in Table 46. No significant differences in abnormal laboratory values occurred across the hemophilia subtypes or inhibitor status. Changes in hemoglobin likely were related to the subjects' bleeding profile.
L724: Overall, no clinically relevant laboratory findings were identified. The majority of abnormal laboratory values were not sustained and were associated with underlying comorbidities. In addition, while there appears to be outliers of low white blood cell counts, low neutrophil counts and low lymphocyte counts in this analysis, no imbalance was seen for fitusiran vs. on demand treatment in the randomized, controlled parent studies.

L754: ## 7.5.2.7. Assessment of Drug-Induced Liver Injury, Pooled Trials LTE15174, LTE14762, and EFC15110, AT-DR Period
L755: eDISH plots for hepatocellular injury are presented in Figure 17 and Figure 18. Two subjects met potential Hy's Law criteria and are presented below. Upon further investigation, both cases were determined to be unlikely DILI due to fitusiran. In the pooled Trials LTE15174, LTE14762, and EFC15110, AT-DR Period, there was no case of confirmed DILI.
L756: - Subject (b) (6) was a 50-year-old male with HA with inhibitors and a history of diabetes mellitus, hypertension, and hepatitis C, who was enrolled in Study EFC014768 at 80 mg QM, and then rolled over to Study LTE15174. On Day 777 of Study LTE15174 (Day 1030 since the initiation of fitusiran in Study EFC14768), he presented with symptoms of pancreatitis. Imaging showed obstruction due to a common bile duct stone. The subject was hospitalized on Day 778, was diagnosed with obstructive cholangitis, and on Day 782 underwent pancreatic sphincterotomy. Liver tests around this time were significant for an ALT &gt;10 \times ULN and bilirubin &gt;2 \times ULN. On Day 786, the study subject recovered from the events of cholangitis and obstructive pancreatitis, and liver tests normalized. Fitusiran had been previously held on Day 677 due to AST elevation and then was resumed Day 784 at 20 mg Q2M and there was no recurrence of elevated liver enzymes. This case is unlikely DILI due to intervening obstructive biliary disease as the cause of ALT and bilirubin increase, and resolved with endoscopic retrograde cholangiopancreatography with no recurrence with drug rechallenge.
L757: - Subject (b) (6) was a 44-year-old male with HA without inhibitors who on Day 589 of Study LTE15174 developed AST &gt;6\times ULN, ALT 2.9\times ULN, and bilirubin of 2\times ULN. There were exposures to alcohol, a cold medication, and protein powder, which were likely the etiology of liver abnormalities. Liver tests normalized 1 week later, and the subject remained on fitusiran treatment.
L758: In the original fitusiran dosing of 80 mg SQ QM, ALT elevations &gt;3\times ULN and potential cases of Hy's Law were observed more frequently than in the AT-DR dosing period. The DILI consult team performed an in-depth analysis of both DILI and gallbladder disease across the fitusiran development program, including the original dosing regimens used in studies EFC14768, EFC14769, EFC15110, and LTE14762; refer to the finalized DILI consult in DARRTS for the full analysis and Section 7.6.3 for further discussion.

L793: ## 7.6. Key Safety Review Issues
L794: ## 7.6.1. Thrombotic Events
L795: ## Issue
L796: Fitusiran was originally developed as an 80 mg QM fixed dose, however, due to serious events of thrombosis, including a thrombosis-related fatality, the dosing regimen was modified to the AT-DR. The risk of thrombotic events was assessed in both subjects who received the original 80 mg QM dosing regimen and the AT-DR.
L797: ## **Background**
L798: Fitusiran is an siRNA that causes degradation of AT mRNA through RNA interference, which results in a reduction of plasma AT protein levels, reduced AT activity, and increased thrombin generation. The mechanism of action of fitusiran to increase thrombin generation inherently increases the ability to make clots, which can increase thrombosis risk though excessive pharmacologic effect. In the hemophilia context, increasing clot formation is beneficial due to underlying pathological bleeding risk, but there is a careful balance between sufficient antithrombin suppression to prevent bleeding and avoidance of the risk of thrombosis, which is associated with significant morbidity and mortality. Thromboembolic events are a common complication in congenital antithrombin deficiency, in which AT activity ranges from 40 to 60% (Patnaik and Moll 2008). Thrombosis formation was observed in nonclinical studies with fitusiran (see Section 7.1).
L799: Mitigation of potential thrombotic risks in the fitusiran development program included exclusion of subjects with severe hemophilia with significant thrombosis history or known thrombophilia disorder, and implementation of bleed management guidelines (BMG), which include recommendations to guide investigators on the use of factor replacement products or bypassing products during acute bleeding events and during surgical procedures. Bleed management guidelines are described in detail in Section 15. At the close of the controlled studies EFC14768, EFC14769, and EFC15110, an analysis of thrombotic events and correlation with AT activity led to institution of the AT-DR to target antithrombin levels between 15 to 35%. Per the Applicant, persistent AT levels &lt;10% increases the risk of thromboembolic events.
L800: ## **Assessment**
L801: In total, 11 out of 335 subjects (3.3%) experienced a thrombotic event in the fitusiran clinical development program. Of the subjects who experienced thrombotic events, 7 out of 270 subjects (2.6%) had a thrombotic event while receiving the original 80-mg dose regimen and 4 out of 286 (1.4%) subjects had a thrombotic event on the AT-DR. No subjects in the on-demand (control) arms in the randomized controlled trials (studies EFC14768 and EFC14769) experienced a thrombotic event. A summary of the subjects with thrombotic events while receiving fitusiran is described in Table 51 below, and full subject narratives from all 11 subjects are in Section 17.3. It is notable that both arterial and venous thrombotic events were reported, including atypical events such as spinal vein thrombosis and thrombosis of the papilla of the eye. Events typically occurred after subjects had been taking fitusiran for over &gt;5 months (range 57 to 1147 days on fitusiran). Almost all subjects had risk factors at baseline (e.g., hypertension, diabetes, smoking history, recent surgery, central line, history of thrombosis), however, the strongest risk factors appear to be recent administration of factor or bypassing factor agent, and persistent AT levels &lt;15%. The risk of thrombosis remains for several months after discontinuing fitusiran, as it may take 6 months for AT activity to recover to &gt;60%.

L810: Abbreviations: AT, antithrombin; CVA, cerebrovascular accident; CVST, cerebral venous sinus thrombosis; DM, diabetes mellitus; DVT, deep vein thrombosis; HA, hemophilia A; HAwI, hemophilia A with inhibitor; HB, hemophilia B; HBwI, hemophilia B with inhibitor; HTN, hypertension; Hx, history; L, left; MOA, mechanism of action; Q2M, every 2 months; QM, once monthly
L811: ## **Conclusion**
L812: Patients exposed to fitusiran are at increased risk of thrombosis due to excessive pharmacologic effect. While implementing bleed management guidelines and reducing fitusiran exposure such that the AT activity range is targeted to between 15 to 35% appears to have decreased the rate of thrombotic events, the risk clearly remains, in particular in the setting of additional risk factors for thrombosis, including high doses of factor administration and persistent AT levels &lt;15%. The risk of thromboembolism should be included as a boxed warning, as knowledge of this risk is necessary prior to a prescriber administering the drug. Patients with a history of thrombosis or significant risk factors for thrombosis will need to consider the benefit/risk profile of the drug, and it is imperative for prescribers to follow bleed management guidelines. With labeling which includes bleed management guidelines, this risk can be mitigated.
L813: ## 7.6.2. Acute and Recurrent Gallbladder Disease
L814: ## **Issue**
L815: Fitusiran has a significant risk of acute and recurrent gallbladder disease. Subjects in clinical trials required surgical intervention and had complications such as pancreatitis. The risk of acute and recurrent gallbladder disease was assessed in subjects who received the original dosing regimen and the AT-DR.
L816: This review issue was largely assessed by the DILI team from the Division of Hepatology and Nutrition; please see the finalized consult review in DARRTS dated March 21, 2025.
L817: ## **Background**
L818: Patients with hemophilia are not typically at increased risk for gallbladder disease. In addition, gallbladder disease was not identified as an adverse reaction during the clinical development programs of other approved siRNA products. Therefore, it was an unexpected finding when a significant number of subjects developed gallbladder disease and related complications in this development program.
L819: Biliary tract abnormalities of biliary hyperplasia and bile duct dilation were noted in mice treated with fitusiran (see Section 13.2.5). However, biliary tract abnormalities were not observed across animal studies.
L820: ## **Assessment**
L821: In the fitusiran development program, 51 (15.2%) subjects experienced a gallbladder TEAE after receiving at least one dose of fitusiran. Of the 51 subjects who experienced a gallbladder event, the most common event was cholelithiasis (47.1%), followed by cholecystitis (33.3%), chronic cholecystitis (11.8%), gallbladder polyp (11.8%), acute cholecystitis (7.8%), biliary colic (5.9%), gallbladder enlargement (5.9%), cholestasis (3.9%), cholangitis (2%), and biliary dyskinesia (2%). The majority of events were moderate in severity. Gallbladder TEAEs were graded mild in 12 subjects (23.5%), moderate in 36 subjects (70.6%) and severe in 9 subjects (17.6%). Frequency of gallbladder TEAEs are described below by dosing cohort.
L822: ## Frequency of Gallbladder Events in Pooled Safety Databases
L823: ## Original 80 mg QM Dose Cohort
L824: 46/270 (17.3%) of subjects experienced gallbladder events.
L825: - Study 14768: 6/41 (14.6%) subjects in the fitusiran treatment arm and 0/19 in the on-demand control arm.
- Study 14769: 7/79 (8.8%) subjects in the fitusiran treatment arm and 0/40 in the on-demand control arm.
- Study 15110: 10/67 (14.9%) subjects during the fitusiran treatment period and 0/78 subjects during the control period of 15110.
L826: ## AT-DR Cohort
L827: 11/286 (3.8%) of subjects experienced 15 acute gallbladder events, including cholelithiasis (4), cholecystitis (3), gallbladder polyp (3), cholecystitis acute (2), cholangitis (1), cholecystitis chronic (1), and gallbladder enlargement (1).
L828: ## Cholecystectomy
L829: Across the 335-subject safety database from the entire fitusiran exposed population, 12 individuals (all on fitusiran) underwent a cholecystectomy (3.6%). Nine of these individuals experienced an SAE of cholecystitis/cholelithiasis before the cholecystectomy, and one experienced an SAE of cholangitis and pancreatitis caused by gallstone disease more than a year after cholecystectomy. Most subjects (11 of 12) did not interrupt fitusiran prior to the procedure; however, all subjects received factor concentrate or a bypassing agent perioperatively.
L830: Of the 12 subjects on fitusiran who underwent cholecystectomy, 11 subjects were receiving a fixed dose of fitusiran, and one was on AT-DR (20 mg Q2M). Eleven of these 12 subjects continued fitusiran after cholecystectomy. One subject experienced cholangitis and pancreatitis caused by gallstone disease more than two years after cholecystectomy and while on the AT-DR dose regimen. The average time to receiving a cholecystectomy was 417 days (range 57 to 1035). Table 164 (Section 17.4) shows the demographics and associated AEs in the cholecystectomy cohort.
L831: ## Conclusion
L832: Gallbladder events only occurred in severe hemophilia patients exposed to fitusiran in clinical studies; none were reported in the control subjects. Gallbladder events were less common on the AT-DR dosing (3.8%) compared to the original 80 mg fixed dose regimen (17.3%), suggesting a fitusiran dose effect. However, this observation may be subject to selection. Most subjects on AT-DR dosing had first been dosed with the original 80 mg regimen. Their ability to stay in the study and change to the AT-DR dosing was therefore contingent on their ability to tolerate the original dosing, leading to potential selection bias in safety evaluation of the AT-DR dosing.
L833: The proportion of subjects who required cholecystectomy after they developed gallbladder disease is concerning for this patient population. Patients with hemophilia are prone to bleeding, which can increase the risk of surgical procedures.
L834: Acute and recurrent gallbladder disease should be included in a boxed warning in the label to alert prescribers of this risk prior to drug administration. Prescribers should consider this risk for patients at high risk for gallbladder disease, or high risk for surgical procedures. With labeling, acute and recurrent gallbladder disease risk can be mitigated. Enhanced pharmacovigilance for gallbladder disease will be recommended.
L835: ## 7.6.3. Hepatotoxicity
L836: ## Issue
L837: Fitusiran has a significant risk of hepatotoxicity identified with the original 80-mg dose, as evidenced by a high incidence of ALT elevations greater than 3\times ULN and a Hy's law case. The risk of hepatotoxicity was assessed in subjects who received the original dosing regimen and the AT-DR. This review issue was largely assessed by the DILI team from the Division of Hepatology and Nutrition; please see the finalized consult review in DARRTS dated March 21, 2025.
L838: ## Background
L839: Fitusiran targets the liver by active uptake via the ASPGR expressed on hepatocytes. Fitusiran inhibits hepatocyte AT production through RNA interference on the *SERPINC1* gene, leading to its degradation by RNA-induced silencing complex and preventing translation of gene expression and function. The Division of Applied Regulatory Science (DARS) found no evidence that the knockdown of AT production would cause hepatotoxicity. Animal studies indicate minimal to moderate liver toxicity potential with findings of liver cell necrosis and bile duct dilatation at the highest dose exposures; however, there were differences in species. Only about 10% of the administered dose is accounted for in excretion studies, and the rest of the product may be retained in the liver, but how much is in smaller nucleotide forms (metabolites) that may be recycled for use by the body is unknown. See the DILI consult review for further details.
L840: FDA has approved several siRNAs that inhibit hepatic mRNA translation by targeted uptake by the ASPGR (see Section 7.2 for further discussion). Of the hepatic targeted siRNAs, givosiran has hepatotoxicity listed as a Warning in the label (Alnylam Pharmaceuticals 2024b).
L841: ## Assessment
L842: Overall, 66 out of 335 subjects (19.7%) who received fitusiran met DILI reporting requirements.
L843: ## Original Dose Regimen
L844: In the parent studies (fitusiran 80 mg QM dose regimen), ALT and AST elevations above 3 times the upper limit of normal (ULN) occurred in 13 subjects (31.7%) with hemophilia with inhibitors (Study EFC14769) and 14 subjects (17.7%) with hemophilia without inhibitors (Study EFC14768), compared to no events of AST/ALT elevation greater than 3\times ULN in the on-demand control groups from either study. See Table 52 and Table 53 below for liver enzyme abnormalities by study. Subjects treated with fitusiran 80 mg in the open-label study EFC15110 had ALT elevations greater than 3\times ULN at similar rates seen in fitusiran subjects treated in Study EFC14768 and EFC14769.

L858: - Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6) was a 19-year-old with HA, receiving 80 mg QM fitusiran, who had normal liver enzymes at baseline and developed DILI that was highly likely due to fitusiran. On Day 42 he had an adverse event of ALT increase (4 to 5× ULN) after the second
L859: dose of fitusiran 80 mg. Liver enzymes improved with drug interruption. The subject was restarted on dosing and experienced concomitant rise in his liver tests. On Day 198, the subject met Hy's law criteria with a TB &gt;2\times ULN and ALT 7\times ULN. There were no concomitant medications. Fitusiran was discontinued and the subject's liver tests improved.
L860: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) was an 18-year-old with HB, receiving 80 mg QM, who had normal liver enzymes at baseline and developed probable DILI. On Day 110 the subject experienced an adverse event of ALT increased that was 3\times ULN. Evaluation for alternative causes of ALT increase was negative and the subject was not taking any other concomitant medications. Fitusiran was interrupted with improvement in liver tests. Fitusiran was reinitiated on Day 281 and a small but not clinically significant increase in ALT was observed. The subject remained on fitusiran in the long-term extension study.

L862: ALT &gt;3\times ULN was less common in the AT-DR period compared to the 80 mg QM dose regimen.

L874: ## **Conclusion**
L875: The below conclusion is adapted from the DILI consult review.
L876: Fitusiran use was associated with an increased incidence of liver test elevations in treated subjects compared to controls. The risk of hepatotoxicity is greater in the 80-mg original dosing compared to the AT-DR dosing. There was one Hy's law case (Study subject [REDACTED](b)(6)) that was highly likely for DILI on the original 80-mg dose. This subject recovered with timely drug discontinuation, but the case indicates that the rise in liver enzymes associated with fitusiran can lead to liver injury with impairment of hepatic function, rather than being benign elevation in aminotransaminases.
L877: On the AT-DR, the incidence of ALT elevations was lower. The dose subjects received was lower (50 mg or 20 mg QM or Q2M) compared to the 80 mg QM in the original studies, indicating the liver injury may be partly dose-dependent. While the incidence of ALT elevation was lower in the AT-DR, the risk of hepatotoxicity was not eliminated. Furthermore, there was no control group for the AT-DR dosing, and the majority of subjects on the AT-DR dose were those who had tolerated the 80-mg dose, such that interpretation of the experience on the AT-DR is confounded by selection bias. Those on the original dosing regimen who tolerated the drug and were able to stay on it would have likely been less likely to have an issue on an even lower dose seen in AT-DR.
L878: Hepatotoxicity should be included as a Warning in the label to closely monitor patients for hepatic injury by monitoring liver blood tests monthly for 6 months and as clinically indicated thereafter, and to direct discontinuation with suspected DILI injury. While prompt discontinuation is not possible, given dosing is QM or Q2M, early recognition with monitoring liver enzymes should decrease the risk of serious liver injury. With labeling, including prescribing the AT-DR, hepatotoxicity risk can be mitigated. Enhanced pharmacovigilance for hepatotoxicity will be recommended.
L879: ## **8. Therapeutic Individualization**
L880: ## **8.1. Intrinsic Factors**
L881: The Applicant performed PopPK analysis using pooled data from all 6 clinical studies to evaluate the effects of intrinsic factors on the plasma PK and PD of fitusiran. The evaluated intrinsic factors included age, body weight, hemophilia type (hemophilia A or B), inhibitor status (inhibitor or noninhibitor), and race. A summary of the PopPK analysis can be found in the Pharmacometrics Assessment in Section 14 below.
L882: PopPK analysis showed that hemophilia type and inhibitor status do not impact fitusiran plasma PK. Body weight, race (Asian versus non-Asian), eGFR, albumin, and bilirubin were identified as statistically significant covariates in subjects with hemophilia, but the magnitude of the effect (&lt;25% on fitusiran plasma PK) is not considered to be clinically relevant (fitusiran effect is driven by fitusiran concentrations in liver, rather than plasma).
L883: ## **Hepatic Impairment**
L884: There was no clinical experience of fitusiran in subjects with hepatic impairment, as the clinical development program excluded subjects with clinically significant liver disease, or with ALT and/or AST &gt;1.5\times upper limit of normal reference range (ULN) or total bilirubin &gt; ULN. The Applicant did not conduct a dedicated clinical study to evaluate the impact of hepatic impairment on fitusiran PK/PD. The review team recommends “avoid use” of fitusiran in patients with all degrees of hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh Class A, B, and C). This recommendation is due to the known risk of hepatotoxicity with fitusiran, and patients with cirrhosis are at a greater risk of decompensation if they have a gallbladder event and require cholecystectomy.
L885: ## **8.2. Extrinsic Factors**
L886: The Applicant conducted a number of in vitro studies with human matrices to assess the potential for drug interactions mediated by drug metabolizing enzymes and transporters. These studies showed that fitusiran is unlikely to interact with drug-metabolizing enzymes and transporters and, therefore, the potential for drug interaction at clinically relevant concentrations is very low (see Section 14.1 for details). Thus, no clinical drug interactions studies were conducted by the Applicant.
L887: ## **8.3. Plans for Pediatric Drug Development**
L888: Fitusiran will be approved for pediatric patients 12 years and older, as this age group was included in the clinical trials used to demonstrate efficacy and safety of fitusiran. The Applicant is currently conducting Study EFC15467, an open-label dose finding study in subjects with hemophilia A and B aged 1 to &lt;12 years. The results of this study may be used to inform pediatric labeling for younger patients in the future.
L889: ## **8.4. Pregnancy, Lactation, and Females/Males of Reproductive Potential**
L890: The following nonclinical information was used in support of the indicated labeling sections. Additional nonclinical details are in Section 13.1.
L891: ## **Animal Data**
L892: Animal reproductive and developmental toxicology studies have not been conducted with fitusiran, as the intended population is males with hemophilia, and the clinical development program was conducted exclusively in males. Hemophilia A and B are X-linked recessive inherited bleeding disorders primarily affecting males (99%), and the likelihood of women of childbearing potential receiving fitusiran is very low. There were no adverse effects of fitusiran on male fertility or reproductive performance parameters assessed in Sprague-Dawley rats in the 26-week repeated dose chronic toxicity study. It is not known whether fitusiran can adversely impact the development of an embryo/fetus when administered to a pregnant woman.
L893: (b) (4)
L894: (b) (4) Additional detailed information is available in Section 13.
L895: **Table 56. Nonclinical Data for Product Labeling**
L896: Labeling Section	Nonclinical Data
8.1 Pregnancy	
There are no available data on QFITLIA use in pregnant women to evaluate for a drug-associated risk of major birth defects, and miscarriage or other adverse maternal or fetal outcomes. Reproduction studies in pregnant animals have not been conducted with fitusiran based on the target population being limited to male subjects. It is not known whether QFITLIA can cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman or can affect reproduction capacity. QFITLIA should be used during pregnancy only if the potential benefit for the mother outweighs the potential risks, including those to the fetus.
The estimated background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage for the indicated population is unknown. All pregnancies have a background risk of major birth defect, loss, or other adverse outcomes; In the U.S. general population, the estimated background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage in clinically recognized pregnancies is 2%-4% and 15%-20%, respectively.

8.2 Lactation	
There are no data on the presence of fitusiran or its metabolite in human milk, the effects on the breastfed child, or the effects on milk production. It is not known whether or not QFITLIA is safe for use during breastfeeding. The developmental and health benefits of breastfeeding should be considered along with the mother's clinical need for QFITLIA and any potential adverse effects on the breastfed infant from QFITLIA or from the underlying maternal condition.

8.3 Females and Males of Reproductive Potential	
Contraception
Females
Use of QFITLIA in women using hormonal contraceptives may increase the risk of thrombotic events. Estrogen based hormonal contraceptives are an established risk factor for thrombosis in women with inherited AT deficiency. Advise patients using hormonal contraceptives to use an alternative nonhormonal contraception prior to starting treatment with and while receiving QFITLIA.
L897: Labeling Section	Nonclinical Data
13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility	
(b) (4)
In a 2-year carcinogenicity study in Sprague Dawley rats, fitusiran was not carcinogenic up to the highest dose tested following subcutaneous administration at doses of 0.03, 0.1 or 0.3 mg/kg/week in males and 0.1, 0.3, or 1 mg/kg/week in females. All doses in males and females were below the maximum recommended human dose (MRHD) of 50 mg per month based on body surface area (BSA) comparisons. Fitusiran was not carcinogenic in Tg-rasH2 mice following subcutaneous administration for 26 weeks at doses of 0.05, 0.15, or 0.5 mg/kg/week in males and 0.025, 0.08, or 0.25 mg/kg/week in females.
Fitusiran was not mutagenic or clastogenic in a battery of genetic toxicity studies (i.e., in vitro bacterial reverse mutation assay, in vitro chromosomal aberration assay in human peripheral blood lymphocytes; in vivo bone marrow micronucleus test in rats).
(b) (4)
In a chronic repeat dose toxicology study in Sprague-Dawley rats, fitusiran-treated males were cohabitated with fitusiran-naïve females following subcutaneous administration at doses of 0.25, 0.5, or 1 mg/kg/week for 16 weeks. No adverse effects on male fertility endpoints were observed. All doses used in males were below the MRHD based on BSA comparisons.

L900: ## 9. Product Quality
L901: ## Approval
L902: The Office of Pharmaceutical Quality review team has assessed the application with respect to chemistry, manufacturing, and controls and has determined that it meets all applicable standards to support the identity, strength, quality, and purity that it purports. As such, the Office of Pharmaceutical Quality recommends approval of this NDA from a quality perspective; see finalized Integrated Quality Reviews in DARRTS dated January 17th, 2025 and March 26, 2025. A shelf life of 36 months is granted for the 20 mg drug product in a 0.2 mL single-dose vial (100 mg/mL) when stored either at 2°C to 8°C (36°F to 46°F) or at 15°C to 30°C (59°F to 86°F). A shelf life of 24 months is granted for the 50 mg drug product in a 0.5 mL single-dose prefilled pen (100 mg/mL) when stored at 2° C to 8° C. The 50 mg drug product may be stored at 15°C to 30°C (59°F to 86°F) for a single period of up to 3 months at the end of storage within the shelf life of 24 months.

L915: ## III. Additional Analyses and Information
L916: ## 12. Summary of Regulatory History
L917: Fitusiran was granted orphan drug designation (ODD) on August 16, 2013, for the treatment of hemophilia A (ODD #13-4028) and August 12, 2013, for the treatment of hemophilia B (ODD #13-4029).
L918: Fitusiran was granted fast track designation (FTD) on December 23, 2020, for routine prophylaxis to prevent or reduce the frequency of bleeding episodes in patients with hemophilia A or B with or without factor VIII or IX inhibitors.
L919: Fitusiran was granted breakthrough therapy designation (BTD) on December 6, 2023, for routine prophylaxis to prevent or reduce the frequency of bleeding episodes in patients with hemophilia B with factor IX inhibitors.
L920: On June 26, 2015, the Sponsor submitted a new IND under Section 505(i) of the Federal Food, Drug, and Cosmetic Act (IND 125632). Prior to the IND submission, one type B meeting was held on April 23, 2015, to discuss the Sponsor's overall chemistry, manufacturing, and controls, nonclinical data, and preliminary results from the ongoing phase 1 clinical study.
L921: On July 24, 2015, the Applicant was advised that the 30-day safety review of the application had concluded and that the study could proceed with their proposed clinical investigation for the treatment of patients with moderate to severe hemophilia A.
L922: On May 10, 2016, an End-of-Phase 2 meeting was held to discuss the Sponsor's nonclinical development plan and proposals for the planned phase 3 trials.
L923: On September 28, 2016, a carcinogenicity special protocol assessment request was submitted, and on November 2, 2016, an agreement communication was issued. On November 22, 2016, another carcinogenicity special protocol assessment request was submitted, and on December 21, 2016, an agreement communication was issued.
L924: On May 9, 2017, a prephase 3 meeting was held to discuss the proposed development of the phase 3 program in support of a marketing application for fitusiran.
L925: On September 8, 2017, the Sponsor was notified that all studies included in IND 125632 were being placed on full clinical hold based on the occurrence of a fatal case of cerebral venous sinus thrombosis reported for one of the subjects (27-year-old male with hemophilia A) from Bulgaria in Study ALN-AT3SC-002. On November 8, 2017, a Type A meeting was held to gain concurrence on the Sponsor's proposal for additional measures to mitigate the risk of thrombosis and to remove the clinical hold on studies with fitusiran following implementation of these measures and IRB approval of the amended clinical study protocols. The Sponsor proposed to amend the study to limit the subject population to hemophilia A and B subjects with inhibitors only. On December 14, 2017, the full clinical hold was removed after the Bleed Management Guide (BMG) was revised.
L926: On January 26, 2018, preliminary comments were sent for a type C meeting request to discuss the combination product development of fitusiran, including development of the proposed (b) (4) autoinjector and clinical bridging plan, as well as the proposed prefilled syringe with safety
L927: ## NDA 219019
## QFITLIA (fitusiran)
L928: device (PFS-S) human factors validation study approach and protocol. On January 30, 2018, the meeting scheduled for January 31, 2018, was canceled because the preliminary responses were clear, and no further discussion was needed.
L929: On October 30, 2020, the Sponsor voluntarily paused dosing to implement the antithrombin-based dose regimen (AT-DR) dose regimen due to analysis of thrombotic events.
L930: On December 10, 2020, the Sponsor resumed Studies EFC14768, EFC14769, EFC15110, LTE14762, and LTE15467, and introduced the AT-DR dose regimen.
L931: On November 29, 2021, the proprietary name QFITLIA was conditionally approved for fitusiran after a June 19, 2021, denial of the proposed name (b) (4).
L932: On June 7, 2022, Written Responses were issued to discuss the strategy intended to support registration of the prefilled pen for fitusiran.
L933: On June 4, 2023, Type C Written Responses was issued to discuss the NDA format in advance of a pre-NDA meeting for fitusiran.
L934: On September 18, 2023, a Type C guidance meeting was held to discuss the Sponsor's plans to submit an NDA for fitusiran. FDA converted the requested pre-NDA meeting to a Type C meeting because FDA did not have sufficient information to determine whether the Sponsor's proposed clinical data package was sufficient for filing. FDA provided feedback on the Sponsor's proposed approach to demonstrating efficacy and safety of fitusiran for the proposed indication.
L935: On March 19, 2024, a pre-NDA meeting was held to discuss FDA's concurrence regarding the Sponsor's plan to submit an NDA in March/April 2024 and to discuss the clinical data package to support the filing and review of the planned NDA, the proposed indication in the label, and planned NDA regulatory milestones.
L936: On March 28, 2024, the Applicant submitted an NDA for fitusiran to prevent or reduce the frequency of bleeding episodes in adults and adolescents \ge 12 years of age with hemophilia A or B, with or without factor VIII or IX inhibitors. The recommended dose regimen for fitusiran is a starting dose of 50 mg once every 2 months (Q2M), which may be escalated to 50 mg once monthly (QM) or de-escalated to 20 mg QM or Q2M based on antithrombin (AT) levels.

L945: ## 13.2. Individual Reviews of Studies Submitted With the New Drug Application
L946: ## 13.2.1. Primary Pharmacology
L947: Pharmacological activity of fitusiran was examined in hemophilia A and B animal models. Fitusiran was effective at enhancing coagulation and bleeding in these models, supporting the proposed prophylactic benefits of fitusiran. The summaries of these studies are shown in Table 57.
L948: **Table 57. Tabulated In Vitro and In Vivo Pharmacology Studies With Fitusiran**
L949: Study Details	Findings
Primary target sequence conservation across various species (per summary materials contained in the Applicant's submission)	Fitusiran is pharmacologically active in mice, rats, cynomolgus monkeys, and dogs because the sequences of antithrombin mRNAs (derived from the SERPINC1 genes) are highly conserved across species. The dog sequence contains a single nucleotide mismatch compared to others. Treatment with fitusiran demonstrated exaggerated pharmacodynamic effects of antithrombin suppression in all four healthy animal species
Study No: BIO-12020
Pharmacodynamic evaluation of fitusiran in wild-type mice following single subcutaneous injection (4 M/dose group)
Dose: PBS, 0.3, 1, 3, 10 and 30 mg/kg	A single SC dose of fitusiran to wild-type mice led to dose-dependent AT suppression (as measured by decreases in mRNA, antigen levels, and activity) of approximately 50% at 1 mg/kg on Day 3 postdose, and maximum AT protein suppression up to 95% at doses up to 30 mg/kg by Day 10. Greater than 80% AT suppression was maintained for approximately 28 days postdose at \ge 10 mg/kg.

Dose: PBS, 1, 3, 10 and 30 mg/kg	A single SC injection of fitusiran at 3, 10 or 30 mg/kg given to hemophilia A mice resulted in a dose dependent suppression of AT (as measured by decreases in mRNA, antigen levels, and activity) of approximately 80-85% at 30 mg/kg on Day 3 postdose compared to the PBS control. Evaluation of Thrombin Generation in Hemophilia B Factor IX Knock-Out Mice After Single Dose of Fitusiran (3 M/dose group)
Dose: PBS, 1, 3, 10 and 30 mg/kg	A single SC dose of 1, 3, or 30 mg/kg fitusiran administered to male HB mice resulted in a dose-dependent suppression of plasma AT levels up to 90% compared to the PBS control on Day 10 postdose. Dose-related increases of up to 155% in endogenous thrombin generation were observed when compared to PBS controls. The study demonstrated that fitusiran is capable of prolonged AT suppression and promoting enhanced thrombin generation following a single SC dose in the hemophilia B mouse model.

2 M/dose group
Dose: PBS and 30 mg/kg	A microvessel laser injury model was used to compare stable hemostatic clot formation in treated versus untreated hemophilia A and hemophilia B mice. A single SC dose of 1 or 30 mg/kg fitusiran administered to Factor VIII-/- knockout mice with hemophilia A (HA) on Day 0 led to a dose-dependent and rapid (180 seconds) accumulation of platelets and fibrin after acute injury induced on Day 10. The formation of thrombus was observed at all laser injury sites (55/55 sites) in all animals treated with fitusiran, which correlated with dose-related decreased liver AT mRNA levels (up to 93% at the 30 mg/kg dose).

L952: Study Details	Findings
Study No.: BIO-13023
Single or Repeat Dose Administration of Fitusiran in Cynomolgus Monkeys
Dose: 0.1, 1, 3, 10, 30 mg/kg (single)
3M/dose group	
Cynomolgus monkeys received single doses of 0.1, 1, 3, 10, 30 mg/kg or 3 weekly doses of 30 mg/kg fitusiran (AD-57213). Single SC administration showed a dose-dependent, durable lowering of AT protein levels at 1, 3, 10, and 30 mg/kg fitusiran. No pharmacologic activity was observed at 0.1 mg/kg with ~50% and >70% AT suppression for ~30 days at 1 mg/kg and 10 mg/kg, respectively. Single SC administration at 30 mg/kg showed minimal decreased mean fibrinogen (FIB) concentration and aPTT on Day 37 (~36% and ~21% respectively compared to Day -8 pretreatment). These changes correlated with increased D-Dimer concentrations peaking between Days 15 and 37 (peaks between 166% and 830%, individual values compared to pretreatment), decreased AT concentrations (\ge 90\%), and increased thrombin generation levels.
Figure 22. AT Suppression After Single Dose of Fitusiran in Monkeys
Source: from Applicant's submission
AT protein concentration from individual animals were normalized to their respective individual predose serum concentration at each time point. Each graphical data point represents the remaining AT for the group mean (N=3 animals, samples assayed in technical triplicates \pm standard deviation).
Abbreviation: AT, antithrombin
Repeated once-weekly administration of fitusiran for 3 weeks at 30 mg/kg was not tolerated, resulting in mortality in 3/3 animals due to moderate to severe thrombosis, which was primarily seen in the pulmonary vasculature, and/or moderate or severe right atrial thrombosis in the heart. More than 95% suppression of AT levels was maintained during the dosing period, which correlated with decreased AT mRNA levels in the liver. Hematological changes of marked increased D-Dimer values (peaks up to 3,887% and 36,259%), decreased red blood cell parameters, platelet counts, and/or FIB concentrations, were considered related to the exaggerated pharmacological effects of fitusiran.
This study demonstrated durable pharmacological activity of fitusiran in normal monkeys up to 30 mg/kg (12 times the MRHD based on BSA comparisons) following single dose.
L953: Study Details	Findings
Study No: BIO-13025A
Repeat Dose
Pharmacology Study in
Cynomolgus Monkeys by
Subcutaneous Doses of
Fitusiran with a 4-week
Recovery
3M/dose group	
The study evaluated different repeated dose regimens in monkeys (three males/group) of once weekly (QW) or once every other weekly (Q2W) dosing, as follows:
g) QW at 0.5 for 8 doses or 0.125 mg/kg for 13 doses
h) Q2W at 1 for 4 doses or 0.25 mg/kg for 13 doses
i) QW at 1.5 mg/kg for 6 doses
j) Q2W at 3 mg/kg for 3 doses
Cumulative weekly doses of 0.5 mg/kg or 1 mg/kg resulted in approximately 90% suppression of AT activity, and a steady-state AT suppression level of ~50% and ~65% at lower doses of 0.125 mg/kg and 0.25 mg/kg, respectively. Animals dosed at a higher cumulative weekly dose of 1.5 mg/kg (1.5 mg/kg QW or 3 mg/kg Q2W) had >90% AT suppression. Reduction of AT was associated with increased thrombin generation, with a roughly 3-fold increase in peak thrombin over baseline levels at approximately 80% AT reduction. The data showed a strong correlation between the peak thrombin values and the levels of AT.
Unscheduled deaths occurred in two monkeys at 3 mg/kg Q2W after 3 doses, which were caused by platelet depletion resulting from systemic clotting, which paradoxically led to severe systemic hemorrhage. These findings were consistent with the effects of the excessively suppressed AT activity of >90% and increase in D-Dimer values compared to predose. Monkeys given 1.5 mg/kg QW survived; clinical signs included swelling of the left leg and groin/inguinal areas, inguinal area redness, left leg lameness, decreased activity, small feces, paleness, and decreased food consumption. Changes in platelet counts were observed at 1.5 and 3 mg/kg, consistent with increased platelet consumption and a compensatory regenerative rebound response.
This study demonstrated durable pharmacological activity with repeat dosing up to 1.5 mg/kg (2× the MRHD based on BSA comparisons).

Study No: BIO-14035
A 6-Month
Pharmacodynamic
Evaluation of Fitusiran
Following Once Monthly
Subcutaneous Injection
of Cynomolgus Monkeys
Doses: 0.5, 1 and
2 mg/kg
3M/dose group	
Animals administered 0.5 and 1 mg/kg were dosed on Days 1, 29, 57, 85, 113, and 141. Animals administered 2 mg/kg were dosed on Days 1, 29, and 57.
Once-monthly doses of 0.5, 1 and 2 mg/kg fitusiran were well-tolerated and led to a dose-dependent reduction in AT activity of 30%, 40% and 70%, respectively. Repeated SC administration once monthly for 6 months led to further AT reduction of 40% at 0.5 mg/kg, 50% at 1 mg/kg and AT reduction of 80% at 2 mg/kg for 3 months. Reductions of AT increased thrombin generation and suggested a similar sustained suppression of AT, e.g., via a monthly clinical dosing regimen, may be effective in the intended patient population. Administration of fitusiran by once-monthly subcutaneous injection was well tolerated in monkeys at the highest dose of 2.0 mg/kg/month (3× the MRHD based on BSA comparisons).

2 M +2 F/dose group	
Six weeks of once-weekly fitusiran dosing suppressed AT activity by 65% and 75% at 0.25 and 0.5 mg/kg, respectively, in monkeys when compared to pretreatment. After the establishment of steady state AT suppression, severe hemophilia was transiently induced with a single intravenous dose (20,000 BU/kg) of an anti-Factor VIII antibody, which decreased FVIII activity and increased clotting times.

Higher doses of fitusiran could not be administered as they were not anticipated to be tolerated.

10 mg/kg	Single or once-weekly repeated dosing of fitusiran at 1.5, 3 or 10 mg/kg for 4 weeks to hemophilia A female dogs resulted in a dose-dependent inhibition of AT activity up to 85% after the final dose. Analysis of plasma thrombin suggested treatment with fitusiran increased thrombin compared to predose values.

L958: ## 13.2.2. Secondary Pharmacology
L959: **Table 61. Assessment for Off-Target Effects of Fitusiran**
L960: Study Details	Findings
Study No: BIO-13007
Study Title: In Vitro "Off-Target" Analysis of ALN-57213, the SERPINC1-Targeting siRNA Component of ALN-AT3	Addition of fitusiran to a human hepatoma cell line (HepG2) in vitro at concentrations ranging from 35.7fM to 10nM resulted in a dose-dependent inhibition of endogenous antithrombin (AT) mRNA levels with an IC50 of 49.6pM. The 19-mer core sequence of A-116861, the antisense strand of fitusiran, was evaluated for off-target potential by conducting a comprehensive search against the human transcriptomes within the human NCBI Refseq dataset. There were a total of 9 mRNA sequences predicted as potentially possessing off-target potential. These 9 transcripts were then assessed experimentally. No IC50 values were calculable for 8 of the 9 off-target transcripts at up to 10nM fitusiran. An IC50 near 10nM was calculated for the remaining transcript, which suggests a 200-fold difference between fitusiran's "on-target" binding and the most sensitive "off-target" transcript.
Analysis of Human for polymorphism variations in support of Fitusiran/BIO15011	A search of the NCBI dbSNP identified a single SNP within the AT gene SERPINC1 transcript NM_000488. The estimated frequency of rs144084678 SNP in the broader human population is <1/0.0002 that appears to fall outside the critical target recognition of fitusiran, suggesting negligible target impact.
Bioinformatic analysis (Study no. BIO-13007) conducted to identify siRNAs targeting the human SERPINC1 transcript in the NCBI Gene database showed no off-target suppression up to concentrations of 10nM, confirming the specificity of fitusiran to SERPINC1 (IC50 of 49.6pM or 0.0496nM).
L961: Source: Applicant's submission
L962: Abbreviations: AT, antithrombin; dbSNP, Single-Nucleotide Polymorphism database; IC50, half maximal inhibitory concentration; mRNA, messenger RNA; NCBI, National Center for Biotechnology Information; siRNA, small interfering RNA; SNP, single-nucleotide polymorphism
L963: ## 13.2.3. Safety Pharmacology
L964: Descriptions of safety pharmacology assessments are described below.
L965: **Table 62. Summary of Safety Pharmacology Studies and Findings**
L966: Study Details	Findings
Study No: PAT0287
Study Title: Effect of Fitusiran on Cloned hERG Potassium Channels Expressed in Human Embryonic Kidney Cells	
Fitusiran did not inhibit hERG at up to 10µM in a GLP-compliant study. The positive control induced statistically significant hERG inhibition confirming the sensitivity and validity of the test system to hERG inhibition.
Table 63. hERG Channel Current Inhibition by Fitusiran
Human Cardiac Ion Channel	Vehicle Control	Fitusiran (1µM)	Fitusiran (10µM)	Terfenadine (60nM)
hERG	1.6±0.8%	1.5±0.3%	3.4±0.2	84.6±3.9%
Source: Reviewer Constructed Table
The data suggest that the risk of fitusiran affecting cardiovascular function through hERG inhibition in humans is negligible at clinically relevant concentrations. There were no clinically significant ECG abnormalities reported in clinical trials with fitusiran.

Study No: ALN-AT3SC
A Cardiovascular and Respiratory Safety Pharmacology Study in Cynomolgus Monkey Using Telemetry
Doses: 0, 1, 3, or 10 mg/kg 0, 3 M/dose group	
In a GLP safety pharmacology study that evaluated both cardiovascular and respiratory function, male telemetered cynomolgus monkeys were subcutaneously administered a single dose of vehicle (0.9% sodium chloride for injection), 1, 3, or 10 mg/kg of fitusiran.
There were no fitusiran-related changes up to 72 hours postdose in systemic blood pressure parameters, heart rate, electrocardiographic intervals, or body temperature at SC doses up to 10 mg/kg (HED 194 mg/day or 4 times the MRHD based on BSA comparisons). There were no effects on respiratory rate or blood gas parameters at timepoints up to 24 hours postdose.
Plasma exposures (C_{max} 3490 ng/mL, Day 1) at the 10 mg/kg dose in male monkeys, exceeded the C_{max} (84.1 ng/mL) at the MRHD (50 mg) by approximately 42-fold.
Taken together, these results did not identify any cardiovascular or respiratory risk associated with fitusiran exposure.

L968: ## 13.2.4. Absorption, Distribution, Metabolism, and Excretion/Pharmacokinetics Studies
L969: Table 64. Summary of ADME/PK Studies
L970: Type of Study	Major Findings
Absorption
Study No: AT3-NCD13-006, AT3-NCD12-007
Species: Rats (9M +9F/dose group)
Dose: 0.3, 1, 3 mg/kg
Species: Monkeys (3M +3F/dose group)
Dose: 0.1, 0.3, 1 mg/kg.
Repeat once weekly SC dosing	Fitusiran was rapidly absorbed in both rats and monkeys. The plasma concentrations of fitusiran peaked at 0.5 hours in rats and 1-2 hours in monkeys and rapidly declined followed by distribution to tissues. The subcutaneous bioavailability was 33% in rats and 19% in monkeys at a dose of 3 mg/kg. The plasma terminal elimination was 1 hour in rats and 3.64 hours in monkeys.
Distribution
Study No: LPR1221
Binding of Fitusiran to Mouse, Rat, Dog, Monkey, and Human Plasma Proteins
Concentrations: 0.5, 1 5, 10, or 50 µg/mL.	The percent protein binding of fitusiran decreased with increasing drug concentrations. There was no apparent difference in plasma protein binding among the different species tested. The mean percentage of fitusiran plasma protein binding ranged from 93.4% to 47.3% for mouse plasma, 99.2% to 69.1% for rat plasma, 95.2% to 58.9% for dog plasma, 98.0% to 83.6% for monkey plasma, and 96.6% to 52.4% for human plasma over the concentration range of 0.5 to 50 µg/mL. Fitusiran has a very short half-life in plasma as it is rapidly cleared and distributed to tissues. Thus, the of plasma protein binding is unlikely to impact safety of the drug.
Study no: AT3-NCD13-002, AT3-NCD14-009
Species: Rats
Dose 3 mg/kg
Single dose IV or SC dosing
Dose: 0.25, 0.5, or 1 mg/kg/week.
Repeat SC doing for 26 weeks.	Fitusiran distributed mainly to liver (the primary target organ of pharmacology), followed by kidney and spleen. Following single IV dose administration of fitusiran at 3 mg/kg, the percentage of the dose in the liver and kidney were 29.1% and 2.39% after 1 hour, respectively. Following single SC administration of 3 mg/kg, the highest percentage of the dose was observed in the liver and kidney: 36.2% (after 4 h) and 1.03% (after 180 h), respectively. The liver tissue terminal elimination half-life (t_{1/2}) was approximately 55 hours and 60 hours by IV and SC, respectively. Exposures in other tissues (e.g., brain, thymus, thyroid, and heart) were negligible. After repeat SC administration to rats, limited accumulation was observed in liver; however, several-fold increase in fitusiran exposure was noted in the kidney.
Metabolism
Study No: BA13003
In vitro:
Matrix: serum and S9 liver fractions from mouse, rat, dog, monkey, and human.
In vivo
Matrix: Plasma and liver
Species:
Mice, dose 25 mg/kg
Rat, dog and monkey, 3 mg/kg	Fitusiran is metabolized by sequential loss of the three sugar moieties and GalNAc linkers from the GalNAc group from the sense strand. The antisense strand of fitusiran is metabolized to AS(N-1)3' in in vivo plasma and liver samples consistent with in vitro liver samples. The sense strand was metabolized to form major metabolites, S(N-10)5' and S(N-11)3' by endonuclease metabolism and S(N-3)3' by exonuclease metabolism in in vitro liver S9 fractions while it was metabolized to S(N-1)5' as major metabolite in in vivo liver samples.
Based on the metabolites identified in the in vitro and in vivo studies for the antisense and sense strands, the following metabolic pathways have been proposed by the Applicant for fitusiran.

L976: ## 13.2.4.1. Excretion
L977: Excretion of fitusiran was evaluated in a single subcutaneous (SC) dose of 3 mg/kg in rats (Study no. AT3-NCD13-002). Approximately 4.67% of the dose administered (3 mg/kg) was recovered in the urine over 8 weeks, with most of the dose (~3.75%) recovered in the first 24 hours postdose. Fecal excretion of fitusiran has not been evaluated. In subjects with hemophilia, mean recovery of intact fitusiran in 0 to 24 hours urine was 14.6% at the recommended dose of 50 mg.
L978: ## 13.2.5. Toxicology
L979: ## 13.2.5.1. General Toxicology
L980: Repeat-dose toxicology studies were conducted in healthy rats for up to 26 weeks, healthy monkeys for up to 39 weeks, and in hemophilia mice for up to 26 weeks. Fitusiran is pharmacologically active in mice, rats, monkeys, and dogs because the antithrombin mRNA sequences are highly conserved across species. Animals were dosed once weekly by the SC route. Key findings from these studies are summarized below.
L981: ## 13.2.5.1.1. A 26-Week Subcutaneous Injection Toxicity, Toxicokinetic, and Fertility and Early Embryonic Development (to Implantation) Study in the Rat Followed By a 13-Week Recovery Period (AT3-NCD14-009)
L982: ## Key Study Findings
L983: ## Toxicity
L984: - Once-weekly SC administration of fitusiran for 26 weeks was well tolerated by female rats up to 1 mg/kg/week and by male rats up to 0.5 mg/kg/week.
- Unscheduled deaths occurred in 5/5 males at 1 mg/kg/week. The cause of deaths was excessive clotting (leading to platelet reductions and, paradoxically, systemic hemorrhage), necrosis, and/or thrombosis in various organs and tissues, consistent with the exaggerated pharmacology of fitusiran.
- In surviving animals, microscopic findings were observed at the injection site secondary to the experimental procedure (needle trauma associated with the injection procedure), that resolved by the end of the recovery period.
- Dose-related decreases in antithrombin protein levels were observed during the dosing period consistent with the pharmacology of fitusiran.
- The no observed adverse effect level (NOAEL) was the high dose (HD) of 1 mg/kg/week in females, based on the absence of toxicity, and the MD of 0.5 mg/kg/week in males, based on severe clotting. Both doses represent doses below the maximum recommended human dose (MRHD) of 50 mg per month based on BSA comparisons.
L985: ## Juvenile Toxicity
L986: There were no drug-related adverse effects on growth or physical development in juvenile rats given up to 1 mg/kg/week fitusiran by once-weekly SC dosing up to Day 58 (equivalent to week 4 to 8 age). The NOAEL in juvenile rats was 1 mg/kg/week (below the MRHD of 50 mg per month based on BSA comparisons), based on no adverse findings up to 1 mg/kg/week.
L987: ## Reproductive Performance
L988: There were no fitusiran-related impacts on male fertility or early embryonic development up to 1 mg/kg/week. The NOAEL for male reproductive effects was 1 mg/kg/week (below the MRHD of 50 mg per month based on BSA comparisons).

L994: **Table 66. Observations and Results, ALN-AT3SC**
L995: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	
Unscheduled deaths occurred in male rats: 1 each in control, LD, and MD, and 10 in HD. The HD males 4029, 4033, 4034, 4113, and 4123 were found dead on Days 175, 49, 78, 110 and 110, respectively. Cause of deaths in 5/5 at high dose in the main study was considered related to the exaggerated pharmacology of fitusiran and consisted of systemic clotting (which led to platelet reductions and, paradoxically, to minimal to marked systemic hemorrhage). Clinical signs noted in one male (#4123) at HD included decreased activity, shallow breathing, and cold to the touch prior to death. Cause of deaths in control was not determined, and the deaths in LD and MD were not fully investigated. Five male rats that were found dead in the TK study were either not fully investigated (2/5) or associated with blood collection procedures.
Macroscopic findings correlated with hemorrhage at 1 mg/kg/week including swelling, thickened or mass in subcutis, thymus, adipose tissue, enlargement of various lymph nodes, prostate, adrenal, tongue, abnormal consistency-firm prostate, seminal vesicle, dark focus in lungs, epididymis, various lymph nodes, stomach, subcutis, thymus, material accumulation-clot in skeletal muscle, pelvic body cavity, and pancreas.
Microscopic findings in the unscheduled deaths included minimal to marked hemorrhage/thrombosis/necrosis in multiple organ and tissues.

Bone Marrow Evaluation	No differences were observed in the myeloid:erythroid (M:E) ratios in the 300-cell bone marrow differential count, and there were no observations in the maturation of the hematopoietic cell lineages on Day 58 or at the end of the main (Day 184) and recovery (Day 275) periods related to the administration of fitusiran.
Gross pathology	There were no gross findings related to fitusiran in the main or recovery studies.
Organ weights	There were no fitusiran-related organ weight changes in the main or recovery studies (Days 58, 184, and 275).
Histopathology	The most frequent microscopic changes at the scheduled necropsy observed at the subcutaneous injection site were minimal to mild perivascular mononuclear cell infiltration, minimal myofiber degeneration, minimal to moderate acute hemorrhage, and minimal subacute inflammation on Day 58 and Day 184. These changes were not considered to be related to fitusiran based on similar incidence and severity in control and treated animals. They were considered secondary to the experimental procedure (needle trauma associated with the injection procedure).
Increased incidence of tubular testicular degeneration and marked oligospermia were observed in MD and HD on Day 58, and in HD on Day 184. At the end of the recovery period, Day 275, the testis findings appeared to resolve, however, marked oligospermia was present in one recovery animal at HD.
Findings of increased incidence of minimal to mild rosette in the eye were noted at HD in males on Day 58, and in one male and one female on Day 184 and Day 275. In short-term single-dose studies (Study No. AT3-NCD12-001 and Study AT3-NCD12-002), findings of porphyrin staining (eyes, nose, paws) were observed at ≥3 mg/kg. No data on the distribution of fitusiran in the eyes were provided. Thus, the toxicological significance of this finding could be related to systemic toxicity due to exaggerated pharmacology of fitusiran.
There were findings in pancreas at terminal necropsy in one male at HD with mild acinar cell atrophy, in one control male, and in one HD female at recovery necropsy. Additional findings at recovery necropsy included minimal hemorrhage, and mild inflammation in males at HD. Findings in pancreas were also observed in the 7-week repeat dose rat study with adverse atrophy/single cell necrosis and/or hemorrhage at 3 mg/kg/week (unscheduled deaths), and mild acinar atrophy in the
L1019: Parameters	Major Findings
39-week monkey study in 1 male at HD (0.5 mg/kg/week). These findings are considered likely related to fitusiran treatment based on consistent findings in multiple studies/species.

Toxicokinetic	Fitusiran concentrations were \leLLOQ of 50.0 ng/mL for LD (0.25 mg/kg) on Days 1, 92, and 183, and for MD (0.5 mg/kg) on Day 1. Exposure to fitusiran (C_{max} and AUC) generally increased with the increase in dose level from 0.5 to 1 mg/kg on Days 92 and 183. Sex differences in exposure were generally less than 2-fold, except for the AUC in males, 3.7-fold, and 2.7-fold higher at 0.5 mg/kg/week on Day 92 and Day 183, respectively. Plasma concentrations reflect rapid clearance and uptake by tissues (primarily liver and kidney) indicating shorter AUC distribution phase.

L1046: ## 13.2.5.1.2. A 39-Week Subcutaneous Injection Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study in the Cynomolgus Monkey Followed by a 13-Week Recovery Period (AT3-NCD14-007)
L1047: ## **Key Study Findings**
L1048: - Once-weekly SC administration of fitusiran for 39 weeks was well tolerated by male and female monkeys up to the highest dose of 0.5 mg/kg/week.
- Fitusiran-related toxicities included elevated D-dimer values at all doses during the dosing period that correlated with decreases in AT levels and tended to recover at the end of recovery period.
- The NOAEL was determined to be the HD, 0.5 mg/kg/week, for both males and females, based on absence of adverse fitusiran-related effects, which is lower than the MRHD of 50 mg per month based on BSA comparisons.

Clinical signs	Skin redness/dryness/scab, thin cover or staining of the fur, and occasional occurrences of liquid/soft feces were noted in all dose groups, including control, and considered incidental and unrelated to fitusiran due to their low incidence and/or lack of dose relationship.

D-dimer	Nonadverse dose-related increased D-dimer levels were observed in males at \ge 0.3 mg/kg/week and females at \ge 0.15 mg/kg/week on Day 92, and at all dose levels with no dose response on Day 183. The D-dimer levels stayed elevated in females on Day 274 and tended to recover following the recovery period. Elevated D-dimer concentrations were also observed in an exploratory single-dose pharmacodynamic study (Study No. BIO-13023), and 7-week repeat-dose toxicity study (Study no. AT3-NCD12-007) in monkeys that were adverse (>500-fold). These findings were correlated with decreased platelet counts, decreased FIB values, and thrombosis consistent with the intended procoagulant effects of target antithrombin protein suppression. Increases in fibrin D-Dimer have been reported in clinical studies with fitusiran.

Organ weights	There were no fitusiran-related organ weight changes noted at the termination of the main or recovery study animals.
L1057: Parameters	Major Findings
Histopathology	Injection site findings (acute hemorrhage and/or presence of inflammatory cells) were similar in control and treated animals from all dose groups and were considered secondary to the experimental procedures (i.e., subcutaneous injection).
L1058: Effects on male reproductive organs were observed that included mild to severe immaturity in epididymis, prostate gland, and seminal vesicle gland at \ge 0.15 mg/kg/week and marked to severe immaturity in testis in control and fitusiran-treated animals consistent with small size of the male reproductive organs at the end of recovery necropsy. However, the organ weights relative to body weight either show no change or increase. These findings are likely related to differences in the sexual maturity of the monkeys, therefore were considered unrelated to administration of fitusiran.
L1059: Additional nonadverse findings included effects on pancreas including minimal angiectasis of islet of Langerhans in a male (LD) and a female (MD) on Day 184 and mild acinar atrophy with ductular hyperplasia in male at HD on day 365 (recovery). Acinar atrophy was also noted in rats in the 26-week chronic study and considered adverse (atrophy/single cell necrosis, hemorrhage at 5\times MRHD) in the 7-week rat study. Although this finding is seen infrequently as a background finding in cynomolgus monkeys, relationship to fitusiran cannot be excluded in light of similar findings in rats.
