L1074: ## 13.2.5.1.3. A 7-Week Repeat Subcutaneous Injection Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study in the Albino Rat With a 2-Month Recovery Period (AT3-NCD13-006)
L1075: ## Key Findings
L1076: - Unscheduled deaths occurred in seven male and six female rats at the HD of 3 mg/kg/week, which were caused by the exaggerated pharmacology of fitusiran (thrombosis in various organs and tissues), which led to platelet reductions and paradoxical systemic hemorrhage.
- Hepatobiliary toxicity was observed in some animals at the high dose, consisting of elevated liver enzymes and adverse histopathology findings that were associated with extensive and prolonged pharmacodynamic activity of fitusiran that led to systemic thrombosis and/or hemorrhage at &gt;80% reduction in AT protein levels.
- A NOAEL of 1 mg/kg/week was considered based on deaths and adverse toxicities at 3 mg/kg/week (less than the MRHD of 50 mg per month based on BSA comparisons).
L1077: **Table 85. Study Information, AT3-NCD13-006**
L1078: Study Features and Methods	Details
GLP compliance	Yes
Dose and frequency of dosing	0, 0.3, 1, or 3 mg/kg once weekly
Route of administration	SC
Formulation/vehicle	0.9% Sodium Chloride for Injection, USP
Species/strain	Sprague-Dawley rats
Number/sex/group	15
Age	8 weeks old
Toxicokinetics	3/sex in control and 9/sex in treatment
L1079: Source: Applicant's submission
L1080: Abbreviations: GLP, good laboratory practice; SC, subcutaneous; USP, United States Pharmacopeia
L1081: ## Unscheduled Euthanasia
L1082: Seven males and six females given fitusiran at 3 mg/kg were found dead or euthanized on days ranging from 7 to 11 and 16 to 34, respectively. Because of the earlier mortalities and adverse clinical signs in the males at 3 mg/kg/week, this entire group was terminated and necropsied on Days 15 and 16, along with 3 control-group males. Histopathology evaluations were performed only on the unscheduled animals from the main study and not on the animals from the toxicokinetic (TK) study.
L1083: - Clinical signs prior to death included decreased activity, cold to touch; swollen firm areas (e.g., hind paws, cranium, ventral cervical), swollen soft areas (e.g., ventral cervical, cranium, tongue, forelimbs, hind paws), red/black fur staining (e.g., around eyes, forelimbs, head, muzzle, urogenital); labored/shallow breathing; thin fur; and dry skin on tail.
- Clinical pathology parameters in 1 male and 2 female rats compared to control on Day 15/16 showed decreases in platelets (-68%), reticulocytes (-40%), and lymphocytes (-38%), and increases in neutrophils (+390%), WBC (+48%), monocytes (+133%). Changes in coagulation included activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT) (+37%) and FIB concentration (+22%). Clinical chemistry changes showed slight increases in alanine
L1084: aminotransferase (ALT) (+54%) and alkaline phosphatase (ALP) (+77%) activity, with increased total bilirubin concentrations (+128%).
L1085: - Clinical pathology findings correlated with microscopic findings in the liver (single cell necrosis, increased mitoses, mononuclear infiltration, and effects of biliary duct hyperplasia or dilation). Dosing was discontinued for the remaining HD males, and they were sacrificed on Day 15/16. Additional hematology findings included marked platelet clumps in female rat 4006.
- Macroscopic findings correlated with hemorrhage and/or edema and thrombosis and included dark discoloration/focus in several organs including adrenals, kidneys, lymph nodes (mandibular, mesenteric), testis, and thymus. In addition, the subcutis was thickened with or without dark discoloration. In Animal No. 4503, the tongue was thickened and dark. In Animal No. 4005, the penis was protruded, thickened, and dark. The gross changes described above usually correlated with hemorrhage microscopically. Dark fluid/material (compatible with blood) was seen in the urinary bladder, muscle, and thymus of a few rats. In one rat (No. 4006) there was a dilatation of the bile duct.
- Microscopic findings included hemorrhage, necrosis, edema, and/or thrombosis in various organs and tissues (e.g., nasal cavity, larynx, lungs, trachea, brain, eye, mandibular lymph node, penis, prostate, subcutis, and tongue), and liver changes recorded in few animals including single cell necrosis, increased mitoses and biliary (biliary duct) hyperplasia, and lymphoid necrosis/depletion in lymph nodes (mandibular and mesenteric), GALT, spleen, and thymus.

L1091: ## Early Euthanasia (Day 15/16)
L1092: Microscopic findings in scheduled euthanasia of HD males included minimal to moderate hemorrhage and/or thrombosis in multiple organs including brain, lung, pancreas, and femorotibial joint.
L1093: Compared to control on Day 44, mild to moderate increases in neutrophil (+68%), lymphocyte (+42%) and monocyte (+100%) counts, reflected in total white blood cell counts (+48%), increases in aspartate aminotransferase (AST) (+117%), ALT (+184%), ALP (+45%), with mild to marked increases in total bilirubin (+372%), cholesterol (+26%), triglyceride (+29%), and globulin (+20%) concentrations were observed at 3 mg/kg/week correlated with histological findings in the liver with minimal to moderate necrosis (focal to multifocal), minimal single cell necrosis, minimal to moderate biliary hyperplasia, minimal portal mononuclear cell infiltration, and minimal increased mitoses.
L1094: ## Scheduled Euthanasia (Day 44)
L1095: Clinical signs: Hypersensitivity to the touch, skin redness/dryness/scab, and thin cover or staining of the fur were noted in males at 0.3 and 1 mg/kg/week.
L1096: Minimal increases in ALP in females at 0.3 (+14%), and 1 mg/kg (+14%) resolved at the end of the recovery period. Histopathology findings included minimal brain hemorrhage in one male at 1 mg/kg and minimal thrombosis in the lungs in two males at 0.3 mg/kg, one male and one female at 1 mg/kg, and one female at 3 mg/kg, and minimal thrombosis of the larynx in one female at 3 mg/kg/week was observed.

L1125: ## 13.2.5.1.4. A 7-Week Repeat Subcutaneous Injection Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study in the Cynomolgus Monkey With a 2-Month Recovery Period (AT3-NCD12-007)
L1126: ## Key Findings
L1127: - Unscheduled deaths occurred in two male monkeys at 1 mg/kg/week with progressive increases in D-dimer values, decrease in fibrinogen, and decrease in platelets (in one monkey) associated with moderate multifocal cerebral necrosis with hemorrhage and moderate thrombosis or minimal pulmonary vessel thrombosis following second dose administration.
- A NOAEL of 1 mg/kg/week was considered based on deaths and adverse toxicities at 3 mg/kg/week (2 times the MRHD of 50 mg per month based on BSA comparisons).

L1132: ## Unscheduled Euthanasia
L1133: Two male monkeys given fitusiran at high dose 1 mg/kg were found dead on Day 22 (4001) and Day 35 (4004). Both animals had changes in coagulation parameters related to fitusiran treatment. Clinical signs included soft swelling of the ventral cervical and cranial regions.
L1134: - Animal #4001 showed progressive increases in D-dimer on Day 15 and 22 (26% to 543%), minimal to mild decreases in fibrinogen concentration (-8% to -26%), and a moderate decrease in platelet count (-58%) compared to individual pretreatment values.
- Animal #4004 had progressive increases in D-dimer (31% to 386% individual values compared to second prestudy value) and mild decreases in fibrinogen concentration (-18% to -26%) between Days 15 and 29, and no noticeable change in platelet count compared to individual pretreatment values.
- These findings correlated macroscopically with moderate multifocal cerebral necrosis with hemorrhage and moderate thrombosis in animal 4004 and chronic and minimal pulmonary vessel thrombosis in animal 4001.
L1135: In surviving animals, clinical observations of skin redness/dryness/scab and thin cover or staining of the fur were observed in all dose groups. Clinical pathology findings included minimal to mild increases in D-dimer values in males at \ge 0.1 mg/kg (83% to 598%) and in females at \ge 0.3 mg/kg (55% to 139%) compared to control on Day 44, and mild decreases in FIB
L1136: at 1.0 mg/kg (-21% to -33% compared to pretreatment values) correlated with decreases in AT protein and activity levels.

L1169: ## 13.2.5.2. Other Toxicology Studies
L1170: ## 13.2.5.2.1. A 26-Week Repeat Subcutaneous Injection Toxicity Study in the Transgenic Hemophilia A Mouse (AT3-NCD14-008)
L1171: ## Key Findings
L1172: - Unscheduled deaths occurred in control untreated hemophilia mice (40%), and in fitusiran treated hemophilia mice 10 (3%) and 30 (8.5%) mg/kg/week caused by hemorrhage mainly at the injection site, associated with hemophilia condition.
- Fitusiran-related findings included dose-related decreases in AT protein levels and decreases in aPTT at both fitusiran dose levels, supporting the reduced incidence of mortality/morbidity secondary to hemorrhage in fitusiran-treated groups.
- The study demonstrates therapeutic benefit of fitusiran in hemophilia A mice when compared to untreated hemophilia mice.
- A NOAEL could not be determined because of unscheduled deaths at both fitusiran doses evaluated in this study.

L1177: Unscheduled deaths occurred in 40% of control mice (11/35 males and 15/35 females) and 6% of fitusiran-treated animals (1 male and 1 female at 10 mg/kg [3%], and 3 males and 3 females at 30 mg/kg [8.5%]). Findings included hemorrhage at injection site, intestine, nasal cavity, spinal cord, and subcutis in the control and injection site, ear, subcutis in the fitusiran-treated mice, suggesting bleeding in association with hemophilia condition. Clinical signs noted prior to death in control animals and fitusiran-treated mice included decreased activity, weakness, partly closed eyes, cold to touch, suspected dehydration, hunched posture, and labored breathing. Mortality attributed to lethal hemorrhage in the control group correlated with the hemostatic disease condition in this strain of mice.

L1198: **Table 102. Toxicological Effects of Fitusiran in Dogs With Hemophilia A**
L1199: Type of Study	Major Findings
Study No: BIO-13027
Study Title: Pharmacology of Fitusiran in Hemophilia A Dogs After Repeat SC Dosing	
Although this study was designed to assess the pharmacodynamic activity in female HA dogs (see primary pharmacology section), routine hematology and clinical chemistry parameters and limited histopathology were assessed. Fitusiran at 10 mg/kg/week in dogs was not tolerated after the 4th dose, producing adverse liver and kidney findings including increases in ALP (32 to 93-fold), ALT (34 to 93-fold), AST (12 to 18-fold), and total bilirubin (2 to 110-fold) and decrease in platelets (33 to 82%).
Based on the degree of fitusiran-related clinical pathology parameter changes, two dogs with HA were humanely euthanized 17 days after the 4th dose of 10 mg/kg/week (26 times the MRHD of 50 mg per month based on BSA comparisons); macroscopic findings included both livers with abnormal appearance (discolored, mottled, pitted). There was no evidence of thrombosis or hemorrhage in the mesenteric vasculature. Microscopic findings were observed in the liver and kidney. Gallbladders were not evaluated.
Liver: minimal to mild hepatocellular degeneration/necrosis and mild to moderate inflammation in both dogs; moderate acute/chronic hemorrhage, and/or sinusoidal congestion. There was no evidence of thrombosis or fibrin clots in the liver.
Kidneys: minimal tubular degeneration and/or necrosis, minimal regeneration, and minimal dilatation, minimal tubule epithelia pigment (presumably hemosiderin).
L1200: Source: Applicant's submission
L1201: Abbreviations: ALT, alanine aminotransferase; ALP, alkaline phosphatase; AST, aspartate aminotransferase; BSA, body surface area; HA, hemophilia A; MRHD, maximum recommended human dose; SC, subcutaneous

The Applicant noted that these impurities will be controlled to a Threshold of Toxicological Concern-based acceptable intake of a mutagenic impurity of 1.5 µg per person per day.
Study nos. AT3-NCD14-010 and AT3-GLP16-021
5-week repeat-dose toxicity studies in rats with fitusiran	Five-week repeat-dose toxicity studies were conducted in rats to evaluate novel impurities of fitusiran to be used in the chronic toxicity studies and clinical trials during development. (drug substance (DS) lot 207305 (Drug Product [DP] Batch No. P02214or DS lot 207068 (DP Batch No. P02314). A total of 6 relative retention times (RRT) impurity groups were identified based on analytical methods. There were no new toxicities related to impurities in the 5-week rat studies at [REDACTED](b) (4)mg/kg/week with both DS lots used in the DP batches.
L1209: Source: Applicant's submission
L1210: Abbreviations: DEREK, Deductive Estimation of Risk from Existing Knowledge; DP, drug product; DS, drug substance; GLP, good laboratory practice; LOD, limit of detection; QSAR, quantitative structure-activity relationship; RRT, relative retention times
L1211: ## **Qualification of Impurities**
L1212: The toxicity of the DS lot 207305 (Drug Product [DP] Batch No. P02214) was evaluated in three chronic repeat-dose GLP subcutaneous toxicity studies: 1) a 26-week study in HA mice (study No. AT3-NCD14-008); 2) a 26-week study in rats (study No. AT3-NCD14-009); and 3) a 39-week study in cynomolgus monkeys (AT3-NCD14-007). Doses of fitusiran \ge 1 mg/kg/week caused mortality in rats and monkeys caused by exaggerated pharmacology that resulted in significantly lower NOAELs [REDACTED](b) (4) mg/kg/week) that were below the MRHD of 50 mg per month based on BSA comparisons. The Applicant utilized the toxicology data from the 26-week study in mice with HA that provided an evaluation of safety relative to dosing of patients with hemophilia. In the 26-week toxicology study with hemophilia mice, a NOAEL was not determined based on unscheduled deaths at both fitusiran doses evaluated in this study (3% at 10 mg/kg/week and 8.5% at 30 mg/kg/week). However, these deaths were predominantly attributed to the inherent hemophilia condition and were not considered related to fitusiran or the impurities present in the lot, as there were more deaths in vehicle-control hemophilia mice (40%). Both doses of fitusiran were effective pharmacologically when compared to vehicle-dosed control animals, and treatment resulted in fewer deaths and the anticipated pharmacodynamic activity, which supports the use of this study for impurity qualification.
L1213: The safety margins (animal:human) of the impurities in the drug substance were estimated using the highest dose in the study, 30 mg/kg. The safety margins for the impurities at the percentage proposed acceptance criteria are [REDACTED](b) (4)-fold higher. Therefore, the proposed impurity levels in drug substance are considered adequately qualified from a nonclinical perspective.

L1219: ## 13.2.5.3. Carcinogenicity Studies
L1220: ## **Study No/ Study Title: ALN-AT3SC: A 2-Year Subcutaneous Injection Carcinogenicity Study in Rats**
L1221: Good laboratory practice (GLP) compliance: Yes
L1222: ## Key Study Findings
L1223: In agreement with doses recommended by CDER's Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee, Sprague Dawley rats were administered fitusiran at subcutaneous doses of 0, 0.03, 0.1, or 0.3 mg/kg/week to males and 0, 0.1, 0.3, or 1 mg/kg/week to females once weekly for 2 years. The highest doses were based on mortality due to hemorrhagic thrombosis at \ge 1 mg/kg in males and at 3 mg/kg in females in a 26-week and a 7-week repeat dose toxicity study, respectively. The survival rate in fitusiran-treated groups was comparable to control groups. There were no dose-dependent or statistically significant fitusiran-related neoplastic findings noted in either male or female rats up to the highest dose tested. All fitusiran dose levels tested in male and female rats were below the MRHD based on BSA comparisons.
L1224: NDA 219019
QFITLIA (fitusiran)
L1225: ## Adequacy of Carcinogenicity Study
L1226: The Committee concluded that the 2-year carcinogenicity study conducted in Sprague-Dawley rats with fitusiran was adequate and there was no evidence of drug-related neoplasms in male or female rats.
L1227: ## Study No/ Study Title: AT3-GLP17-001 / ALN-AT3SC: A 26-Week Subcutaneous Injection Carcinogenicity Study in TgRasH2 Mice
L1228: Good laboratory practice (GLP) compliance: Yes
L1229: ## Key Study Findings
L1230: In agreement with doses recommended by CDER's Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee, Tg.RasH2 mice were administered fitusiran at subcutaneous doses of 0, 0.05, 0.15, or 0.5 mg/kg/week to males and 0, 0.025, 0.08, or 0.25 mg/kg/week to females once weekly for 26 weeks. The highest doses were selected based on body weight gain decrements in a dose range finding study in wild-type littermates. A positive control group of Tg.RasH2 mice was administered a single intraperitoneal dose of N-methyl-N-nitrosourea (MNU) at 75 mg/kg on Day 1. The survival rate in fitusiran-treated animals was comparable to control animals. No statistically significant increases in dose-response relationships or pairwise comparisons were observed for neoplastic findings after exposure to doses of fitusiran up to 0.5 mg/kg. The positive control MNU induced increased incidences of malignant squamous cell carcinoma and papilloma of the stomach in male and female RasH2 mice relative to control.
L1231: ## ECAC Conclusions
L1232: The Committee concluded that the 26-week carcinogenicity study conducted in Tg.RasH2 mice with fitusiran was adequate and there was no evidence of drug-related neoplasms in male and female mice.
L1233: ## 13.2.5.4. Reproductive Toxicology
L1234: No reproductive and developmental toxicity studies were conducted with fitusiran in female animals because the intended population is almost exclusively male, and the clinical development program was conducted exclusively in males. Hemophilia A and B are X-linked recessive inherited bleeding disorders predominantly affecting males (99%), and the likelihood of women of childbearing potential receiving fitusiran is very low; therefore, only a male fertility study was integrated into the 26-week repeated dose chronic toxicity study in rats. There were no fitusiran-related effects on male fertility and early embryonic development up to 1 mg/kg/week (below the MRHD based on BSA comparisons). It is not known whether fitusiran can adversely impact the development of embryo/fetus when administered to a pregnant woman. Only male subjects were enrolled in clinical trials with fitusiran. (b) (4)
L1235: ## Female Fertility and Early Embryonic Development
L1236: Because hemophilia is primarily an X-linked disease, no embryo-fetal toxicity studies in rats and rabbits have been performed.
L1237: ## Juvenile Animal Studies
L1238: Non-GLP range finding (AT3-DSM16-001) and repeat-dose tolerability (AT3-NCD14-005) studies in juvenile rats were conducted. Four-day-old pups (n=8/sex/group) were administered fitusiran by SC administration with doses of 0, 1, 3, or 10 mg/kg/week \times4 or 0, 0.5, and 1.0 mg/kg/week \times5.
L1239: ## **Results**
L1240: Study AT3-DSM16-001: At 10 mg/kg/week, mortality was observed in 7 pups (6 males and 1 female) occurring between Study Days 19 and 28, due to jugular venipuncture or head trauma based on swelling or clotting in the ventral cervical region (4 pups) or in the cranial cavity (1 of those 4 pups) likely due to the pharmacological effect of fitusiran. One pup had poor and/or deteriorating condition that led to unscheduled euthanasia, likely due to unilateral findings of irregular surface and mottled discoloration in the kidney. The cause of the death was undetermined for the remaining two pups.
L1241: Clinical signs in these moribund pups included abnormal gait, decreased activity, cold to touch, incoordination, eyes partly closed, skin pallor, and labored breathing. Decreased body weight gains were noted at 10 mg/kg/week.
L1242: ## Toxicokinetics
L1243: ## *Plasma*
L1244: Plasma TK parameters at 1 mg/kg/week were below the lower limit of quantitation on Days 4 postpartum (PP) and 25 PP. There were no sex differences in exposure (C_{max} and AUC); the data are presented for males and females combined.

L1255: Study AT3-NCD14-005: Five weekly SC administrations of fitusiran up to 1 mg/kg/week were well tolerated. There were no adverse findings in clinical signs, body weights, and clinical pathology parameters including hematology, clinical chemistry, and coagulation in fitusiran-treated juvenile rats compared to control juvenile rats. L1257: Study Type/ Study No	Key Study Findings
Local Tolerance
ALN-AT3SC: A Single Dose Subcutaneous Local Tolerability Study in Male New Zealand White Rabbit
Fitusiran was administered as a single SC injection in the lumbar area of male New Zealand rabbits (n=3/group) at 0, 50 mg/site or 200 mg/site using 2 mL dosing volume	
There were no findings of erythema at 50 or 200 mg/site or edema at 50 mg/site. At 200 mg/site, increased incidence of slight edema was observed in all 3 animals between 1-4 hours postinjection compared to control, that resolved 6 hours postinjection. Gross pathology findings included dark focus at the injection site in 2 animals at 50 mg/site, correlated histologically with minimal focal subcutaneous hemorrhage likely related to procedure. Nonadverse histopathology findings were observed at the injection site in 2 animals at 200 mg/site that included minimal multifocal mixed (neutrophilic and histiocytic) cell inflammation in the subcutaneous tissues or minimal focal thrombosis of a deep dermal blood vessel, and mild multifocal neutrophilic inflammation in the epidermis (surface and follicular), and dermis, correlated with edema.
Overall, single SC administration of fitusiran up to 200 mg/site was well tolerated by New Zealand rabbits.

Immunotoxicity
Evaluation of AD-57213 Cytokine Induction in an in vitro Human Whole Blood Assay/BIO13003
A DOTAP mock-transfection control was also included as a transfection control.	
The mean cytokine responses to fitusiran transfected to human whole blood at 300nM were less than 2-fold higher than medium alone for all 13 cytokines (IL-1\beta, IL-RA, IL-6, IL-8, IL-10, IL-12 (p70), granulocyte colony-stimulating factor (G-CSF), IFN-\gamma, interferon-inducible protein 10 (IP-10), macrophage inflammation protein 1 alpha (MIP-1\alpha), macrophage inflammation protein 1 beta (MIP-1\beta), monocyte chemoattractant protein 1 (MCP-1) and TNF-\alpha measured for the four human donors. Thus, fitusiran was not considered to induce these cytokine or chemokines.

L1264: ## 14.1.1.1. Study AT3-RS13-001: Plasma Protein Binding of Fitusiran
L1265: The Applicant evaluated the binding of fitusiran to animal (mouse, rat, monkey, dog) and human plasma proteins. Briefly, as detailed in the nonclinical study report LPR1221 (submitted on September 16, 2024, Module 4.2.2.3), in vitro human plasma protein binding of fitusiran at concentrations of 0.5, 1, 5, 10, 25, and 50 \mug/mL was 96.6%, 95.6%, 90.2%, 83.9%, 78.7% and 52.4% respectively.
L1266: ## 14.1.2. Metabolism and Transport Characterization, and Drug-Drug Interaction Studies
L1267: ## 14.1.2.1. In Vitro Metabolic Stability Study BA13003
L1268: Study BA13003 evaluated the in vitro metabolic stability of fitusiran in mouse, rat, dog, monkey, and human serum and liver S9 fractions, and conducted its in vitro and in vivo metabolic profiling. The proposed metabolic pathway of fitusiran is presented in Figure 26 below.
L1269: Figure 26. Proposed Metabolic Pathway of Fitusiran
L1270: whether the N-1 metabolite identified in the HPLC chromatograms was the AS(N-1)3' or AS(N-1)5' metabolite. However, based on in vitro metabolic stability studies and metabolite profiling of in vitro human serum and liver S9 fractions, the N-1 metabolite in human plasma was expected to be the AS(N-1)3' metabolite.
L1271: ## 14.1.2.3. Study 319N-1520: CYP450 Isoforms Involved in the Metabolism of Fitusiran
L1272: Study 319N-1520 evaluated the potential human CYP450 isoforms involved in the metabolism of ALN-AT3SC using cDNA-expressed enzymes. The objective of this study was to identify if any of the cytochrome P450 enzymes (CYP) are involved in the metabolism of fitusiran by using a panel of recombinant cytochrome P450 enzymes (rCYPs).
L1273: In this study, fitusiran was incubated in triplicate with individual rCYPs: rCYP1A2, rCYP2B6, rCYP2C8, rCYP2C9, rCYP2C19, rCYP2D6, rCYP3A4 and rCYP3A5. The reaction mixture consisted of a specific rCYP, 100mM potassium phosphate buffer (pH 7.4), 1mM NADPH, and fitusiran at 2 concentrations, 800 ng/mL and 8,000 ng/mL. Reactions with fitusiran were terminated at 0 and 45 minutes with ice-cold methanol (1:1 v/v) and with the positive controls at 30 minutes with ice-cold acetonitrile containing internal standard (1:1 v/v). Probe substrates for each of the rCYP enzymes were included in the study as positive controls. The negative control incubations consisted of fitusiran with rCYPs but without NADPH. Fitusiran was analyzed by liquid chromatography-time of flight-mass spectrometry (LC-TOF-MS). Positive control substrates were analyzed by ultra-high-performance liquid chromatography-mass spectrometry/mass spectrometry (UHPLC-MS/MS). Peak area ratios were used to calculate the percent parent drug remaining at 45 or 30 minutes.
L1274: The results showed that the percentage of fitusiran remaining after 45 minutes ranged from ~90% to ~100% for all rCYPs tested in the presence of NADPH. In contrast, positive control incubations showed significant metabolism as parent compound was depleted for rCYP2C9, rCYP2D6, and rCYP3A4/5, with 35.9% to 68.0% remaining for the other rCYPs tested.
L1275: These results indicated that fitusiran is not a substrate of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, CYP3A4, or CYP3A5.
L1276: ## 14.1.2.4. Study AT3-NCD13-014 (CYP Inhibition)
L1277: Study AT3-NCD13-014 evaluated the inhibitory potential of fitusiran against eight principal human CYP isozymes using human liver microsomes: CYP1A2, CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4/5.
L1278: In this study, CYP1A2, CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP2D6 activity was investigated using phenacetin, coumarin, bupropion, amodiaquine, tolbutamide, S-mephenytoin, and dextromethorphan as probe substrates, respectively. CYP3A4/5 activity was investigated using both midazolam and testosterone as probe substrates. The inhibitory activity was evaluated using pooled human liver microsomes and was measured at 10 fitusiran concentrations (0 to 10,000 µg/mL), in triplicate. Reaction was initiated by addition of NADPH and following incubation at 37°C (3 to 30 minutes for different probe substrates). The reaction was terminated by addition of acetonitrile. The metabolites of the probe substrates in pooled
L1279: samples (acetaminophen for CYP1A2, 7-hydroxycoumarin for CYP2A6, hydroxybupropion for CYP2B6, N-desethylamodiaquine for CYP2C8, 4-hydroxytolbutamide for CYP2C9, 4'-hydroxymephenytoin for CYP2C19, dextrorphan for CYP2D6, 6\beta-hydroxytestosterone and 1'-hydroxymidazolam for CYP3A4/5) were measured using electrospray ionization techniques.
L1280: ## **Results**
L1281: There were no changes in activity as compared to the vehicle control for CYP1A2, CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, or CYP3A4/5 (both midazolam and testosterone as substrates), indicating that fitusiran was not an inhibitor of any of these CYPs. Fitusiran showed inhibition of CYP2C8, with a calculated half maximal inhibitory concentration (IC_{50}) value for fitusiran of ~800 \mug/mL, indicating that fitusiran was not an inhibitor of CYP2C8 enzymes at clinically relevant concentrations (mean maximum plasma concentration at the highest recommended clinical dose of 50 mg is 84.1 ng/mL [LTE15174], which is ~9500-fold lower than the CYP2C8 IC_{50} value). The calculated IC_{50} values for positive controls were consistent with reported literature values.
L1282: To summarize, fitusiran is unlikely to inhibit any of these tested CYPs at clinically relevant concentrations.
L1283: ## **14.1.2.5. Study 319N-1517 (CYP Induction)**
L1284: Study 319N-1517 evaluated the potential of fitusiran to induce CYP1A2, CYP2B6 and CYP3A4 in sandwich cultured human hepatocytes.
L1285: Evaluation of the degree of CYP induction was performed by the following two methods: (i) measurement of mRNA levels by reverse transcription-polymerase chain reaction (TaqMan), and (ii) measurement of enzyme activities using isoform-specific probe substrates. Cultured human hepatocytes from three separate adult donors were cultured on collagen-coated plates and incubated with vehicle alone, positive and negative control compounds, or fitusiran for 48 or 72 hours (mRNA and enzyme activities, respectively). Fitusiran was evaluated at 4 concentrations: 0.005, 0.25, 2.5, and 25 \mug/mL. Activity levels of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, and CYP3A4 were measured in situ with a cocktail of phenacetin, bupropion, and midazolam using qualified liquid chromatography-tandem mass spectrometry (LC MS/MS) methods (details in 319N-1517). The criteria for positive CYP induction were increases in mRNA levels 2-fold above the vehicle control or enzyme activity \ge40% (or 0.4-fold) of the relevant positive control induction.
L1286: ## **Results**
L1287: Hepatocytes treated with fitusiran showed no statistically significant increase in CYP1A2, CYP2B6, or CYP3A4 mRNA levels at the concentrations tested, and no increased CYP1A2, CYP2B6, or CYP3A4 enzyme activity under the conditions utilized in this study. Based on these results, fitusiran appeared unlikely to be a CYP inducer at concentrations that were at or below 25 \mug/mL (mean maximum plasma concentration at the highest recommended clinical dose of 50 mg is 84.1 ng/mL [LTE15174], which is ~300-fold lower than the highest concentration of 25 \mug/mL tested in this study).
L1288: To summarize, fitusiran is unlikely to induce any of these tested CYPs at clinically relevant concentrations.
L1289: ## 14.1.2.6. Study Alnylam-03-22Jul2016: Transporter Inhibition and Substrate Evaluations
L1290: Study Alnylam-03-22Jul2016 evaluated whether fitusiran is a substrate and inhibitor of efflux and uptake transporters, through in vitro interaction studies of fitusiran with human BSEP, BCRP and MDR1 efflux (ABC) transporters, and with human MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 uptake transporters. The objective of this study was to evaluate potential interaction of fitusiran with 11 human efflux and uptake transporters. The transporters evaluated, assays performed, and fitusiran concentrations tested are summarized in Table 109.

L1298: In this study, vesicular transport inhibition and substrate assays were performed with inside-out membrane vesicles prepared from cells overexpressing human ATP-binding cassette (ABC) transporters in human embryonic kidney cell line 293 (HEK293). Uptake transporter inhibition and substrate assays were performed using Madin Darby canine kidney cells II (MDCKII) or HEK293 cells stably expressing the respective uptake transporters.
L1299: ## **Results**
L1300: ## **Vesicular Transport Inhibition Assays**
L1301: Fitusiran did not influence the BCRP-, BSEP-, and multidrug resistance protein 1 (MDR-1)-mediated probe substrate accumulation at the concentrations evaluated.
L1302: ## Vesicular Transport Substrate Assays
L1303: ATP-dependent accumulation of fitusiran was similar in the presence of ATP and AMP, indicating no active accumulation of fitusiran in transporter (BCRP, BSEP and MDR1) expressing or control vesicles.
L1304: ## Uptake Transporter Inhibition Assays
L1305: Fitusiran inhibited the organic cation transporter (OCT)2-mediated metformin transport by 24% at 487 µg/mL. Fitusiran did not influence the multidrug and toxin extrusion transporter (MATE)1-, MATE2-K-, organic anion-transporting polypeptide (OATP)1B1-, OATP1B3-, organic anion transporter (OAT)1-, OAT3- and OCT1-mediated probe substrate accumulation at the applied concentrations.
L1306: ## Uptake Transporter Substrate Assays
L1307: Accumulation of fitusiran was similar in the OAT1-, OAT3- and OCT2-expressing and the control cells, indicating no active accumulation of fitusiran. Transporter-specific accumulation of fitusiran was observed in cells expressing OATP1B1 (2.45-fold at 487 µg/mL), OATP1B3 (2.51-fold at 487 µg/mL), MATE1 (2.24-fold at 48.7 µg/mL) and MATE2-K (8.64-fold at 48.7 µg/mL, 2.39-fold at 4.87 µg/mL, and 0.81-fold at 0.487 µg/mL). These results indicated that fitusiran was likely a substrate of MATE2-K uptake transporters at high concentrations, but probably not at clinically relevant concentrations (no accumulation observed at 0.487 µg/mL [487 ng/mL], which is 5.79-fold higher than the mean maximum plasma concentration of 84.1 ng/mL observed at the highest recommended clinical dose of 50 mg [LTE15174]).
L1308: To summarize, fitusiran is unlikely to be a substrate or inhibitor of the tested efflux and uptake transporters at clinically relevant concentrations.
L1309: ## 14.2. In Vivo Studies
L1310: ## 14.2.1. Single Ascending Dose and Multiple Ascending Dose PK Study in Healthy Subjects
L1311: ## 14.2.1.1. Phase 1 Study TDR14767
L1312: Study TDR14767 was a phase 1 single ascending dose and multiple ascending dose safety, tolerability, and pharmacokinetics study of subcutaneously administered fitusiran in healthy adult volunteers and hemophilia A or B subjects (moderate or severe hemophilia). The primary objective was to evaluate the safety and tolerability of fitusiran when administered to healthy adult male subjects and when administered to male subjects with moderate or severe hemophilia A or B.
L1313: ## **Study Design**
L1314: This study consisted of 4 parts: Part A, a single-ascending-dose phase in healthy subjects; Part B, a multiple-ascending-dose phase in subjects with moderate or severe hemophilia A or B; Part C, an exploratory multiple-dose phase in male subjects with moderate or severe hemophilia A or B; and Part D, a multiple-dose phase in subjects with moderate or severe hemophilia A or B with inhibitors.
L1315: The doses were: Part A: 0.03 mg/kg (single dose); Part B: 0.015, 0.045, or 0.075 mg/kg once weekly (QW) (total of 3 doses); Part C: 0.225, 0.45, 0.9, or 1.8 mg/kg, or 80 mg QM (total of 3 doses); Part D: 50 or 80 mg QM (total of 3 doses). A total of 46 subjects were enrolled (45 treated with fitusiran, 1 placebo).
L1316: Intensive PK sampling was performed in Part A (healthy subjects) following a single dose of fitusiran, in Part B (subjects with hemophilia A or B) after the first (Day 0) and third QW (Day 14) dose of fitusiran, and in Parts C and D (subjects with hemophilia A or B) after the first (Day 0) and third QM (Day 56) dose of fitusiran. PK sampling timepoints were: predose, then 0.083, 0.167, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, 48, 72, and 168 hours postdose for 0.015- to 0.075-mg/kg dose cohorts; and predose, then 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, and 24 hours postdose for 0.225-mg/kg to 1.8-mg/kg and 50- and 80-mg dose cohorts. Urine samples for PK analysis were collected from 0 to 6, 6 to 12, and 12 to 24 hours after the first and last doses.
L1317: Samples for AT and thrombin generation (TG) measurements were collected at the following timepoints: Part A (single dose): at screening, on Day (D)0 (predose/baseline), then D1, D2, D3, D7, D10, D14, D21, D28, D42, and D56; Part B (3×QW doses): at screening, on D0 (predose/baseline), then D1, D2, D3, D7 (predose), D8, D9, D14 (predose), and D15; Parts C and D (3×QM doses): at screening, on D0 (predose/baseline), then D1, D7, D14, D28 (predose), D29, D42, D56 (predose), D57, D70, D84, D98, and D112. All samples for ADA assessment were collected at predose/trough time points on D0 (Part A), D0, D7, and D14 (Part B), and D0, D28, and D57 (Parts C and D).

L1337: Overall, the PK data indicate that following SC administration to subjects with hemophilia, fitusiran exposures (Cmax, AUC) increased with dose on both PK assessment days 0 and 56 (after first and third dose); plasma half-lives were similar on Day 0 (~3 to 5 hours) and Day 56 (~2 to 6 hours). Because plasma half-life was very short relative to the dosing interval (QW or QM), no significant accumulation was expected, and the data showed no fitusiran accumulation with monthly dosing.
L1338: Dose proportionality evaluations indicated that fitusiran exposure increased approximately dose proportionality on Day 0 and Day 56. Representative examples of fitusiran concentration-time profiles are shown for 50 and 80 mg QM doses (Part D) in Figure 27 (Day 0) and Figure 28 (Day 56).

L1343: ## Urine Pharmacokinetics
L1344: Urinary excretion could not be determined in many subjects due to missing urine volumes. Among the 3 subjects with urine excretion data in Part C (Day 56) who received 1.8 mg/kg (n=1) or 80 mg (n=2), fraction excreted (fe, presented as % of administered dose) was similar across the 3 subjects (9.8% to 14.0%). In Part D, geometric mean fe (N=9) was 17.0% on Day 56, with a CV of 54%.
L1345: ## Pharmacodynamics (Plasma AT and TG)
L1346: Plasma AT-time profiles of mean AT activity after fitusiran administration, normalized to baseline AT levels, for Parts A, B and C are presented in Figure 29, and for Part D (subjects with inhibitors) in Figure 30.
L1347: ## NDA 219019
## QFITLIA (fitusiran)
L1348: Fitusiran treatment resulted in a dose-dependent decrease in plasma AT. AT reduction was measurable at 7 days postdose, reaching a maximum after ~28 days, and was sustained between monthly doses at the dose levels tested. The higher monthly doses evaluated (1.8 mg/kg QM and 50 mg and 80 mg QM in Parts C and D) resulted in &gt;80% AT reduction.

L1357: Time taken for recovery of AT activity after the last (third) dose was generally dose-dependent. For example, based on a visual evaluation of Part C data (Figure 10), the time taken for mean AT activity from the 0.225-mg/kg dose group to return to ~50% of starting levels is ~28 days (from Day 84 [trough activity for third dose] to ~Day 112), whereas time taken for mean AT activity from the 80-mg dose group to return to ~50% of starting levels is ~98 days (from Day 84 [trough activity for third dose] to ~Day 182).

L1369: ## Immunogenicity
L1370: No subject developed treatment-induced ADA. Among the treated subjects (n=46), 1 subject in Part B (0.015-mg/kg dose group) had positive ADA results both at baseline and throughout the study. ADA titers in this subject were: predose =400, Day 7=200, and Day 70=400. The PK and pharmacodynamic (PD) parameters for this subject were within the variability observed in other dose groups.
L1371: ## 14.2.2. PK/PD Studies in Patients (Five Studies)
L1372: ## 14.2.2.1. Phase 1/2 Study LTE14762
L1373: Study LTE14762 was a phase 1/2 long term extension study (up to 7 years) in adult subjects with hemophilia who had completed the phase 1 study TDR14767. The primary objective was to evaluate the long-term safety and tolerability of fitusiran in male subjects with moderate or severe hemophilia A or B.
L1374: ## Study Design
L1375: This study was an open-label extension study. As mentioned above, Study TDR14767 was conducted in 4 parts. Subjects from Part B rolled over into the LTE1462 study after completing Day 70 in the parent study. Subjects from Parts C and D were expected to roll over into the LTE14762 study approximately 1 month after completing the 3 monthly doses of study drug in the parent study, thereby maintaining their monthly dosing schedule.
L1376: Therefore, the fitusiran doses evaluated in this study were 50 mg and 80 mg SC, which were the doses evaluated in the parent study TDR14767. Doses in this study were initially administered in terms of fitusiran sodium salt, but later as fitusiran free acid. Dosing frequency during the period that PK samples were collected (up to 2 years from enrollment) was QM. A dosing pause period for the original dose and regimen was implemented on October 30, 2020, due to thrombotic events observed in other clinical studies within the fitusiran clinical program. Later, a 50 mg Q2M regimen was introduced as part of a risk mitigation strategy against potential thrombotic events since November 25, 2020.
L1377: Intensive plasma PK sampling was performed on Day 1, Day 361 (Month 12), and Day 721 (Month 24). The sampling timepoints were: predose, then 0.5, 2, 4, 8, and 24 hours postdose. Urine samples (0 to 6, 6 to 12, and 12 to 24 hours) were collected on Day 721.
L1378: Samples for AT activity and TG measurements were collected at the following timepoints: (i) subjects who remained on original dose regimen: D1, then monthly up to Month (M) 24, then M30, M36, M42 and M48; (ii) subjects who changed to modified dose regimen: D1 (day of dose restart), then monthly from post-restart (PR) M1 to PRM12, then PRM16, PRM20, PRM24, PRM28, PR M32, and PRM36. All AT and TG samples were collected predose.
L1379: Samples for ADA evaluation were collected on D1, M1, M2, M3, M6, M9, M12, M15, M18, M21, M24, M36, M48, M60, M72, and M84 for the original dose regimens, and PRD1, PRM6, PRM12, PRM24, and PRM36 for the post-restart dose regimens. All ADA samples were collected predose.
L1380: ## **Results**

L1405: ## Pharmacodynamics
L1406: Monthly dosing with fitusiran (original dose and regimen, PD Set 1) resulted in a change in AT activity to 14.4% of baseline starting at Month 1 and persisting over several months. In the revised AT-DR regimen (PD Set 2), the change in AT activity was 23.3% of baseline. There were 3 subjects who discontinued the study because their AT levels were &lt;15%. The rest of the AT activity levels were maintained between 15 to 35%.
L1407: Consistent with fitusiran's mechanism of action, TG increased with AT lowering, reaching a peak mean of 74.42nM by endpoint evaluation that remained elevated during the course of fitusiran treatment for the overall subjects in PD Set 1. In PD Set 2, TG reached a mean of 88.08nM.
L1408: ## Immunogenicity
L1409: Two subjects developed treatment-induced ADA; one was positive at one timepoint (Month 10) with a titer of 200, and the other was positive at Month 48, Month 60, and Month 72, with a titer of 400 at Month 48 and 100 (equivalent to minimum required dilution [MRD] of the assay) at the other timepoints.
L1410: ## 14.2.2.2. Phase 3 Pivotal Study EFC14768
L1411: Study EFC14768 was a phase 3 study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of fitusiran in subjects with hemophilia A or B, with inhibitory antibodies to Factor VIII or IX. The primary objective was to evaluate the efficacy of fitusiran compared to on-demand treatment with BPAs, as determined by the frequency of bleeding episodes.
L1412: ## Study Design
L1413: Sixty subjects were enrolled in this study, 41 on fitusiran and 19 on bypassing agents (BPA) on demand). PK and PD samples were only collected from the fitusiran-treated subjects.
L1414: The fitusiran SC dose evaluated in this study was 80 mg QM, except in 3 subjects from mainland China who received a single first dose of 50 mg, before being switched to 80 mg QM.
L1415: Fitusiran treatment duration was 9 months.
L1416: Intensive PK sampling (predose, then 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, and 24 hours postdose) was performed after the first fitusiran dose in East Asian subjects, whilst sparse PK samples (4 hours postdose and trough) were collected from all subjects at visits specified in the study protocol. Urine samples for PK analysis were collected at 0 to 6, 6 to 12, and 12 to 24 hours after the first fitusiran dose.
L1417: Samples for AT activity and TG measurements were collected at screening, then D1 (baseline), D2 (East Asian only), D8 (East Asian only), D15, D29 (M1), D43, D57 (M2), then monthly up to M9. All AT and TG samples, except D2, D8, and D15, were collected predose. Sampling timepoints for ADA analysis in phase 3 studies are summarized in Section 14.4.1
L1418: ## Results

L1442: ## Pharmacodynamics
L1443: Monthly dosing with fitusiran resulted in a sustained reduction of AT activity, reaching 15.9% of baseline by Day 29. Consistent with fitusiran's mechanism of action, TG increased with AT reduction, reaching a peak mean of 46.7nM by Day 43 (540% change from baseline) that remained elevated during the course of fitusiran treatment, thus indicating persistent and sustained improvement of coagulation potential.
L1444: ## Immunogenicity
L1445: One subject developed treatment-induced ADA at one timepoint, with a titer of 50, equivalent to minimum required dilution (MRD) of the assay, indicating that fitusiran has low potential to elicit an immunogenic response.
L1446: ## 14.2.2.3. Phase 3 Pivotal Study EFC14769
L1447: Study EFC14769 was a phase 3 study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of fitusiran in subjects with hemophilia A or B, without inhibitory antibodies to Factor VIII or IX. The primary objective was to evaluate the efficacy of fitusiran compared to on-demand treatment with factor concentrates, as determined by the frequency of bleeding episodes.
L1448: ## Study Design
L1449: In this study, 120 subjects were enrolled, with 79 on fitusiran and 40 on factor (on demand). PK and PD samples were only collected from the fitusiran-treated subjects. The fitusiran dose
L1450: ## NDA 219019
## QFITLIA (fitusiran)
L1451: evaluated in this study was 80 mg (free acid) QM, and fitusiran treatment duration was 9 months. Intensive PK sampling was performed after the first fitusiran dose in East Asian subjects, while sparse PK samples (4 h postdose and trough) were collected from all subjects. PK, PD (AT and TG) and ADA timepoints were the same as in Study EFC14768.

L1475: ## Pharmacodynamics
L1476: Monthly dosing with fitusiran resulted in a sustained reduction of AT activity levels, reaching 17.9% of the baseline by Day 29. Consistent with fitusiran's mechanism of action, TG increased with AT reduction, reaching a peak mean of 40.7nM by Day 225 (703.36% change from baseline) that remained elevated during the course of fitusiran treatment, thus indicating improvement of persistent and sustained TG potential.
L1477: ## Immunogenicity
L1478: Four subjects from the fitusiran prophylaxis group developed treatment-induced ADA at one or more timepoints. Three subjects from the factor on-demand arm were also positive at one or more timepoints. ADA titer in each case was 50 (equivalent to MRD of the assay).
L1479: ## 14.2.2.4. Phase 3 Pivotal Study EFC15110
L1480: Study EFC15110 was an open-label, multinational switching study to describe the efficacy and safety of fitusiran prophylaxis in subjects with hemophilia A and B previously receiving factor or bypassing agent prophylaxis. The primary objective was to characterize the frequency of bleeding episodes while receiving fitusiran treatment, relative to the frequency of bleeding episodes while receiving factor concentrate or bypassing agent (BPA) prophylaxis.
L1481: ## Study Design
L1482: PK and PD samples were only collected during the fitusiran treatment phase of the study.
L1483: Fitusiran doses evaluated in this study were 80 mg QM and 50 mg Q2M (administered in terms of free acid), and fitusiran treatment duration was 7 months. Intensive PK sampling was performed after the first fitusiran dose in East Asian subjects. Sparse PK samples (4 hours postdose and trough) were collected from all subjects. Since the change in dose regimen from 80 mg QM to 50 mg Q2M, introduced as part of a risk mitigation strategy against potential thrombotic events, was only made towards the end of the study when the majority of subjects had already completed their 7-month fitusiran treatment period, all the PK data from this study are with the 80 mg QM dose.
L1484: Sample collection timepoints for intensive and sparse plasma PK and urine PK analyses from East Asian subjects after the first fitusiran dose were the same as in Study EFC14768. Samples for AT activity and TG measurements were collected at screening, then D1 (baseline), D2 (East Asian only), D8 (East Asian only), D15, D29 (M1), D43, D57 (M2), then monthly up to M7. All AT and TG samples, except D2, D8, and D15, were collected predose. Sampling timepoints for ADA analysis in phase 3 studies are summarized in Section 14.4.1.
L1485: ## Results
L1486: ## Pharmacokinetics
L1487: A summary of single-dose PK parameters is presented in Table 118. The data indicated that, following fitusiran administration at 80 mg QM, mean plasma PK parameters and recoveries of fitusiran in 0 to 24 h urine were similar in East Asian subjects with and without inhibitors.

L1506: ## Pharmacodynamics
L1507: Monthly dosing with fitusiran resulted in a sustained reduction of AT levels, reaching 16.6% of baseline by Day 29 that persisted over several months. The lowest AT level reached was 9.4% of baseline at Day 169, with 12.7% for Cohort A (inhibitor) and 7.8% for Cohort B (noninhibitor), respectively.
L1508: Consistent with fitusiran's mechanism of action, TG increased with AT reduction, reaching a peak mean of 62.6nM (472% change from baseline) by endpoint evaluation (defined as the last nonmissing postbaseline assessment on or before EOS/ET visit for fitusiran 80 mg prophylaxis period) that remained elevated during the course of fitusiran treatment for the overall subjects. The peak TG levels were 54.1nM (564% change from baseline) for subjects in Cohort A (inhibitor) and 66.5nM (429% change from baseline) for the subjects in Cohort B (noninhibitor), respectively.
L1509: ## Immunogenicity
L1510: Two subjects had treatment-induced ADA at one timepoint each. The ADA titer in each case was 50 (equivalent to the MRD of the assay).
L1511: ## 14.2.2.5. Phase 3 Pivotal Study LTE15174
L1512: Study LTE15174 was an open-label long-term safety and efficacy study of fitusiran in subjects with hemophilia A or B, with or without inhibitory antibodies to factor VIII or IX. This study used the AT-DR. The primary objective was to characterize the long-term safety and tolerability of fitusiran. The primary endpoints of this study were the incidence, severity, relatedness, and seriousness of adverse events (AEs), and laboratory assessments.
L1513: ## Study Design
L1514: This study enabled subjects who had completed any of the phase 3 studies of fitusiran to continue to be evaluated for long-term safety and efficacy. It was initially planned as a long-term extension study for subjects from phase 3 studies EFC14768, EFC14769, and EFC15110 at the same dose regimen (80 mg QM) as the parent studies. The majority of subjects from the parent studies had already rolled over into LTE15174 when dosing was temporarily paused to evaluate a revised AT-DR. Unlike the dose regimen in the parent studies, which targeted AT reduction of \ge 85\% in the majority of subjects, the postpause strategy targeted AT activity values between 15% to 35% with fitusiran treatment.
L1515: The final dose regimens evaluated in LTE15174 were: 20 mg Q2M, 20 mg QM, 50 mg Q2M, 50 mg QM, and 80 mg QM. All doses were administered in terms of fitusiran free acid. Following the dosing pause, all subjects except 11, who had AT activity of 15 to 35% at 80 mg QM regimen, were restarted on fitusiran treatment at 50 mg Q2M. Of these 11 subjects, 4 later required a dose reduction, but the other 7 remained on the 80 mg QM dose regimen. Subjects who had AT values &gt;35\% after 2 to 3 doses at 50 mg Q2M, were dose escalated to 50 mg QM (and if needed, to 80 mg QM); if they had AT values &lt;15\%, they were dose de-escalated to 20 mg Q2M (and if needed, escalated to 20 mg QM from 20 mg Q2M), as appropriate, based on the criteria shown in Figure 5 in section 6.1.3. Subjects who had AT activity &lt;15\% at 20 mg Q2M were discontinued from the study.
L1516: Fitusiran dosing in all previous clinical studies was conducted using a glass vial and syringe device, and the same device was used to deliver the different doses (20 mg, 50 mg, 80 mg) in this study. In addition, a 50 mg fitusiran prefilled pen (PFP) device was also evaluated in this study.
L1517: Intensive PK sampling was performed in the following 3 subgroups of subjects: (a) after the first 50-mg dose in East Asians (at least 12 each from mainland China [mainly de novo subjects] and South Korea, and all subjects [who consented to intensive PK sampling] from Japan and Taiwan), and Non-East Asians (at least 12 subjects from rest of the world [ROW]); (b) after the first 20-mg dose to at least 6 East Asian subjects (from any East Asian country or region [mainland China, Japan, South Korea, Taiwan]), and at least 6 Non-East Asian subjects (from ROW), and (c) 12 subjects (any region of the world) for PK comparison between 50-mg dose administered first using a glass vial and syringe device (all 12 subjects), and at the next dose (Q2M or QM), using a PFP device (all 12 subjects). Eleven (11) subjects completed intensive sampling following administration by both devices. Intensive PK samples were not collected from any of the subjects who remained on the 80 mg QM dose in this study. Sparse PK samples (at 4 hours postdose and trough) were collected from all subjects. Urine PK samples were collected from 7 subjects in the 50 mg Q2M group only.
L1518: Sample collection timepoints for intensive and sparse plasma PK and urine PK analyses were the same as for the other phase 3 studies. Details of the AT and TG sampling times for subjects under different dose regimens are provided in the schedule of assessments of LTE15174 protocol, but in general they were: (a) D1 (baseline), D15, D29 (M1), M2, M3, then every 3 months up to M24, then every 6 months up to M48, for subjects who remained on original dose regimen; and (b) PRD1 (day of dose restart), then monthly from PRM1 to PRM12, then every 4 months up to PRM36, then PRM42 and PRM48, for subjects who changed to AT-DR. All AT and TG samples, except Day 15, were collected predose. Sampling timepoints for ADA analysis in phase 3 studies are summarized in Section 14.4.1.
L1519: ## Results
L1520: ## **Pharmacokinetics**
L1521: A summary of PK parameters following fitusiran administration at 20 mg Q2M and 50 mg Q2M is presented in Table 121. Increases in systemic exposure with dose were approximately dose-proportional, with a 2.4- to 2.6-fold increase in mean C_{max} and AUCs for the 2.5-fold increase in dose from 20 mg to 50 mg, respectively.

L1548: ## Pharmacodynamics
L1549: The fitusiran revised AT-DR was successful at maintaining AT levels within the targeted AT range. Most subjects were able to achieve AT levels between 15 to 35% with the revised AT-DR. Overall, 200 of 213 rolled-over subjects (93%) required no or 1 dose change to maintain AT activity between 15 to 35%; 167 (78%) subjects were on Q2M regimens. None of the subjects who initiated dosing at 50 mg Q2M needed a dose higher than 50 mg QM to achieve AT activity in the target 15 to 35% range. Of subjects at the lowest dose evaluated (20 mg Q2M), 11.7% repeatedly had AT &lt;15% and thus required fitusiran discontinuation.
L1550: Under the revised AT-DR, dosing with fitusiran resulted in a sustained lowering of AT activity levels. The mean (SD) AT level was 23.5% (4.64%) during the Primary Efficacy Period. The results showed that the majority of subjects were within the target AT range of 15 to 35% at steady-state and remained within the range for the duration of the study under the revised AT-DR, regardless of the actual fitusiran dose. Following discontinuation of fitusiran treatment, fitusiran effects (reduced AT activity) persisted over several months. In the majority of subjects AT had recovered to &gt;60% in 6 months.
L1551: ## Immunogenicity
L1552: Overall, 4 out of 5 confirmed ADA-positive subjects had treatment-induced ADA, which were generally transient. One subject was ADA-positive only at study entry and not at subsequent timepoints. One subject had persistent treatment-induced ADA with a peak ADA titer of 100 and last titer of 50.

L1609: ## ADA Effect on Efficacy and Safety of Fitusiran
L1610: Given the complexity of hemophilia, no evident impact on annualized bleeding rate (ABR) was associated with ADA as observed among the subjects who developed treatment-emergent ADA and the ABR of the overall study populations.
L1611: No new safety findings were identified in subjects who had confirmed positive ADA during the studies. The observed treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) were consistent with those expected of the underlying disease and the known safety profile of fitusiran.
L1612: ## Summary of Immunogenicity Assessment
L1613: The clinical findings support that fitusiran has a low immunogenicity risk relative to ADA impact on clinical outcomes. Subjects who developed ADA had a low titer response that did not meaningfully influence PK, PD (AT activity), efficacy as assessed by ABR, or safety parameters when compared to subjects who did not develop ADA.
L1614: ## 14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
L1615: ## 14.5.1. Applicant's Population Kinetic-Pharmacodynamic Model Analysis
L1616: Upon single or multiple dose administration of fitusiran in nonhuman primates (NHP) and in man, plasma concentrations decline rapidly and are below the lower limit of quantitation in 1 to 2 days postdose, while suppression of the target protein, antithrombin (AT), is sustained for days,
L1617: highlighting the disconnect between plasma concentrations and PD effect. This lack of correlation is to be expected based on the mechanism of action of fitusiran, i.e., (i) the GalNAc-mediated targeted delivery of the double-stranded small interfering ribonucleic acid (siRNA)-conjugate to the liver following subcutaneous administration; (ii) uptake of the siRNA into hepatocytes; and (iii) binding of the antisense strand of the siRNA to the RNA-induced silencing complex (RISC), causing specific degradation of the AT mRNA in the hepatocytes, the primary source of AT production. The time taken for this process to result in measurable decreases in plasma AT levels, including the time for the elimination of preexisting AT, explains the duration of onset of pharmacological effect of fitusiran following administration. Further, since a single RISC-bound antisense strand in the hepatocyte can cause the catalytic degradation of multiple target AT mRNAs, the duration of the pharmacology of fitusiran is significantly longer than what might be estimated based on plasma PK. Based on the biology and mechanism of action, it is clear that liver concentrations of fitusiran are linked to the gradual decrease in serum AT and the ensuing long duration of AT suppression.
L1618: Since liver concentrations are not practical to measure in man and were not measured in monkeys at pharmacologic doses of fitusiran, the Applicant used allometric scaling to predict liver concentrations of fitusiran in both NHPs and humans. This is based on other siRNA molecules (where liver biopsies in NHP were collected) with similar physicochemical characteristics to fitusiran, for which observed dose-proportional liver concentrations scale allometrically in rats and monkeys. It was noted that the liver PK of siRNA molecules scale allometrically between species with a clearance exponent of 0.75 and a volume exponent of 1.0. The overall modeling strategy was executed in a stepwise manner as follows: (1) fitusiran liver PK parameters, which were estimated based on fitusiran liver concentrations in rats, were allometrically scaled to humans; (2) fitusiran concentrations in the RISC complex (fitusiran-RISC) were estimated based on scaled liver PK parameters and observed PD; (3) fitusiran-RISC concentrations were linked to AT lowering, with an inhibitory effect on synthesis rate of AT activity. A schematic of the modeling strategy is shown in Figure 35.
L1619: **Figure 35. Schematic Representation of PopK-PD Modeling Strategy**
L1620: Source: study-poh1133, Figure 6
Abbreviations: AT, antithrombin; K_{deg}, degradation rate of AT; K_{syn}, synthesis rate of AT; PK, pharmacokinetics; RISC, RNA-induced silencing complex
L1621: The fitusiran population kinetic-pharmacodynamic (PopK-PD) model (POH1133) was first developed based on plasma AT activity data from 41 hemophilia A or B subjects enrolled in Study ALN-AT3SC-001 (Parts B, C and D) and ALN-AT3-002. AT activity was measured
L1622: biweekly for Study ALN-AT3SC-001, and monthly for Study ALN-AT3SC-002. Fitusiran liver concentrations in rats were allometrically scaled to predict human liver PK. Fitusiran concentrations in the RISC complex (fitusiran-RISC) were predicted based on total liver PK and observed PD, where fitusiran-RISC concentrations had an inhibitory effect on synthesis rate of AT activity. Simulations of steady-state residual AT activity (at 1 month after the fifth dose) in 1000 virtual hemophilia patients receiving monthly doses of fitusiran over a dose range of 1 to 140 mg were conducted to guide the dose selection for pivotal trials. As shown in Figure 36, a sharp decline in mean steady-state AT lowering is predicted as fitusiran dose increases from 1 mg to 20 mg. A further increase in dose from 20 mg to 50 mg shows a less rapid decline in AT activity. At doses greater than 80 mg, the dose-response curve reaches an asymptote, suggesting that any increase in dose beyond 80 mg will cause minimal further decline in AT activity. In addition, less variability in AT lowering is predicted at 80 mg (95th to 5th percentile of 77% to 92%) compared to 50 mg (95th to 5th percentile of 72% to 90%) (Table 130). The 80 mg QM dosing regimen was selected for later stage clinical trials based on these simulation results as well as the observed safety, efficacy, and pharmacodynamic profile in trial TDR14767.
L1623: **Figure 36. Mean (90% Confidence Interval) Dose-AT Lowering Relationship for Fitusiran in Virtual Hemophilia Patients Across a Dose Range of 1 mg to 140 mg Administered Monthly**
L1624: Solid blue line= Mean AT Lowering response, shaded area=90% confidence interval
L1625: Source: study-poh1133, Figure 20
Abbreviation: AT, antithrombin
L1626: **Table 130. Model-Simulated AT Reduction and Variability by Dose, Study POH1133**
L1627: Dose (mg)	95th Percentile	Median	5th Percentile	Mean (90% CI)
50	71.6	82.5	89.8	82.4 (79.7-85)
80	77.3	86.6	92.3	86.8 (84.7-88.8)

L1628: Source: study-poh1133, Table 6
L1629: Abbreviations: AT, antithrombin; CI, confidence interval
L1630: In October 2020, when the phase 1/2 (LTE14762), phase 3 (EFC14768, EFC14769, EFC15110), and phase 3 extension (LTE15174) studies were ongoing, concerns regarding thrombotic events potentially related to fitusiran resulted in placing the ongoing clinical studies on a voluntary pause by the Sponsor to evaluate risk factors. All available clinical data were analyzed to assess the potential role of AT reduction and the associated risk of thrombotic events, as well as bleeds. An optimized dose regimen targeting AT activity levels between 15% and 35% was implemented. The rationale for the selection of these targets was based on review of thrombotic event incidence as well as bleeding data at different AT activity levels. The clinical data, along with PopK-PD modeling, was used to assess doses and dose regimens that would achieve and maintain AT activity between 15% and 35%. The PopK-PD model (POH0721) was updated based on data from 45 healthy volunteers and hemophilia A or B subjects enrolled in Study ALN-AT3SC-001 and ALN-AT3SC-002. This dataset included 3 healthy volunteers, 34 subjects with hemophilia A, and 8 subjects with hemophilia B. Amongst the hemophilia subjects, 25 subjects were without inhibitors and 17 subjects were with inhibitors. The updated model structure was identical to the first PopK-PD model. AT activity data until week 24 from phase 3 trials (EFC14768, EFC14769, and EFC15110) were applied to validate the model. Simulation analysis was performed to guide the dose regimen selection which could achieve target AT activity range of 15% to 35%.
L1631: The simulation results showed that with a starting dose of 80 mg QM, the minimum AT activity in virtual subjects could go down to 6.9%, where for the 50 mg Q2M regimen, the minimum AT activity would remain above 10% (Table 131 and Figure 37). Therefore, based on the simulation results, the starting dose was revised to 50 mg Q2M (instead of 80 mg QM), with an option to sequentially escalate to 50 mg QM and 80 mg QM if AT activity was &gt;35%. This revised dose regimen was implemented in December 2020 across studies (except EFC14768 and EFC14769, which were nearly complete at the time of the voluntary dosing pause).

L1658: The model structure is shown in Figure 38. Based on the mechanism of action of fitusiran, the liver pharmacokinetic parameters (i.e., absorption rate [Ka], clearance from liver central compartment [CL], liver intercompartmental clearance [CL2], liver central volume [V2] and liver peripheral volume [V3]) were predicted by allometric scaling from rat liver PK and were fixed in the current PopK-PD model. The minimum uptake into RISC compartment (minQ) was fixed to 0.0225 g/h and the volume of RISC compartment (RV), that is V2+V3, was fixed to be 9375 g. The bioavailability was set to 1 and the turnover rate of fitusiran from peripheral liver compartment into RISC compartment Q was assumed to be dose-dependent and defined as follows:
L1659: ## **Equation 1. Turnover Rate of Fitusiran from Peripheral Liver Compartment into RISC Compartment Q**
L1660: Q = \min Q \times (1 - \text{POP}) + \text{POP} \times Q^{\max} \times \left(\frac{\text{Dose}}{50}\right)^{\theta_Q}
L1661: Q^{\max} is the maximum turnover rate of fitusiran from the peripheral liver compartment and \theta_Q is the hill coefficient correlating the impact of dose on the turnover rate of fitusiran from the peripheral compartment. Q^{\max} and \theta_Q were estimated. POP is a conditional variable where POP=1 if the subject is de-escalated to the 20 mg Q2M dose and POP=0 otherwise. The dynamics of AT activity were described by an indirect response model linking the predicted human liver concentrations to suppression of synthesis rate of AT through an effect compartment representing fitusiran loaded onto RISC. The key parameters for AT dynamics such as CL_R (clearance from RISC compartment), K_{out} (AT elimination rate), K_{in} (AT production rate), I_{max} (maximum inhibition of AT production), and IC_{50} (RISC concentration for 50% of maximum effect) were estimated.
L1662: Figure 38. Schematic Representation of PopK-PD Model for AT Lowering
L1663: **Abbreviations:** C_{RISC}, RNA-induced silencing complex (RISC) concentration of active small interfering RNA; CL, clearance; CL_R, clearance from RISC compartment; I_{C50}, concentration required to produce half-maximal effect of fitusiran; I_{max}, maximum inhibitory effect of fitusiran on K_{in}; K_a, uptake rate constant to liver; K_{in}, zero-order synthesis rate of AT; K_{out}, first-order degradation rate constant for AT; Q, intercompartmental clearance; Q, turnover rate of fitusiran from peripheral liver compartment into RISC; V_2, volume of distribution of central liver compartment; V_3, volume of distribution of peripheral liver compartment
L1664: Source: study-poh1116, Figure 2
L1665: **Table 136** summarizes all covariates that were investigated during the development of the PopK-PD final model. The covariate model development started with a detailed exploratory covariate analysis that aimed to identify: (1) covariate distribution; (2) correlation between covariates; and (3) potential relationship between covariates and PopK-PD model parameters. The result of the exploratory covariate analysis was used as input to formally screen statistically significant covariates in the PopK-PD model. The formal covariate analysis was performed using a stepwise selection procedure. This procedure involves stepwise testing of linear and nonlinear relationships in a forward inclusion (\Delta OFV of 6.63, p&lt;0.01 for 1 degree of freedom [DF]) and backwards exclusion (\Delta OFV of 10.8, p&lt;0.001 for 1 DF) procedure. The covariate analysis identified statistically significant effects of bilirubin on RISC clearance and RACE on K_{out}, with AT protein being eliminated faster in Asians (i.e., Japan, Korea, China, Taiwan, India, and Malaysia) than the remaining category of race considered in the analysis dataset, i.e., Black, Caucasian, and “other” races (i.e., American Indian or Alaska Native, Native Hawaiian or other Pacific Islander, other multiple races, and not reported races).

L1699: Overall, the Applicant's revised PopK-PD modeling and simulation analyses appear to be reasonable. Approximately 6% of subjects were predicted to discontinue treatment due to AT &lt;15% with 20 mg Q2M, which was lower than the observed 11.7% (Table 138). This inconsistency between model prediction and observation might be due to the Applicant's simulation approach, which did not consider the carryover effect due to the sustained AT activity inhibition from (1) the 80 mg QM regimen before the voluntary dosing pause; (2) AT-guided dose titration in the trial after the voluntary dosing pause (e.g., 50 mg Q2M de-escalate to 20 mg Q2M). To better evaluate the proposed dosing regimen and AT monitoring program, the reviewer performed independent PopK-PD model simulation analyses. The reviewer's simulation matched with the observed individual AT profile relatively well (Figure 43 and Figure 44).

L1704: ## 14.5.2. Evaluation for the Target AT Activity Range of 15% to 35%
L1705: Observed ABR records from 242 subjects in Studies EFC14768 (ITT), EFC14769 (ITT), and EFC15110 with fixed 80 mg QM were used to evaluate the correlation between AT activity and the efficacy endpoint ABR. Observations with the AT-based dosing regimen were not incorporated due to potential confounding by dose titration. The onset period for the 3 trials was the first 28 days. The efficacy period was Day 29 onwards until Month 9 for EFC14769, until Day 246 for EFC14769, and until Month 6 for EFC15110. Due to the limited duration of the onset period, a high number of zero-ABR values was observed. The average AT during the onset and efficacy periods was used to correlate with the observed ABR in the respective periods. The association plots, both with (Figure 45) and without (Figure 46) zero-ABR records, indicated that lower AT activity was potentially associated with lower ABR. However, it is unclear at which level of AT activity the ABR lowering plateaued, as the data did not provide sufficient information. Furthermore, the variability in follow-up duration among trials and between the onset and efficacy periods may have confounded the results.
L1706: Figure 45. Observed ABR Over Averaged AT Activity (Zero ABR Included)
L1707: Source: reviewer's analysis
Abbreviations: ABR, annualized bleeding rate; AT, antithrombin
L1708: Figure 46. Observed ABR Over Averaged AT Activity (Zero ABR Excluded)
L1709: Source: reviewer's analysis
L1710: Abbreviations: ABR, annualized bleeding rate; AT, antithrombin
L1711: In addition, ALT and AST data from 293 subjects in EFC14768, EFC14769, EFC15110, and LTE15174 were used to explore the correlation between AT activity and ALT/AST elevation. Figure 47 and Figure 48 suggested both ALT and AST elevation were associated with AT activity suppression. Targeting AT activity within the range of 15% to 35% could potentially reduce the percentage of subjects exhibiting &gt;1 \times ALT (Figure 49) and &gt;1 to 3 \times AST (Figure 50) levels compared to targeting AT activity below 15%.

L1724: In addition to ALT and AST elevation, AT activity below 10% is associated with increased risk for thromboembolic events. Taking these results into consideration, the target AT activity range of 15% to 35% to balance efficacy and safety of fitusiran appears reasonable.
L1725: ## 14.5.3. Addition of a Lower 10 mg Q2M Dose for Patients With AT &lt;15% on 20 mg Q2M
L1726: In the clinical trials, 11.7% of subjects discontinued treatment due to AT activity &lt;15% with the 20 mg Q2M dosing regimen. However, the majority of subjects, who received 80 mg QM before the voluntary dosing pause, did not exhibit AT levels returning to baseline when starting the AT-based dosing regimen after the dosing pause, which suggested a carryover effect of the 80 mg QM dosing regimen. As a result, the true percentage of subjects discontinuing treatment with the proposed dosing regimen without considering the carryover effect should be less than what was observed. The Applicant estimated that approximately 6% of the total virtual patient population would discontinue treatment due to AT levels below 15% with the 20 mg Q2M regimen. However, the Applicant's simulation approach did not account for the carryover effect following dose titration. The reviewer conducted a PopK-PD simulation that considered the carryover effect due to changes in dosage.
L1727: ## NDA 219019
## QFITLIA (fitusiran)
L1728: The virtual patient population was generated by resampling each studied subject 10 times and incorporating sampled interindividual variability for corresponding PopK-PD parameters. Dose modification for each virtual patient was based on trough AT levels for down-titration and maximum (predose) AT levels for up-titration. A 6-month AT profile was simulated for each dose level after last dose modification and before next potential dose modification.
L1729: The simulation indicated that 54.3% of the total virtual patient population remained on the 50 mg Q2M dosage regimen over time. Furthermore, 13.5% of the total virtual patients up-titrated to 50 mg QM, while 32.3% down-titrated to 20 mg Q2M. Among the patients who were down-titrated to 20 mg Q2M, 22% (representing 7% of the total virtual patient population) exhibited trough AT levels below 15%. To explore a lower dosage regimen that could retain these 7% of patients on fitusiran treatment, the reviewer simulated 10 mg Q2M and 15 mg Q2M using the virtual patients who had AT levels below 15% with the 20 mg Q2M regimen. The results suggested that 5.88% and 4.26% of the total virtual patient population could remain on treatment with the 10 mg Q2M and 15 mg Q2M regimens, respectively. Notably, neither regimen resulted in patients with AT levels exceeding 35%. Consequently, the review team recommended adding 10 mg Q2M as a lower dose option for patients who would otherwise discontinue treatment due to AT levels below 15% with the 20 mg Q2M regimen. The patient distribution with the review-team-proposed dosing regimen is shown in Figure 51.
L1730: Figure 51. Patient Distribution After Adding 10 mg Q2M
L1731: Source: reviewer's analysis
(): percentage of total virtual patients
Abbreviations: AT, antithrombin; Q2M, every 2 months
L1732: ## 14.5.4. Evaluation of AT Monitoring Program
L1733: The Applicant proposed that AT activity be measured prior to initiation of fitusiran treatment and (b) (4) If AT activity is &lt;15\%, then a dose reduction is required, and the lower dose should be initiated 3 months after the prior dose. If AT activity is &gt;35\% after 6 months, or if the patient has not achieved satisfactory bleed control, dose modification should be considered. In the clinical trials, subjects with more than one AT activity level &lt;15\% within a 12-month period must discontinue fitusiran dosing and may have the option to enroll into the lower dose cohort. Upon the first AT level &lt;15\%, the patient must have another AT sample drawn within 1 week of site receipt of the results. If this result is &lt;15\%, it was considered the second AT activity level &lt;15\%. Dose escalation in the clinical trial was based on 2 steady-state (Month 4, Month 5, and Month 6) AT activity levels &gt;35\%. To evaluate the appropriateness of the proposed AT monitoring program, the reviewer performed a PopK-PD simulation.
L1734: For the Q2M regimen, AT inhibition reached its maximum approximately 4 weeks after each dose (Month 1, Month 3, and Month 5), whereas the predose AT activity was close to the minimum AT inhibition within each dosing interval (Figure 52). The dose de-escalation based on the AT activity measured predose every other month led to 14.2% of patients being down-titrated to 20 mg Q2M, whereas down-titration would occur in approximately 32.3% of patients if using the peak effect of fitusiran on AT activity measured at 4 weeks post each dose (Month 1, Month 3, and Month 5) (Table 139). Considering the severe safety outcome associated with low AT activity, the review team recommended that dose de-escalation be based on AT activity measured at 4 weeks after each dose (Month 1, Month 3, and Month 5) rather than AT activity measured predose every other month. For the QM regimen, the fluctuation of AT activity between maximum and trough AT activity was minimal, and the AT activity measured at each month was close to the maximum AT inhibition (Figure 53). Additionally, patients on the QM dosing regimen had less concern for low levels of AT. As a result, the review-team-proposed AT monitoring program can be applied to a QM dosing regimen as well.
L1735: **Figure 52. AT Activity Over Time for Virtual Patients Receiving 50 mg Q2M**
L1736: Source: reviewer's analysis
Abbreviations: AT, antithrombin; Q2M, every 2 months

L1738: Percentage of total virtual patients
De-escalation based on	20 mg Q2M	Discontinue due to AT < 15% with 20 mg Q2M
Pre-dose AT	14.2%	1.8%
AT measured at one month post each dose	32.3%	7%
L1739: Source: reviewer's analysis
L1740: Abbreviations: AT, antithrombin; Q2M, every 2 months
L1741: To evaluate the proposed dose escalation rule, the reviewer simulated three scenarios: (1) all 3 predose AT &gt;35%; (2) 2 out of 3 predose AT &gt;35%; (3) at least 1 predose AT &gt;35%. The proposed AT monitoring after 6 months is equivalent to the simulation scenario where 3 predose ATs are required to be &gt;35% since the AT activity gradually decreases over time in each 6-month period and AT at 6 months would be the maximum predose inhibition (Figure 53). All three scenarios had 0.46% of total virtual patients who had AT &gt;35% after dose escalation. If the dose is escalated with only one predose AT &gt;35%, then there is a potential risk to induce AT below 15% (Table 140). Based on the simulation results, the proposed dose escalation rule appears to be reasonable.
