L1749: ## 14.5.5. Evaluation of Covariate Effect of Race on AT
L1750: In clinical trials, the majority (93.9%) of the subjects who discontinued the drug due to low AT levels were Asians. The covariate effect of race on AT activity was explored using PopK-PD simulation. The original PopK-PD model dataset consists of 41.9% of Caucasian (n=142), 1.5% of Black or African American (n=5), 53.4% of Asian (n=181), and 3.2% of other races (n=11). The virtual patients were resampled from the studied subjects while maintaining the same race
L1751: distribution as observed in the trial. The dose distribution stratified by race is shown in Figure 54. The simulation indicated Asian patients had the highest number of treatment discontinuations due to AT &lt;15%. The percentage of Asian patients who discontinued treatment was larger than that of Caucasian patients. The percentage of discontinued patients was comparable between Asian and Race: Other groups. However, the limited number of patients in the Black and Race: Other groups makes it difficult to draw solid conclusions. The higher number of Asian patients who discontinued treatment in the trial was partially due to their larger representation in the studied population. The reviewer did not identify potential confounding factors (including body weight) that were imbalanced between patients who discontinued or remained on treatment (Figure 55). Since a lower 10 mg Q2M was recommended for all patients, no additional dose adjustment is needed based on race.

L1759: ## 15. Study/Trial Design
L1760: ## 15.1. Study EFC14768
L1761: ## Study Title
L1762: ATLAS-INH: A Phase 3 Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of fitusiran in Patients with Hemophilia A or B, with Inhibitory Antibodies to Factor VIII or IX
L1763: ## Study Dates
L1764: Study initiation date: February 14, 2018 (date of first subject screened)
L1765: Study completion: June 23, 2021 (last subject last visit)
L1766: ## Study Objectives
L1767: ## Primary
L1768: To evaluate the efficacy of fitusiran compared to on-demand treatment with BPAs (bypassing agents), as determined by the frequency of bleeding episodes.
L1769: ## Secondary
L1770: - To evaluate the efficacy of fitusiran compared to on-demand treatment with BPAs, as determined by:The frequency of spontaneous bleeding episodes.
- The frequency of joint bleeding episodes.
- Health-related quality of life (HRQOL) in subjects \ge 17 years of age
- To determine the frequency of bleeding episodes during the onset period (defined as the first 28 days after the first dose of fitusiran, during which the target pharmacodynamic effect is reached).
- To determine the safety and tolerability of fitusiran.
L1771: ## Study Endpoints
L1772: ## Primary
L1773: Annualized Bleeding Rate (ABR) for treated bleeds in the treatment period (defined in Section 6.2, Table 14).
L1774: ## Secondary
L1775: - ABR for treated bleeds in the efficacy period.
- Annualized treated spontaneous bleeding rate in the efficacy period (defined in Section 6.2, Table 14).
- Annualized treated joint bleeding rate in the efficacy period.
- Change in Hemophilia Quality of Life Questionnaire for Adults (Haem-A-QOL) physical health score and total score in the treatment period.
L1776: ## Study Design
L1777: The ATLAS-INH (EFC14768) study was a multicenter, multinational, randomized, open-label phase 3 study designed to evaluate the efficacy and safety of fitusiran in male subjects aged \ge 12 years with hemophilia A or B and inhibitory antibodies to factor FVIII or factor FIX, who are not receiving prophylactic therapy.
L1778: Eligible patients were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to either the fitusiran treatment arm, receiving 80 mg administered subcutaneously (SC) as prophylaxis once monthly, with use of on-demand BPAs for treatment of breakthrough bleeding episodes; or the on-demand arm, receiving BPAs for treatment of breakthrough bleeding episodes. Randomization was stratified by the number of bleeding episodes in the 6 months prior to Screening (\le 10 versus &gt; 10). All subjects were treated
L1779: for a total of 9 months; subjects randomized to the fitusiran treatment arm received a total of 9 SC injections of fitusiran. Because the full PD effect of fitusiran is not achieved until approximately 28 days after receiving the first dose, efficacy was assessed during the final 8 months on study (Day 29 to Month 9). Scheduled assessments included monthly physical exams, triglyceride levels, AT activity levels, hematology labs, liver tests, and serum chemistry. Subjects from both the fitusiran and on-demand treatment arms who completed the study were eligible for participation in an open-label extension study.
L1780: BPA include recombinant factor VIIa, Factors VIII and IX, and activated prothrombin complex concentrates (aPCC).
L1781: Breakthrough bleeding is defined as any occurrence of hemorrhage that requires administration of factor concentrates or BPA infusion, e.g., hemarthrosis, muscle, or mucosal bleeding. A spontaneous bleeding episode is a bleeding event that occurs for no apparent or known reason, particularly into the joints, muscles, and soft tissues. A joint bleeding episode is characterized by an unusual sensation in the joint ("aura") in combination with: (1) increasing swelling or warmth over the skin over the joint; (2) increasing pain; or (3) progressive loss of range of motion or difficulty in using the limb as compared with baseline. A muscle bleed may be characterized by pain, swelling, and loss of movement over the affected muscle group. A target joint is defined as a joint where 3 or more spontaneous bleeding episodes in a single joint within a consecutive 6-month period has occurred; where there have been \le 2 bleeding episodes in the joint within a consecutive 12-month period the joint is no longer considered a target joint. A traumatic bleeding episode is one that is caused by a known injury or trauma. Bleeding episodes sustained during sports and recreation were counted as traumatic bleeding episodes.
L1782: ## **Bleed Management Guidelines**
L1783: ## **On-Demand Arm**
L1784: For subjects not receiving fitusiran (on-demand arm), bleeding management therapy with BPAs was to be managed based on the local standard practice for treating hemophilia patients with inhibitors, as routinely administered by the physician. Where clinical circumstances allowed, it was recommended that the subject contact the Investigator for all events suspected to be or characteristic of a bleeding episode. If adequate hemostasis did not occur after two doses of BPA, the subject was to contact the site for further instruction. If the subject felt the need to administer doses higher than the subject's bleeding episode management plan recommended, or at a higher frequency, it was recommended that the subject contact the site.
L1785: ## **Fitusiran Treatment Arm**
L1786: Given the mechanism of action and PD profile of fitusiran, the BPA dose necessary to safely and effectively treat breakthrough bleeding episodes in subjects receiving fitusiran (fitusiran treatment arm) was lower than standardly prescribed (see Table 141 below). This was supported by data from the phase 1 study in which bleeding events were managed with factor or BPA, as well as additional modeling and ex vivo spiking data.
L1787: Subjects on fitusiran were provided a written bleed management plan (as described below) with appropriate dosing of BPA for use during Day 1 through 7, as well as a bleed management plan with dosing for Day 8 and beyond. Thereafter the bleed management plan was reviewed and updated at monthly visits, and new written plans were provided to the subject if dosing changed.
L1788: Importantly, during the treatment period, subjects were not to use BPA or other hemostatic agents as prophylaxis for bleeding episode prevention, including doses related to anticipated hemostatic challenges such as physical activity.
L1789: ## Day 1 to Day 7
L1790: Subjects were to be instructed to call the site prior to administering factor or BPA. Subjects were to continue with their standard BPA regimens.
L1791: ## Day 8 and Beyond
L1792: - Subject was to be instructed to call the study center to discuss symptoms to determine whether or not they were consistent with a bleeding event and to discuss the appropriate factor/BPA dose to use. This interaction between subject and Investigator is recommended prior to the administration of each dose of factor or BPA. Confirmation of bleeds at the study center prior to treatment may be considered.
- If a determination was made that symptoms required treatment, the recommended treatment algorithm for bleeding episodes is described below:A single dose could be administered according to the guidelines in Table 141.
- The subject was to be instructed to reevaluate symptoms in 24 hours for bleeds treated with FVIII, FIX or aPCC and in 2 to 3 hours for bleeds treated with rFVIIa.
- Administration of FIX Extended half-life should not be more frequent than every 5 to 7 days.
- If a second dose (in the case of FVIII, FIX or aPCC) or a third dose (in the case of rFVIIa) was needed, the subject was to call the study center before dosing.Study staff were to consider evaluation and treatment of the subject at the study center and confirmation of bleeds when any repeated doses were needed.
- If more than two doses of FVIII, FIX, or aPCC, or three doses of rFVIIa were needed, the subject was to be seen at the study center within 48 to 72 hours.
- Doses were not to be administered at less than 24-hour intervals (except rFVIIa as indicated in Table 141).
- Doses were not to exceed the protocol maximum recommended dose indicated in Table 141.
- Consultation with the study Medical Monitor and Clinical Advisor was to be considered for clinical circumstances below, that may warrant AT replacement:Doses of factor or BPA higher than those recommended in Table 141
- Dosing of factor or BPA at decreased intervals than those recommended in Table 141
- Multiple or repeated doses of factor or BPA
- Antifibrinolytics were not to be used in combination with factor or BPA.

L1798: ## Perioperative Treatment Plan
L1799: In subjects in both the on-demand and fitusiran treatment arms undergoing surgery, a written perioperative treatment plan was reviewed with the study Medical Monitor before conducting the procedure, unless clinical circumstances did not allow. In subjects on fitusiran, the perioperative treatment plans were developed using the same principles as bleed management.
L1800: If a fitusiran dose was scheduled to occur on or in close proximity to the day of surgery, or anytime during the perioperative period, the dose should be withheld. The Perioperative Evaluation Period was defined as the day of the surgery through the final day on which supplemental hemostatic or antithrombotic treatments were administered as part of the perioperative treatment plan. Fitusiran dosing could be resumed at the next scheduled visit following the Perioperative Evaluation Period at the discretion of the Investigator. If multiple consecutive doses of fitusiran were withheld, the Investigator was to consult with the study Medical Monitor, who would determine if the subject could continue on study.
L1801: ## 15.2. Study EFC14769
L1802: ## Study Title
L1803: ATLAS-A/B: A Phase 3 Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of Fitusiran in Patients With Hemophilia A or B, Without Inhibitory Antibodies to Factor VIII or IX
L1804: ## Study Dates
L1805: Study initiation date: March 1, 2018
L1806: Study completion: July 14, 2021
L1807: ## Study Objectives
L1808: ## Primary
L1809: To evaluate the efficacy of fitusiran compared to on-demand treatment with factor concentrates, as determined by the frequency of bleeding episodes
L1810: ## Secondary
L1811: - To evaluate the efficacy of fitusiran compared to on-demand treatment with factor concentrates, as determined by:The frequency of spontaneous bleeding episodes
- The frequency of joint bleeding episodes
- Health-related quality of life (HRQOL) in subjects \ge 17 years of age
- To determine the frequency of bleeding episodes during the onset period
- To determine the safety and tolerability of fitusiran
L1812: ## Study Endpoints
L1813: ## Primary
L1814: Annualized Bleeding Rate (ABR) for treated bleeds in the treatment period.
L1815: ## Secondary
L1816: - ABR for treated bleeds in the efficacy period.
- Annualized treated spontaneous bleeding rate in the efficacy period.
- Annualized treated joint bleeding rate in the efficacy period.

L1817: ## **Study Design**
L1818: The ATLAS-A/B (EFC14769) study was a multicenter, multinational, randomized, open-label phase 3 study designed to evaluate the efficacy and safety of fitusiran in male subjects aged \ge 12 years, with hemophilia A or B and without inhibitory antibodies to factor VIII (FVIII) or factor IX (FIX), who are not receiving prophylactic therapy.
L1819: Eligible patients were to be randomized in a 2:1 ratio to either the fitusiran treatment arm receiving fitusiran 80 mg administered subcutaneously (SC) as prophylaxis once monthly, with use of on-demand factor concentrates or treatment of breakthrough bleeding episodes, or the on-demand arm with on-demand factor concentrates for treatment of breakthrough bleeding episodes. All patients will be treated for a total of 9 months; patients randomized to the fitusiran treatment arm will receive a total of 9 SC injections of fitusiran. Because the full PD effect of fitusiran is not achieved until approximately 28 days after receiving the first dose, efficacy will be assessed during the remaining 8 months on study (Day 29 to Month 9). Subjects from both the fitusiran and on-demand treatment arms who complete the study may be eligible for participation in an open-label extension study.
L1820: Breakthrough bleeding definitions, Bleed Management Guidelines, and Perioperative Treatment Plan were the same as Study EFC14768 (See Section 15.1 above).
L1821: ## **15.3. Study LTE15174**
L1822: ## **Study Title**
L1823: ATLAS-OLE: An Open-label, Long-term Safety and Efficacy Study of Fitusiran in Patients with Hemophilia A or B, with or without Inhibitory Antibodies to Factor VIII or IX
L1824: ## **Study Dates**
L1825: Study initiation date: January 10, 2019
L1826: Study completion: June 14, 2023
L1827: ## **Study Objectives**
L1828: ## **Primary**
L1829: To characterize the long-term safety and tolerability of fitusiran.
L1830: ## **Secondary**
L1831: - To characterize the long-term efficacy of fitusiran as assessed by the frequency of:Bleeding episodes
- Spontaneous bleeding episodes
- Joint bleeding episodes
- To characterize the effects of fitusiran on health-related quality of life measures in subjects \ge 17 years of age
L1832: ## **Study Endpoints**
L1833: ## **Primary**
L1834: Incidence, severity, relatedness, and seriousness of AEs, and laboratory assessments.
L1835: ## **Secondary**
L1836: - Annualized bleeding rate for treated bleeds in the treatment period.
- Annualized treated spontaneous bleeding rate in the treatment period.
- Annualized treated joint bleeding rate in the treatment period.
- Change in Haem-A-QoL physical health score in the treatment period (in subjects \ge 17 years of age).
L1837: ## **Study Design**
L1838: The ATLAS-OLE (LTE15174) study was a multicenter, multinational, open-label extension study of the long-term safety and efficacy of fitusiran in males \ge 12 years of age with hemophilia A or B with or without inhibitory antibodies to FVIII or FIX. Subjects from all three parent studies (EFC14768, EFC14769, and ATLAS-PPX) were considered for enrollment.
L1839: The study had 3 periods:
L1840: - A screening period of up to 60 days (under certain circumstances, screening could be extended beyond 60 days following consultation with the Sponsor).
- An open-label treatment period that could vary depending on the timing of subject's restart after lifting of the dosing pause implemented in Q4 2020 (i.e., subjects were to be dosed up to 48 months after the restart):Subjects that started fitusiran dosing in this study after lifting of the dosing pause were planned to have a maximum treatment duration of 48 months.
- Subjects that had started fitusiran before the 2020 dosing pause were planned to have a maximum treatment duration of 76 months.
- An approximately 6-month AT follow-up period after the last dose of fitusiran (note that some subjects may have undergone two 6-month AT follow-up periods, one at the end of the core study, i.e., before starting in the low dose cohort, and one at the completion of the whole study).
L1841: On-demand factors or BPAs were utilized for treatment of breakthrough bleeding episodes. BPAs include recombinant factor VIIa, Factors VIII and IX, as well as activated prothrombin complex concentrates (aPCC).
L1842: Scheduled assessments included monthly physical exams, hematology labs, and serum chemistry. Triglyceride levels, AT activity levels, and liver tests were done monthly for the first half of the study and then every 4 months.
L1843: Breakthrough bleeding definitions, Bleed Management Guidelines, and Perioperative Treatment Plan were the same as Study EFC14768.
L1844: The AT-DR initially consisted of a starting dose of 50 mg once every 2 months (Q2M) with options to escalate to 50 mg once monthly (QM) or 80 mg QM, or to de-escalate to 20 mg Q2M or 20 mg QM, based on an individual's AT response. The AT-DR was initiated after a voluntary dosing pause in October 2020. In detail, the final AT-DR used in study LTE15174 stated that if at the starting dose of 50 mg Q2M, AT was &lt;15% twice (per central lab), the study subjects would have to discontinue fitusiran administration at this dosing regimen and de-escalate to 20 mg Q2M. If the steady state AT on at least two occasions was &gt;35% (per central lab), the fitusiran dose was to be escalated to 50 mg QM (or 20 mg QM, respectively in the low dose cohort). If 50 mg QM also resulted in AT activity level &gt;35%, then the subject could escalate to 80 mg QM, provided that he had no prior history of central AT activity &lt;15% more than one time during any prior exposure to fitusiran. Subjects previously exposed to fitusiran at a dose of 80 mg monthly with no more than 1 AT activity level &lt;15% at any time during fitusiran treatment (based on at least 3 months of prior AT measurements) had the option to remain on that dose. The Investigator could consider dose escalation on clinical grounds even if the subjects AT values were within 15 to 35% if bleed control was considered inadequate.
L1845: ## **Eligibility Criteria**
L1846: ## **Inclusion Criteria**
L1847: - Subject must be at least 12 years of age inclusive, at the time of signing the informed consent.
- Subjects with severe hemophilia A or B who have completed a phase 3 fitusiran clinical trial.
- Male - There are no contraceptive requirements for this study except where required by local regulations.
- Capable of giving signed informed consent which includes compliance with the requirements and restrictions listed in the ICF and in this protocol. In countries where legal age of majority is above 18 years, a specific ICF must also be signed by the subject's legally authorized representative.
L1848: ## **Exclusion Criteria**
L1849: - Completion of a surgical procedure within 14 days prior to screening, or currently receiving additional factor concentrate or BPA infusion for postoperative hemostasis.
- Current participation in immune tolerance induction therapy.
- Current use of factor concentrate or BPAs as regularly administered prophylaxis designed to prevent spontaneous bleeding episodes. However, subjects requiring factor concentrates or BPAs as prophylaxis during the study dosing pause period, per Investigator's judgement in agreement with the study Medical Monitor, may be permitted to be enrolled.
- Use of compounds other than factor concentrates or BPAs for hemophilia treatment, including use of emicizumab (Hemlibra®) within 6 months prior to screening.
- Received an investigational drug or device, other than fitusiran, within 30 days of anticipated IMP administration or 5 half-lives of the IMP, whichever is longer.
- Current or prior participation in a gene therapy trial.
L1850: - ALT and/or AST &gt;1.5\times upper limit of normal reference range (ULN) for subjects who were naïve to fitusiran at study start; ALT and/or AST &gt;5\times ULN for subjects who were in the fitusiran arm in the parent study.
- Individuals accommodated in an institution because of regulatory or legal order; prisoners or subjects who are legally institutionalized.
- Any country-related specific regulation that would prevent the subject from entering the study – see Appendix 8 (Section 10.8) (country-specific requirements).
- Subject not suitable for participation, whatever the reason, as judged by the Investigator, including medical or clinical conditions, or subjects potentially at risk of noncompliance to study procedures.
- Subjects who are dependent on the Sponsor or Investigator (as defined in Section 1.61 of the ICH-Good Clinical Practice E6) (November 2016).
- Subjects are employees of the clinical study site or other individuals directly involved in the conduct of the study, or immediate family members of such individuals.
- Any specific situation during study implementation/course that may rise ethics considerations.
- Sensitivity to any of the study interventions, or components thereof, or drug or other allergy that, in the opinion of the Investigator, contraindicates participation in the study.

L1853: ## Disposition
L1854: Of 85 subjects screened, 57 were randomized to study treatment: 19 in the BPA on-demand arm and 38 in the fitusiran prophylaxis arm. There were 3 subjects who were treated but not randomized (due to protocol amendment).

L1863: ## **Primary Endpoint Results**
L1864: Fitusiran demonstrated a reduced ABR for treated bleeds in the treatment period. The ABRs estimated based on negative binomial regression model in the treatment period were 2.0 (95% CI: 1.3, 3.1) for the fitusiran prophylaxis arm and 18.8 (95% CI: 11.5, 30.7) for the BPA on-demand arm, corresponding to an 89.2% reduction in the bleeding rate associated with fitusiran prophylaxis during the treatment period (rate ratio of 0.11 [95% CI: 0.06, 0.21], p&lt;0.0001).
L1865: ## **Secondary Endpoint Results**
L1866: There was a significant reduction in the spontaneous estimated ABR for treated bleeds in subjects treated with fitusiran (0.9 [95% CI: 0.5 to 1.6]) compared to those treated with BPA on demand (15.7 [95% CI: 9.3 to 26.5]), corresponding to a 94.4% reduction in the treated spontaneous bleeds associated with fitusiran prophylaxis during the efficacy period (rate ratio of 0.06 [95% CI: 0.03, 0.12], p&lt;0.0001).
L1867: The estimated ABR for treated bleeds in joints was 1.3 (95% CI: 0.8, 2.3) and 13.8 (95% CI: 8.0, 23.8) in the fitusiran prophylaxis and BPA on demand arms, respectively, corresponding to a 90.2% reduction in treated joint bleeds associated with fitusiran prophylaxis during the efficacy period (rate ratio of 0.10 [95% CI: 0.05, 0.21], p&lt;0.0001).
L1868: Total scores (transformed) of Haem-A-QoL demonstrated overall improvements in perceived QoL in fitusiran prophylaxis arm, with reported LS mean (95% CI) -15.3 (-19.3, -11.2) and -0.4 (-5.7, 4.8) reductions from baseline in the fitusiran prophylaxis and BPA on-demand arms, respectively. The observed LS mean difference was -14.9 (-21.4, -8.3) in favor of fitusiran (p&lt;0.0001).
L1869: ## 16.2. Study EFC14769
L1870: ## Disposition
L1871: Of 177 subjects screened, 120 were randomized to study treatment: 80 in the fitusiran prophylaxis arm and 40 in the factor on demand arm. Out of 177 subjects screened, 57 were screen failures. There was 1 subject randomized to the fitusiran prophylaxis arm but not treated. The subject voluntarily withdrew his consent from study. There were no subjects who were treated but not randomized.

L1878: ## Primary Endpoint Results
L1879: A reduced ABR for treated bleeds was demonstrated with fitusiran prophylaxis in the treatment period. The ABRs for treated bleeds estimated based on negative binomial regression model were 4.1 (95% CI: 3.1, 5.3) for the fitusiran prophylaxis arm and 31.4 (95% CI: 23.0, 43.0) for the factor on demand arm, corresponding to an 87.0% reduction in the bleeding rate associated with fitusiran prophylaxis during the treatment period (rate ratio of 0.13 [95% CI: 0.09, 0.19], p&lt;0.0001).
L1880: ## Secondary Endpoint Results
L1881: There was a significant reduction in the spontaneous estimated ABR for treated bleeds in subjects treated with fitusiran (1.8 [95% CI: 1.2, 2.7]) compared to those treated on-demand (22.0 [95% CI: 14.2, 34.3]), corresponding to a 91.7% reduction in the treated spontaneous bleeds associated with fitusiran prophylaxis during the efficacy period (rate ratio of 0.08 [95% CI: 0.05, 0.14], p&lt;0.0001).
L1882: The estimated ABR for treated bleeds in joints was 2.3 (95% CI: 1.6, 3.3) and 23.4 (95% CI: 15.4, 35.7) in the fitusiran prophylaxis and factor on demand arms, respectively, corresponding to a 90.3% reduction in treated joint bleeds associated with fitusiran prophylaxis during the efficacy period (rate ratio of 0.10 [95% CI: 0.06, 0.16], p&lt;0.0001).
L1883: The transformed total scores of Haem-A-QoL demonstrated overall improvements in perceived QoL in the fitusiran prophylaxis arm, with a reported LS mean (95% CI) of -9.7 (-12.5, -6.9), compared to the factor on demand arm LS mean of -2.6 (-6.3, 1.0). The LS mean difference was -7.1 (-11.2, -2.9) in favor of fitusiran (p=0.0011).
L1884: ## 16.3. Study LTE15174

One subject was escalated to 80 mg and de-escalated to 50 mg by the end of the study. L1902: Pool C was not adequately powered for the analysis of ABR for treated bleeds and did not have formal hypothesis testing for either superiority or noninferiority of fitusiran. Therefore, data from Pool C are considered descriptive in nature only.
L1903: ## 16.4.1. Design, Pool C
L1904: ATLAS-PPX (EFC151110) was a multicenter, open-label study in male subjects aged \ge 12 years with hemophilia A or B with or without inhibitory antibodies to FVIII or FIX, who switched to fitusiran from prior bypassing agent (BPA, Cohort A) or factor (Cohort B) prophylaxis. The study design is shown in Figure 56.
L1905: The study had three periods. There was a 6-month factor or BPA prophylaxis period in which subjects continued their baseline, regularly scheduled prophylaxis regimen with factor concentrates or BPAs. Next, a 1-month onset period in which subjects received their first dose of fitusiran while continuing their factor or BPA prophylaxis for up to 7 days. Then, a 6-month fitusiran efficacy period in which subjects received fitusiran as prophylaxis. Together, the 1-month onset period and the 6-month fitusiran efficacy period constitute the fitusiran treatment period. Throughout the study, subjects may have received on-demand treatment for breakthrough bleeding episodes with factors or BPAs, as appropriate. Details on management of breakthrough bleeds are described in Section 15. All subjects were treated for 9 months and, upon completion, were eligible for participation in an open-label extension study.
L1906: Figure 56. Study Design, EFC151110
L1907: Source: Applicant's Clinical Study Protocol for ATLAS-PPX
L1908: Note: For subgroup of Cohort A subjects enrolling directly into the fitusiran treatment period, see Figure 2.
L1909: Note: For the United States specific requirements, see Section 11.6.1.2.
L1910: a Subjects continued to receive prescribed factor or BPA prophylaxis for the first 7 days of the onset period.
L1911: b Following final fitusiran dose, AT activity level was monitored at monthly intervals following the final fitusiran dose until activity levels return to approximately 60% (per the central laboratory) or per Investigator discretion in consultation with the study Medical Monitor.
L1912: Abbreviations: AT, antithrombin; BPA, bypassing agent
L1913: The Study design for Study ATLAS-OLE (LTE15174) is described in Section 15.3.
L1914: ## 16.4.2. Objectives and Endpoints, Pool C
L1915: ## Objectives
L1916: The primary objective was to evaluate the efficacy of fitusiran treatment at the revised AT-DR compared to standard-of-care (SOC) prophylaxis as determined by the frequency of bleeding episodes.
L1917: The major secondary objectives:
L1918: - To characterize the following while receiving fitusiran treatment, relative to receiving factor or BPA prophylaxis:The frequency of spontaneous bleeding episodes.
- The frequency of joint bleeding episodes.
- Health-related quality of life (HRQOL) in subjects \ge 17 years of age.
- To characterize the frequency of bleeding episodes during the onset and treatment periods in subjects receiving fitusiran.
L1919: ## Endpoints
L1920: The primary efficacy endpoint was annualized bleeding rate (ABR) for treated bleeds in the fitusiran primary efficacy period compared to the control period (Table 14).
L1921: The major secondary endpoints are listed below:
L1922: - Annualized treated spontaneous bleeding rate (AsBR).
- Annualized treated joint bleeding rate (AjBR).
- Change in Haemophilia Quality of Life Questionnaire for Adults (Haem-A-QoL) Physical Health domain score.
L1923: ## 16.4.3. Eligibility Criteria, Pool C
L1924: Eligibility criteria for Study EFC151110 are described below. Study LTE15174 included subjects who completed a previous parent phase 3 clinical study of fitusiran and had central AT activity level \ge 22\% (prior to dosing).
L1925: ## ATLAS-PPX (EFC151110)
L1926: ## Key Inclusion Criteria
L1927: - Males \ge 12 years of age.
- Severe hemophilia A or B as evidenced by:A central laboratory measurement at screening or documented medical record evidence of FVIII &lt;1\% or FIX level \le 2\%.
- A minimum of 2 bleeding episodes requiring BPA treatment within the last 6 months prior to Screening for patients with inhibitory antibodies to Factor VIII or Factor IX (Cohort A). A minimum of 1 bleeding episode requiring factor treatment within the last 12 months prior to Screening for patients without inhibitory antibodies to Factor VIII or Factor IX (Cohort B).
- Must meet either the definition of inhibitor or noninhibitor patient as below:InhibitorUse of BPAs for prophylaxis and for any bleeding episodes for at least the last 6 months prior to Screening, and meet one of the following Nijmegen-modified Bethesda assay results criteria:Inhibitor titer of \ge 0.6 BU/mL at Screening, or
- Inhibitor titer of &lt;0.6 BU/mL at Screening with medical record evidence of 2 consecutive titers \ge 0.6 BU/mL, or
- Inhibitor titer of &lt;0.6 BU/mL at Screening with medical record evidence of anamnestic response
- The subgroup of patients in Cohort A patients must additionally meet the following criteria to be eligible to start treatment with fitusiran directly after the screening period:Hemophilia B with inhibitory antibody to Factor IX as defined above
- Not responding adequately to BPA treatment (historical ABR \ge 20) prior to enrollment
- In the opinion of the Investigator, with approval of Sponsor Medical Monitor, 6-month BPA prophylaxis period should be omitted.
L1928: b. Noninhibitor

i. Use of factor concentrates for prophylaxis and for any bleeding episodes for at least the last 6 months prior to Screening, and meet each of the following criteria:

- Nijmegen-modified Bethesda assay inhibitor titer of &lt;0.6 BU/mL at Screening and
- No use of bypassing agents to treat bleeding episodes for at least the last 6 months prior to Screening and
- No history of immune tolerance induction therapy within the past 3 years prior to Screening.
5. Prescribed prophylactic treatment (documented in the medical or pharmacy records) of hemophilia with factor concentrates or BPAs for at least 6 months prior to Screening; the regimen must be consistent with the approved prescribing information for the product or local recommendations, allowing for adjustment to individual subject response, and designed to decrease spontaneous bleeding.

6. Adherent to the prescribed prophylactic therapy for at least 6 months prior to Screening per Investigator assessment.
L1929: Other Key Exclusion Criteria are the same as Pool A as described in Section 6.2.1.3.
L1930: ## 16.4.4. Statistical Analysis Plan, Pool C
L1931: ## Analysis Population
L1932: The following analysis sets were defined for the statistical analysis:
L1933: Full analysis set for Pool C of Study EFC15110 (ATLAS-PPX)/Study LTE15174 (ATLAS-OLE):
L1934: - For treatment period: AT-DR fitusiran treatment data were generated from both the extension study LTE15174 and study EFC15110.
- For control period: SOC prophylaxis control data were generated from the control period of the original switching study EFC15110.
L1935: ## Analysis of the Primary Endpoint
L1936: The primary efficacy endpoint was annualized bleeding rate (ABR) for treated bleeds.
L1937: The primary endpoint was analyzed using a repeated measures negative binomial model. The variable was the number of bleeding episodes. The study period for Switching in Pool C (primary efficacy period or control period) was treated as fixed effects. The robust sandwich covariance matrix constructed to account for within-subject dependence was considered for Switching Pool C. The logarithm of the duration (in years) that each subject spent in each study period matching the bleeding episode data being analyzed was included as an offset variable to account for unequal follow-up time due to early withdrawal, surgery, etc.
L1938: Neither the parent study EFC15110 nor Pool C was adequately powered for the analysis of ABR for treated bleeds. Pool C did not have any formal hypothesis to test either superiority or noninferiority of fitusiran prophylaxis compared to prior SOC prophylaxis.
L1939: ## Analysis of Major Secondary Endpoints
L1940: ## **Annualized Treated Spontaneous Bleeding Rate**
L1941: The same analysis method for the primary endpoint was used for this key secondary endpoint.
L1942: ## **Annualized Treated Joint Bleeding Rate**
L1943: The same analysis method for the primary endpoint was used for this key secondary endpoint.
L1944: ## **Change in Haem-A-QoL Physical Health Domain Score**
L1945: Change from baseline in Haem-A-QoL physical health score (in subjects \ge 17 years of age) in the control period and primary efficacy period were analyzed using a mixed model for repeated measure analysis (MMRM) with robust sandwich covariance matrix, with fixed effects of study period (factor or BPA prophylaxis period and fitusiran treatment period) and baseline Haem-A-QoL Physical Health domain score for each period.
L1946: ## **Multiple Testing Strategy**
L1947: No multiplicity control was provided.
L1948: ## **Handling of Intercurrent Events and Missing Data**
L1949: Handling of intercurrent events and missing data are the same as described for Pool A in Section 6.2.1.4.
L1950: ## 16.4.5. Results of Analyses, Pool C
L1951: ## **Subject Disposition**
L1952: The population for Pool C was comprised of subjects in Study LTE15174 and Study EFC15110 who switched from SOC prophylaxis to fitusiran prophylaxis. The full analysis set for the SOC prophylaxis arm is comprised of all EFC15110 enrolled subjects who received factor or BPA prophylaxis in Study EFC15110 and any dose of fitusiran. The fitusiran prophylaxis arm is comprised of all subjects who received at least one dose of fitusiran. Fifty-nine (85.5%) subjects were enrolled and entered the fitusiran prophylaxis arm in the treatment period (dose resumption Day 1 to Day 364). There were 54 subjects who received at least one dose of fitusiran who completed the study period and 5 subjects who received at least one dose of fitusiran who did not complete the study period. Of these 5 subjects, one (1.4%) discontinued the study because of adverse events, 2 (2.9%) withdrew consent, and 2 (2.9%) were discontinued because of more than 1 AT measurement &lt;15% (Table 146).

L1962: ## **Analysis of the Primary Endpoint**
L1963: **Table 148** summarizes the annualized bleeding rates (ABR) for treated bleeds in the fitusiran prophylaxis period compared to the SOC prophylaxis period as determined by the Applicant. The estimated ABR was 7.5 (95% CI: 5.6, 10.1) for the SOC prophylaxis period and 5.7 (95% CI: 3.9, 8.2) for the fitusiran prophylaxis (AT-DR) period. The rate ratio (AT-DR prophylaxis/SOC prophylaxis) was 0.75 (95% CI: 0.47, 1.2). There were no formal hypotheses preplanned and the results were exploratory only and should be interpreted with caution. However, the results observed appear descriptively to be similar with both types of prophylaxis. For exploratory purposes, the results of the subjects on prior prophylaxis with a fixed dose of fitusiran in study
L1964: EFC15110 were compared to the AT-DR in study LTE15174. The comparison results of 80 mg QM versus SOC prophylaxis from the parent study are also presented in Table 148 for reference.

L1970: The FDA review team obtained slightly different efficacy results than described above. The FDA review team derived an estimated ABR for treated bleeds of 5.8 (4.0, 8.4) in the fitusiran period and 7.5 (5.6, 10.1) in the SOC prophylaxis period, with a corresponding rate ratio of 0.77 with a nominal p-value of 0.26. The team sent an information request (IR) on December 5, 2024, to request clarification, and the Applicant responded that the discrepancy in the estimates was numerically small and the overall conclusion remains unchanged. The difference appears to be related to a technical issue in the operating system of running SAS programs.
L1971: Subgroup analyses were conducted for the primary endpoint by hemophilia types (i.e., HA or HB) and by different inhibitor cohorts (i.e., with or without inhibitors). In the Type C Meeting, on July 26, 2018, the Division recommended formal statistical testing for study EFC15110 in order to determine the treatment effect of fitusiran in subjects switched from factor or BPA prophylaxis. In addition, the Division recommended to include sufficient numbers of subjects with and without inhibitors, in order for efficacy to be assessed in both populations. However, neither study EFC15110 nor Pool C included formal statistical testing for efficacy endpoints, including the primary efficacy endpoint ABR. The ABRs in different inhibitor cohorts were not prespecified endpoints. In the final statistical analysis plan for study EFC15110, the determination of the sample size for study EFC15110 was based on clinical considerations rather than a power calculation, and the Applicant stated that the p-value should be interpreted with caution.
L1972: Although the final clinical study reports for study EFC15110 and Pool C presented superiority testing results for the primary efficacy endpoint ABR, without adequately powered formal statistical testing to detect differences, the analyses are considered exploratory. The Agency notified the Applicant in a Type C Meeting on September 18, 2023, that the results of this study would be descriptive, as there was no prespecified decision rule. Given the exploratory nature of this analysis, results from Pool C will not be included in labeling.
L1973: ## 16.5. Patient-Reported Hemophilia-Related Symptoms and Physical Functioning for Pools A and B
L1974: The patient-reported outcome (PRO) endpoint of change in Haem-A-QoL Physical Health domain score from baseline was included by the Applicant in the statistical testing hierarchy as the third key secondary endpoint for Pools A and B (b) (4). Analysis of this endpoint for Pool A failed to reach statistical significance, whereas in Pool B, this endpoint did reach statistical significance.
L1975: The Haem-A-QoL Physical Health domain is a reasonable option to assess patient-reported hemophilia signs/symptoms (swellings and joint pain) and physical functioning (b) (4). However, in a Type C Guidance meeting on September 18, 2023, the Agency raised the concerns that the open-label design limits interpretation of the PRO, as a subject's knowledge of treatment assignment may lead to systematic over- or underestimation of the treatment effect, the magnitude of which is unknown. In addition, the Agency informed the Applicant that the 2-year difference between the AT-DR in the long-term extension study also posed challenges in interpretation of the PRO endpoint.

L1980: The following concerns limit the interpretability of the PRO data comparing the fitusiran AT-DR treatment with the control treatment:
L1981: ## Baseline Data
L1982: The AT-based dosing regimen does not have any true baseline values (i.e., the transition from fitusiran 80 mg to the AT-based dosing regimen consisted of a variable period that was individualized); as such, it is difficult to compare the data between control and the fitusiran
L1983: AT-based dose regimen from an efficacy standpoint. Further, there is no matching control for the AT-based dosed regimen in the primary efficacy period.
L1984: ## Open-Label Design
L1985: The interpretability of PRO data is limited because subjects' knowledge of treatment assignment may lead to systematic overestimation or underestimation of treatment effect, the magnitude of which is unknown. In settings where blinding is not feasible, or there is high likelihood of inadvertent unblinding due to toxicity, lack of blinding will need to be overcome by demonstrating a large and durable magnitude of effect in the setting of strict adherence to a carefully conducted clinical trial. Pool A failed to achieve statistical significance, and Pool B only achieved a marginally significant p-value for the superiority test comparing fitusiran AT-DR treatment with control treatment for the PRO endpoints. The effect sizes in Pools A and B may not be able to account for the bias introduced by the open-label design of the study for PRO endpoints.
L1986: ## Time Lag in the Pooled Analysis
L1987: In Pools A and B, the calculation of change from baseline in the fitusiran arm started from Day 1 of the parent studies with the original fitusiran 80 mg QM dose regimen treatment to the end of the primary efficacy period with the fitusiran AT-DR treatment, which involves the period of both the fitusiran 80 mg QM original dosing regimen treatment and the fitusiran AT-DR treatment. The time lag introduces further uncertainty on the estimated treatment effect for the PRO endpoints, with the much longer period of the subjects on fitusiran treatment (i.e., over 21 months, including at least 9 months on fitusiran 80 mg QM plus 12 months on fitusiran AT-DR compared to 9 months for the control treatment) and the higher rate of subject dropout due to the long treatment period for the fitusiran treatment. Please refer to Figure 57 below for the length of each period.
L1988: **Figure 57. Graphic Illustration of Treatment Periods for Subjects in Pool A and Pool B**
L1989: Source: DCOA Reviewer's sketch

L1996: In the primary analysis for the PRO endpoint change in Haem-A-QoL Physical Health domain score from baseline using the MMRM approach, no imputation of missing data in the control period was performed for subjects who withdrew in the control period; unobserved data for the primary efficacy period were imputed via multiple imputation method with missing at random (MAR) assumption based on observed data during the primary efficacy period. To evaluate the robustness of the primary analysis result against the approach to handle missing data, the FDA statistical review team requested several sensitivity analyses via information request dated November 22, 2024, for Pools A and B, including:
L1997: - The result of multiple imputation can vary due to reasons such as the selection of imputation method/model under MAR assumption and the selection of seed for the imputation. Furthermore, a multiple imputation for missing data under MAR assumption is not necessary for MMRM analysis since this model already imputes missing data under the MAR assumption. A sensitivity analysis using MMRM approach was conducted based only on the observed data without any imputation for the unobserved data for Pools A and B (Table 151).

None of the above sensitivity analyses for Pool B achieved the same conclusion (i.e., nominal p-value&lt;0.05) as the primary analysis results, indicating that the primary analysis result for Pool B is not robust against the approach to handle missing data. The p-value for the primary analysis should be interpreted with caution.

L2010: ## 17. Clinical Safety
L2011: ## 17.1. Safety Analysis of Pooled Trials EFC14768 and EFC14769
L2012: Trials EFC14768 and EFC14769 are open-label controlled trials that randomized subjects with hemophilia A and B with and without inhibitors, respectively, to receive fitusiran 80 mg SQ QM or on-demand therapy over a 9-month treatment period. High-level safety data are provided below. For dose-related adverse reactions, the safety profile of 80 mg QM could differ from that of the AT-DR, as the 80 mg QM regimen is a higher dose. However, these analyses are useful because they provide randomized, controlled comparisons and could potentially identify safety signals that were not seen in the uncontrolled long-term extension study.

L2033: ## Serious Adverse Events
L2034: Table 155 shows the SAEs by system organ class and PT. Only fitusiran-treated patients had SAEs reported in the hepatobiliary disorders SOC (PTs biliary colic, cholecystitis acute, cholecystitis chronic, cholecystitis and cholelithiasis), vascular SOC (PTs thrombosis and subclavian vein thrombosis), and spinal vascular disorder.
L2035: Overall, the most prevalent SAEs occurring only in the fitusiran arms in studies EFC14768 and EFC14769, were hepatobiliary disorders (biliary colic, cholecystitis acute, cholecystitis chronic). This mirrored the most prevalent SAEs associated with fitusiran use in the AT-DR open-label study, cholecystitis (Table 41, Section 7.5.2.1).

L2056: ## **Most Common TEAEs**
L2057: The most common TEAEs by FMQ are shown in Table 157.
L2058: The most common preferred term (PT), hepatic injury, occurred nearly exclusively in the fitusiran treatment arms compared to the on-demand arms, and in each study the risk difference 95% confidence interval did not cross zero. Cholecystitis and dyspepsia were prevalent PTs reported only in the fitusiran treatment arms. Abdominal pain was also mostly reported in the fitusiran treatment arms. Dyspepsia and abdominal pain will be included as clinically relevant adverse reactions.

L2083: Figure 58. Hepatocellular Drug-Induced Liver Injury Screening Plot, Safety Population, Pooled Trials EFC14768 and EFC14769
L2084: ## 17.2. Safety Analysis of Pooled Trials EFC14768 and EFC14769 On-Demand Controls and LTE15174 AT-DR Treatment Phase Subjects Originally Randomized to Fitusiran in Parent Study EFC14768 and EFC14769
L2085: To further assess potential treatment effects of fitusiran AT-DR, an exploratory analysis compared subjects receiving the AT-DR during LTE15174 (N=103; 35 inhibitor and 68 noninhibitor) with the subjects randomized to the on-demand treatment arm (N=59; 19 inhibitor and 40 noninhibitor) from studies EFC14768 and EFC14769. For this analysis, we only included AT-DR subjects who had originally been randomized to the fitusiran treatment arm in studies EFC14768 and EFC14769. An important limitation of this analysis is that patients on the AT-DR who tolerated the higher dose of fitusiran in the parent studies and then continued into the long-term extension study may not reflect what would occur in patients who are treatment-naïve to fitusiran AT-DR.
L2086: ## Exposure Data
L2087: Table 159 shows the duration of exposure to fitusiran AT-DR in Study LTE15174 and exposure to the on-demand treatment in EFC14768 and EFC14769. The mean duration of treatment of 107.2 weeks in the fitusiran AT-DR cohort is longer than the mean duration of 35.4 weeks for the on-demand cohort. In addition, as this analysis is adhering to the original randomization, there are more subjects in the fitusiran AT-DR cohort due to the original 2:1 randomization scheme.

L2100: Treatment-emergent adverse events defined as any AE with onset or ongoing after fitusiran dose restart and before the data cut off (June 14, 2023) for NDA for the fitusiran group. However, AEs were not considered treatment-emergent if they occurred during the follow-up after a subject's AT value was 60% or greater. For the on-demand group, AEs were defined as treatment-emergent if they occurred after the Day 1 visit during studies EFC14768 and EFC14769.
L2101: Duration is up to 128 weeks for the fitusiran group and 36 weeks for the on-demand group.
L2102: Events occurred during major surgery periods are excluded.
L2103: The revised regimen period is from dose restart Day 1 to data cut off for interim lock and follow-up AT back to 60% whichever comes first.
L2104: Person-years for the AT-DR group were calculated as the total duration starting from the first dose of AT-DR until the initial occurrence of the event for subjects who experienced an event, or the data cutoff date for subjects who did not. However, for subjects who discontinued earlier, the follow-up period was considered completed once the AT recovered to 60%. Person-years for the on-demand group were calculated as the total duration starting from the Day 1 visit until the initial occurrence of the event for subjects who experienced an event, or the last day in the study for subjects who did not.
L2105: Risk difference (with 95% confidence interval) is shown between total treatment and comparator.
L2106: Severity as assessed by the investigator.
L2107: Note that differences are sometimes observed between Table 3 and Table 5 because these tables are derived from different datasets and sometimes differ in definitions or how cases are counted.
L2108: Asterisk (*) indicates that 95% confidence interval excludes zero.
L2109: Abbreviations: AE, adverse event; AT, antithrombin; AT-DR, antithrombin-based dose regimen; CI, confidence interval; EAIR, exposure-adjusted incidence rate (per 100 person-years); N, number of subjects in treatment arm; n, number of subjects with at least one event; PY, person-years (total exposure); py, person-years (at risk); SAE, serious adverse event

L2126: ## **Most Common Adverse Events**
L2127: Table 162 shows the adverse events by SOC and FDA Medical Query. Many AEs were reported in only 1-2 subjects, limiting conclusions. More frequently reported AEs of cough, bronchospasm, and rash were reported almost exclusively with fitusiran treatment compared to on-demand treatment, similar to the distribution of these AEs in the randomized controlled trials. The PT viral infection was reported at a higher rate with fitusiran compared to on-demand treatment, like in the randomized controlled trials, although bacterial infection and nasopharyngitis rates were lower with fitusiran, in contrast to that seen in the randomized trials.

L2137: Treatment-emergent adverse events defined as any AE with onset or ongoing after fitusiran dose restart and before the data cut off (June 14, 2023) for NDA for the fitusiran group. However, AEs were not considered treatment-emergent if they occurred during the follow-up after a subject's AT value was 60% or greater. For the on-demand group, AEs were defined as treatment-emergent if they occurred after the Day 1 visit during studies EFC14768 and EFC14769.
L2138: Duration is up to 128 weeks for the fitusiran group and 36 weeks for the on-demand group.
L2139: Events occurred during major surgery periods are excluded.
L2140: The revised regimen period is from dose restart Day 1 to data cut off for interim lock and follow-up AT back to 60% whichever comes first.
L2141: Person-years for the AT-DR group were calculated as the total duration starting from the first dose of AT-DR until the initial occurrence of the event for subjects who experienced an event, or the data cutoff date for subjects who did not. However, for subjects who discontinued earlier, the follow-up period was considered completed once the AT recovered to 60%. Person-years for the on-demand group were calculated as the total duration starting from the Day 1 visit until the initial occurrence of the event for subjects who experienced an event, or the last day in the study for subjects who did not.
L2142: Risk difference (with 95% confidence interval) is shown between total treatment and comparator.
L2143: Each FMQ is aligned to a single SOC based on clinical judgment. However, please be aware that some FMQs may contain PTs from more than one SOC.
L2144: Some preferred terms are not included in any FDA medical query. Those preferred terms are not shown or counted in this table.
L2145: Asterisk (*) indicates that 95% confidence interval excludes zero.
L2146: Abbreviations: AE, adverse event; AT, antithrombin; AT-DR, antithrombin-based dose regimen; CI, confidence interval; EAIR, exposure-adjusted incidence rate (per 100 person-years); FMQ, FDA medical query; N, number of subjects in treatment arm; n, number of subjects with at least one event; PT, preferred term; PY, person-years (total exposure); py, person-years (at risk); SOC, system organ class; TIA, transient ischemic attack

L2161: ## 17.3. Thrombotic Event Narratives
L2162: Subject narratives for thrombotic events are summarized below.
L2163: ## EFC14768, Fitusiran 80 mg QM
L2164: - Subject (b) (6) was a 17-year-old with hemophilia B with inhibitors and a relevant history of pancreatic insufficiency, malabsorption syndrome, prior central catheter infections, and prior deep venous thrombosis of the right upper extremity, who presented on Day 63 with an SAE of **deep vein thrombosis, subclavian vein thrombosis and superficial thrombophlebitis** (PTs). The subject had a recent catheter-device-related infection that occurred on Day 57, which required hospitalization, removal of both an indwelling port and a right-sided PICC line, and extended intravenous antibiotic therapy. The thrombotic event occurred during the hospitalization for the catheter-device infection and extended the hospitalization. The thrombotic event was in the anatomical location of the recent PICC catheter, and imaging showed occlusive thrombus in the right brachial vein, chronic nonocclusive thrombus in the right subclavian vein, and right axillary occlusive thrombus. Fitusiran was continued and the subject completed the study treatment period, with the last dose of fitusiran 80 mg SC QM occurring on Day 239. The clinical review team considers that while there were many ongoing risk factors for DVT, fitusiran likely contributed to the risk of this event.
- Subject (b) (6) was a 19-year-old who experienced an SAE of **thrombosis (suspected spinal vessel thrombosis)** on Day 204. The subject had a history of hemophilia A with inhibitors. He presented with right hand and bilateral thigh numbness, which progressed to right hand weakness and bilateral leg weakness, requiring aids for ambulation. On Day 228, MRI of the lumbar spine showed nonspecific C6/C7 spinal cord hyperintensity with mild cord expansion, but did not show explicit evidence of hematoma, acute spinal infarct or spinal artery thrombosis, or spinal tumor. A whole-spine MRI showed a focal area of T2 hyperintensity within the spinal cord, centered at C7, consistent with an area of myelopathy. Differential diagnosis included cord ischemia or demyelination. An angiogram was not performed. The subject was treated with antithrombin concentrates on Days 255 and 256, and then on Days 302 to 306. Neurological findings improved gradually over 2 to 3 months following administration of antithrombin concentrates. Fitusiran was stopped, with last dose given (80 mg QM) on Day 204. The review team considers that the most consistent imaging findings were of spinal cord ischemia, and while the nature of the underlying spinal cord process (whether primary thrombotic, infectious or inflammatory) is unclear, vascular insufficiency appears to have played a role. Features of this case suggesting that fitusiran was related were that the symptoms resolved over time without other treatment than fitusiran withdrawal and as antithrombin levels recovered to the normal range, and that vascular insufficiency due to thrombosis is compatible with fitusiran's mechanism of action.
L2165: ## **EFC15110, Fitusiran Dose 80 mg QM**
L2166: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) was a 36-year-old male with a history of hemophilia A without inhibitors, prior deep vein thrombosis, obesity, hypertension, hyperlipidemia, tobacco use, and diabetes mellitus who experienced the SAE of **cerebrovascular accident**. On Day 160 of fitusiran dosing, the subject presented with left-sided paralysis, and imaging showed lesions in the right basal ganglia and corona radiata. A CT angiogram showed occlusion of the M1 division of the right middle cerebral artery. The last dose of fitusiran (80 mg QM) was given on Day 146, and fitusiran was discontinued. The clinical review team recognizes that the subject had many vascular risk factors, but fitusiran contribution is likely.
- Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) was a 56-year-old male with HA with inhibitors and a history of hypertension and renal calculi, who experienced the SAE of **thrombosis, papilla** of left eye on Day 257. He presented on Day 247 with 95% sudden loss of vision in his left eye, associated with fatigue, headache, and dizziness. MRI did not show vascular or other lesions. Carotid ultrasounds showed a calcified plaque at the right carotid bulb. Eight days later, the subject presented to a neurologist and exam confirmed severe loss of vision and papilledema of the left eye. Prednisolone was started. Three days later, cranial MR venography did not reveal cerebral sinus thrombosis. The neurologist concluded that the episode was due to capillary occlusion of the left papilla, on Day 257. The subject received the last dose of fitusiran on Day 170 (80 mg QM) and remains with vision loss. The review team considers that this acute painful visual loss associated with papilledema likely represents a microvascular arterial or venous thrombotic event, and given the mechanism of action, fitusiran likely contributed.
L2167: ## **LTE14762: Fitusiran Dose 80 mg QM**
L2168: Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) was a 25-year-old White male with hemophilia A without inhibitors who experienced cerebral venous sinus thrombosis that resulted in a fatal outcome (originally reported as PT subarachnoid hemorrhage). The subject narrative can be found in Section 7.5.2.2.
L2169: ## **LTE15174: Fitusiran dose 80 mg QM**
L2170: Subject LTE15174 [REDACTED](b) (6) was a 62-year-old with hemophilia A without inhibitors with a past medical history of hepatitis C, prostate cancer post radical prostatectomy 3 years earlier, HIV infection, and bronchiectasis, who experienced a cerebral artery embolism on Day 436. The subject presented with aphasia. MRI showed numerous small infarcts involving the left middle cerebral artery territory with absent flow in the M2 branch. Fitusiran was discontinued. The subject improved but did not fully recover neurologic deficits. The review team considers that while the subject had cardiovascular risk factors of HIV, prior cancer, and bronchiectasis, fitusiran, due to its mechanism of action, is a contributing factor to the cerebral artery aneurysm.
L2171: ## **LTE15174: Fitusiran Dose 50 mg Q2M**
L2172: - Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) was a 13-year-old male with hemophilia B with inhibitors who experienced a serious TEAE of **venous thrombosis**. Of note, per-protocol TEAEs that occurred during the perioperative period of a major surgery were not considered SAEs. The subject had a prior history of deep venous thrombosis of the vena cava and subclavian vein thrombosis, thrombectomy, and venous angioplasty. On Day 687, he presented with pain in the left upper extremity, and ultrasound of the extremity revealed venous thrombosis of the left axillary, brachial and basilic veins. The event of venous thrombosis was considered serious because it required another admission to the hospital. Of note, the subject had recently undergone right inguinal hernia repair (Day 682), which was complicated by an incision site hematoma that required incision and drainage and drain placement. The subject was treated with argatroban and by Day 690 recovered from both the venous thrombosis and incision hematoma revision and was discharged. The subject continued to receive fitusiran. The clinical review team's assessment is that while the subject was at increased risk of venous thrombosis due to recent hernia surgery with incisional hematoma and revision, and prior venous thrombotic events in the same anatomic region, fitusiran likely increased venous thrombosis risk.
- Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) was a 40-year-old male with HA who permanently discontinued fitusiran due to the AE of **deep vein thrombosis postoperative**. See subject narrative in Section 7.6.1.
- Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) permanently discontinued after the AE of **cerebral infarction**. See subject narrative in Section 7.6.1
- Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) was a 54-year-old male with hemophilia who experienced an SAE of **embolic stroke**. The subject narrative can be found in Section 7.6.1.
L2173: ## 17.4. Acute Gallbladder Events
L2174: Table 164 shows the demographic features, fitusiran dosing, and gallbladder AEs in the 12 subjects who underwent cholecystectomy in the fitusiran development program. Most cholecystectomies were performed while subjects were receiving non-AT-DR dose regimens, although one subject had transitioned to AT-DR dosing 589 days before cholecystectomy, and another developed cholangitis and pancreatitis while on AT-DR dosing 889 days after cholecystectomy.

L2192: ## 22. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L2193: A clinical inspection summary was completed by the Division of Clinical Compliance Evaluation in the Office of Scientific Investigations with a final signature date of November 11, 2024. Two clinical investigators, Drs. Savita Rangarajan (India) and Chur-Woo You (South Korea), were inspected.

L2195: ## 23. Labeling: Key Changes
L2196: This Prescribing Information (PI) review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant's draft PI (Table 165). The PI was reviewed to ensure that it meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if appropriate) with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare practitioner.
L2197: **Table 165. Key Labeling Changes and Considerations**
L2198: Full PI Sections1	Rationale for Major Changes to Finalized PI2 Compared to Applicant's Draft PI
BOXED WARNING	
Added Boxed Warning to describe the risks of Thrombotic Events and Acute and Recurrent Gallbladder Disease. These risks are considered crucial for prescribing decisions. Patients developed thrombosis who did not follow bleeding management guidelines on the prescribed dose, so it is important for providers to be aware of this prior to prescribing. In addition, one patient on the 80 mg dose died due to thrombosis.
For acute gallbladder disease, a significant proportion of patients developed cholelithiasis/cholecystitis resulting in surgery, and one patient had a complication of pancreatitis. It is important for providers to be aware of this risk prior to prescribing the drug to ensure prompt diagnosis/intervention. In addition, patients with a history of symptomatic gallbladder disease may want to consider alternative therapies.

1 INDICATIONS AND USAGE	Revised “adolescent” to “pediatric” patients and specified the ages of 12 years and over for clarity and per the Pediatric Use guidance.
2 DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION	
2.1 Added AT activity monitoring recommendations as determined by the FDA-cleared text. Trough AT levels should be monitored at Weeks 4, 12, and 20, which coincide with trough AT levels.
2.2 Revised Table 1 by adding a column for “dosage modification” to reduce the risk of dosing errors. Added Missed Dose instructions.
2.3 Added new subsection to describe Bleed Management. Added “use during surgical interventions.”

4 CONTRAINDICATIONS	n/a
5 WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS	
5.1 Thrombotic Events-Revised to include situations that increase the risk of these events and to clarify mitigating strategies.
5.2. Added a new W&P for Acute and Recurrent Gallbladder Disease (to 5.2 as it's in the Boxed Warning) to describe this serious risk, monitoring recommendations, and mitigation strategies.
5.3 Renamed [REDACTED] (b) (4) to “hepatotoxicity” to more accurately describe the risk. Revised the risk in consultation with the DILI team. Mitigating strategies added. Revised [REDACTED] (b) (4) and replaced with “avoid use in patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment.”
Deleted [REDACTED] (b) (4) as none were reported and no basis was provided.

L2276: NONCLINICAL SUMMARY	3
8 CARCINOGENICITY	4

L2277: ## Nonclinical Summary
L2278: Fitusiran (AD-57213 or ALN-AT3SC) is a chemically synthesized double strand small interfering RNA (siRNA) covalently bound to a triantennary Nacetylgalactosamine ligand (GalNAc) that specifically targets AT messenger RNA to lower production of AT in the liver. Targeting AT mRNA, fitusiran enhances thrombin generation potential and rebalance hemostasis in patients with hemophilia.
L2279: The Sponsor submitted final reports for carcinogenicity studies of fitusiran
L2280: - A 26-week study in TgRasH2 mouse study-at3-glp17-001.pdf,
- A 2-year study in rats study-car0186.pdf.
L2281: The mouse and rat tumor data were analyzed by FDA statistician Dr. Hepei Chen
Statistical Review IND 125632 NDA 219019.
L2282: In both studies, fitusiran was administered by subcutaneous injection once weekly. In the mouse study, TGRasH2 mice (n=25/sex/group) were administered fitusiran doses of 0 (0.9 % sodium chloride), 0.05, 0.15, or 0.5 mg/kg/week in males and 0 (0.9 % sodium chloride), 0.025, 0.08, 0.25 mg/kg/week in females. In addition, a positive control group of male and female TgRasH2 mice received a single, intraperitoneal dose of 75 mg/kg N-methyl-N-nitrosourea (MNU) on Day 1. In the rat study, Sprague-Dawley rats were administered fitusiran doses of 0 (0.9% Sodium Chloride), 0.03, 0.1 or 0.3 mg/kg/week in males, and 0 (0.9% Sodium Chloride), 0.1, 0.3 or 1 mg/kg/week in females.
L2283: The percent survival in fitusiran treated male and female mice was &gt;96 and 100%, respectively; survival for the mouse vehicle control groups was 96%. Fitusiran was not carcinogenic in mice based on dose response-wise or pairwise comparison analysis conducted by both the Sponsor and the FDA statistical analysis. The positive control group showed statistically significant increased mortality and increases in malignant tumors compared to the vehicle control group. On Day 183, the Cmax values at 0.5 mg/kg/week and 0.25 mg/kg/week were 24.3 ng/mL and 16.8 ng/mL, respectively, and AUC0-t values were 70.9 ng·h/mL and 28.7 ng·h/mL, respectively. The mouse to human exposure multiples is &lt;1-fold the MRHD based on AUC.
L2284: The percent survival in fitusiran treated male and female rats was &gt;25% and &gt;35%, respectively; survival for the vehicle control male and female rats was 33%, respectively. Fitusiran was not carcinogenic in rats based on dose response-wise or pairwise comparison analysis conducted by both the Sponsor and the FDA statistical analysis. On Day 176, in males, the Cmax and AUC0-t values at 0.3 mg/kg/week were 91.1 ng/mL and 163 ng/mL, respectively. In females, the Cmax and AUC0-t values at 1 mg/kg/week were 190 ng/mL and 372 ng·h/mL, respectively. The rat to human exposure multiples is 1-2-fold the MRHD based on AUC.

L2300: ## Tumor Findings:
L2301: - There were no fitusiran-related statistically significant increase in the incidence of any neoplasm in RasH2 mice.
- There was no statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in mortality in male or female RasH2 mice.
- The mouse to human exposure multiples is &lt;1-fold (adjusted to bimonthly dosing) at the MRHD based on AUC.
- Positive control, N-Methyl-N-Nitrosourea (MNU) significantly increased incidences of malignant squamous cell carcinoma and papilloma of the stomach in male and female RasH2 mice relative to control. There was a statistically significant higher mortality rate (15/25 males, 17/25 females) for positive control related to tumor findings.

L2331: ## Histopathology
L2332: Peer Review Conducted: Yes (b) (4)
Historical Control Provided for Tumor Incidence: Yes
L2333: The percentage of carcinoma + adenomas in the lungs lacked dose-response relationship with administration of fitusiran. These tumors are considered spontaneous
L2334: and/or incidental with low incidence rate and/or consistent with the tumor profile known for RasH2 Tg/WT mice of this age (Paranjpe et al 2013; Paranjpe et al 2019; Nambiar et al 2012). On the contrary, splenic hemangiosarcomas were observed only in fitusiran-treated mice and the incidence slightly exceeds the maximum range for this tumor type in the historical control data of the Test Facility. Based on the absence of a dose-relationship, these tumors were considered incidental in origin. No statistically significant dose-response relationship and pairwise comparisons in tumor data was noted in the FDA statistical analysis for both male and female mice.

L2349: ## Toxicokinetics
L2350: AUC values were not determined for LD and MD males and LD and HD females on Day 1 and LD males and LD and HD in females on Day 183 because of insufficient measurable blood quantities. As a result, TK interpretation is only being performed on Day 183 results. Exposure on Day 183, as assessed by fitusiran Cmax values, the increases were generally greater than dose proportional from MD to HD, and in females, they were generally less than dose proportional from LD to MD and greater than dose proportional from MD to HD.

Dosing Solution Analysis:	Formulation analysis showed concentration verification values were within the specified range except some concentrations on Week 1 and 5 (0.015 mg/mL), 34 (0.015, 0.05, 0.15, 0.5 mg/mL), 66 (0.5 mg/mL) and 98 (0.05 and 0.15 mg/mL). These discrepancies were not considered to impact the study outcome.
L2364: ## **Observations and Results**
L2365: ## **Mortality**
L2366: There were several unscheduled deaths in males and females at all doses including control groups. There was no statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality compared to control groups. Two deaths, one male at MD (0.1 mg/kg/week) and one female at HD (1 mg/kg/week) were related to fitusiran based on necrosis in the brain, associated with the presence of thrombi. Although the frequency is within or slightly higher than the laboratory historical control data, based on the mechanism of action, the deaths are considered related to fitusiran. FDA statistical analysis of survival rate concurred that there was no statistically significant dose-response relationship or pairwise comparisons in mortality for both male and female rats.
L2367: **Table 7: Summary of Unscheduled Deaths in Rats**
L2368: Control (Vehicle)	LD	MD	HD
Male	40 (67%)	45 (75%)	45 (75%)	42 (70%)

L2369: LD, MD, and HD = fitusiran 0.03, 0.1, 0.3 mg/kg/week to males and 0.1, 0.3, 1 mg/kg/week to females, respectively
L2370: Due to increased mortality in males receiving 0.03 and 0.1 mg/kg/week all surviving male animals in these dose groups were euthanized during Week 94 and Week 98, respectively, of the study. Due to increased mortality in male and female rats receiving vehicle control (Group 1), all surviving female rats and males receiving 0.3 mg/kg/week were euthanized during Week 93 and Weeks 98/99, respectively of the study. Early termination of all groups in this study was considered to have no impact on the assessment of carcinogenic potential because the number of animals evaluated, and the study duration were consistent with FDA guidances.
L2371: Source: Reviewer's analysis of the data submitted to the study report Car0186
L2372: The neoplasms observed in unscheduled deaths included mainly pituitary adenoma of the pars distalis in males and females and neoplasms of the mammary gland in females across all groups, commonly encountered neoplasms in rats of this strain and age range. There were no other neoplasms observed unique to fitusiran at any dose group.

L2396: ## Toxicokinetics
L2397: The lower limit of quantitation for concentration analysis was &lt;4 ng/mL as a result concentration analysis from males administered 0.03 mg/kg/week was not performed.
