L2420: ## 1. Background
L2421: In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats and one in mice. These studies were to determine the carcinogenic potential of ALN-AT3SC, when given by subcutaneous injection once weekly for 104 consecutive weeks (total of a 104 doses) to rats, and for 26 weeks (total of 27 doses) to the TgRasH2 hemizygous mouse.
L2422: In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component (trend) of the effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as dose increases.
L2423: ## 2. Rat Study
L2424: Two separate experiments, one in male rats and one in female rats were conducted. As indicated in Table 3, in the experiment there were three treated groups, and one vehicle control group. Two hundred and forty rats of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 60 rats per group. ALN-AT3SC was administered weekly by subcutaneous injection at doses of 0.03, 0.1 or 0.3 mg/kg/week to male rats and 0.1, 0.3 or 1 mg/kg/week to female rats for a total of 104 doses. In this review the three treated groups were referred to as the low, mid, and high dose groups (Group 2, 3, 4 for male rats, and Group 5, 6, and 7 for female rats), respectively. The rats in the vehicle control group (Group 1) were administrated with 0.9% Sodium Chloride for injection, USP, and handled for the same duration and in the same manner as the treated groups.
L2425: **Table 1: Experimental Design in Rat Study**
L2426: Group No.	No. of Toxicity Animals	Test Material	Dosage Level (mg/kg/week)
Male	Female	Male	Female	Male	Female
1	1	60	60	Vehicle control	0	0
2	5	60	60	ALN-AT3SC Low	0.03	0.1
3	6	60	60	ALN-AT3SC Mid	0.1	0.3
4	7	60	60	ALN-AT3SC High	0.3	1
L2427: Due to the number of survivors, female rats of all groups (Group 1, 5, 6, and 7) and male rats of the low dose group (Group 2) were terminated on week 93/94; male rats of the vehicle control group, and the mid and high dose groups (Group 1, 3 and 4) were terminated on week 98/99.
L2428: Evaluations for potential ALN-AT3SC-related effects were based on survival, clinical signs (including presence of palpable masses), body weights, food consumption, hematology parameters, toxicokinetic parameters, macroscopic observations at necropsy, and microscopic examinations. A complete gross pathological examination was performed on all animals, as specified in the Study Plan.
L2429: ## 2.1. Sponsor's analyses
L2430: ## 2.1.1. Survival analysis
L2431: In the sponsor's report, the statistical analyses for mortality Main Study data were performed separately for each sex. The survival function of each group was estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product-limit method applied on daily intervals and graphs were produced. Any animal with accidental injury that caused its death or its unscheduled sacrifice, or still alive at the day following the last day of the experimental period were censored in the estimation. The log-rank test was applied to the four groups in order to assess the significance of the overall group effect on mortality data. If this test was found to be significant, then, using dose level scores, the significance of an overall dose-related trend in mortality and pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control group (Group 1) and each of the test item-treated groups (Groups 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7) were evaluated via a Peto's two-sided test (Peto et al., 1980). All statistical tests were conducted at the 5% significance level.
L2432: ## Sponsor's findings:
L2433: The sponsor's analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 20 (33%), 15 (25%), 15 (25%), and 18 (30%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and high dose groups for male rats, respectively, and 20 (33%), 27 (45%), 21 (35%), and 26 (43%) for female rats, respectively. There was no ALN-AT3SC-related increase in the incidence of mortalities and no statistically significant changes in mortality.
L2434: ## 2.1.2. Tumor data analysis
L2435: In the sponsor's analysis, the statistical evaluation of tumor data was done separately for each sex and was limited to subcutis and hemolymphoreticular tissue using all study animals, to all non-secondary neoplastic lesions found in Study Plan-required tissues/sites, and to the combination of hemangiosarcoma findings across whole body. Statistical analyses of histopathological data were conducted according to the requirements specified in the study plan/study plan amendment. Palpable neoplastic lesions found under skin, subcutis, and under Study Plan-required glands were statistically analyzed in a "mortality independent" context according to Peto's onset rate using all study animals. Whereas, non-palpable neoplastic, incidental and fatal findings were statistically analyzed in a "mortality dependent" context according to Peto's prevalence and death rate methods, respectively. For each dataset of interest, Peto's test, using integer dose level scores, was conducted in order to assess significance of tumor rate increase across all groups and in each of Groups 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 when compared to Group 1.
L2436: These multicentric neoplasms were shown as primary tumor only once per animal under the tissue "hemolymphoreticular tissue" in order to present true incidence of the given multicentric neoplasm for each affected animal. The presence of multiple benign tumors of the same type (e.g. several hepatocellular adenomas of the liver located in different lobes) was recorded only once, and the individual locations correlated with gross observations in the individual animal data record.
L2437: As per Lin (Lin, 1997), the discrete permutation distribution was used for each statistical test involving a dataset with 10 or less tumor occurrences. All tests were performed at the 5% significance level and, as recommended by the FDA (Guidance for Industry, 2001), are discussed
L2438: in the Anatomic Pathology Report according to the tumor prevalence classification (common or rare).
L2439: ## Adjustment for multiple testing:
L2440: In the sponsor's report, no specific adjustment for multiple testing was discussed. A tumor type was considered rare if the spontaneous rate was 1% or less based on concurrent control data and historical control data from this facility or reported in the literature, otherwise a tumor type was classified as a common tumor.
L2441: ## Sponsor's findings:
L2442: In the sponsor's report, There were no ALN-AT3SC-related neoplastic microscopic findings and no statistical significance for palpable and non-palpable neoplasms.
L2443: ## 2.2. Reviewer's analyses
L2444: To verify the sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing toxicologist, this reviewer independently performed the survival and tumor data analyses using the data provided by the sponsor electronically.
L2445: ## 2.2.1. Survival analysis
L2446: In the reviewer's analysis, the survival distributions of rats in all four groups (Groups 1, 2, 3, 4 for male rats, and 1, 5, 6, 7 for female rats) were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The dose response relationship was tested across Groups 1, 2, 3, 4 for male rats, and 1, 5, 6, 7 for female rats using the likelihood ratio test, and the homogeneity of survival distributions was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are given in Figures 1A and 1B in the appendix for all four groups in male and female rats, respectively. The intercurrent mortality data of all four groups and the results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals for Groups 1, 2, 3, 4 for male rats, and 1, 5, 6, 7 for female rats are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L2447: ## Reviewer's findings:
L2448: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 20 (33%), 15 (25%), 15 (25%), and 18 (30%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and high dose groups for male rats, respectively, and 20 (33%), 27 (45%), 21 (35%), and 26 (43%) for female rats respectively. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in mortality was noted for both male and female rats.
L2449: ## 2.2.2. Tumor data analysis
L2450: The tumor data were analyzed for dose response relationships across the vehicle control group, and low, mid, and high dose groups, and pairwise comparisons of each of the three treated groups against the vehicle control group, using the Poly-k method described in the paper of Bailer and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams (1993).
L2451: In the poly-k method, the adjustment for differences in mortality among treatment groups is made by modifying the number of animals at risk in the denominators in the calculations of overall tumor rates in the Cochran-Armitage test to reflect less-than-whole-animal contributions for animals that die without tumor before the end of the study (Bailer and Portier 1988). The modification is made by defining a new number of animals at risk for each treatment group. The number of animals at risk for the i-th treatment group R^* is defined as R^* = \sum W_{ij} where w_{ij} is the weight for the j-th animal in the i-th treatment group, and the sum is over all animals in the group.
L2452: Bailer and Portier (1988) proposed the weight w_{ij} as follows:
L2453: w_{ij} = 1 to animals dying with the tumor, and
L2454: w_{ij} = (t_{ij} / t_{sacr})^3 to animals dying without the tumor,
L2455: where t_{ij} is the time of death of the j-th animal in the i-th treatment group, and t_{sacr} is the planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice. The above formulas imply that animals living up to the end of the planned terminal sacrifice date without developing any tumor will also be assigned w_{ij} = 1 since t_{ij} = t_{sacr}. Also animals developed the tumor type being tested before the end of the study will be assigned as w_{ij} = 1.
L2456: Certain treatment groups of a study or the entire study may be terminated earlier than the planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice due to excessive mortalities. However, based on the principle of the Intention-to-treat (ITT) analysis in randomized trials, the t_{sacr} should not be affected by the unplanned early terminations. The t_{sacr} should always be equal to the planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice. For those animals that were sacrificed later than t_{sacr}, regardless their actual terminal sacrifice time, t_{sacr} was used as their time of terminal sacrifice in the analysis.
L2457: One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104 week standard rat and mouse studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data.
L2458: ## Multiple testing adjustment:
L2459: For a submission with one two-year study in one species and one short-term study with another species, in order to keep the overall false-positive rate at the nominal level of approximately 10%, as indicated in Table 2, this reviewer used the methodologies suggested in the Expanded Statistical Decision Rules for Interpretations of Results of Rodent Carcinogenicity Studies of Pharmaceuticals by Lin and Rahman (2018).
L2460: A rare tumor is defined as one in which the published spontaneous tumor rate is less than 1%. However, if the background information for the common or rare tumor is not available, the number of animals bearing tumors in the vehicle control group in the present study was used to determine the common or rare tumor status in the review report. That is, if the number of animals bearing tumors in the vehicle control group is 0, then this tumor is considered as the rare tumor; otherwise, if the number of animals bearing tumors in the control group is greater than or equal to 1, then this
L2461: tumor is considered as the common tumor.
L2462: **Table 2. Recommended decision rules for controlling the overall false positive rates**
L2463: Tests for positive trend alone	Control-high pairwise comparison alone	Tests for positive trend and control-high pairwise comparison jointly
Alternative ICH studies (one two-year study in one species and one short- or medium-term alternative study, two sexes)	Common and rare tumors are tested at 0.005 and 0.025 significance levels, respectively, in the two-year study, and at 0.05 and 0.05 significance levels, respectively, in the alternative study	Common and rare tumors are tested at 0.01 and 0.05 significance levels, respectively, in the two-year study, and at 0.05 and 0.05 significance levels, respectively, in the alternative study	Common and rare tumors are tested at 0.005 and 0.025 significance levels, respectively, in trend test, and at 0.05 and 0.10 significance levels, respectively, in control-high pairwise comparison in the two-year study, and at 0.05 and 0.05 significance levels, respectively, in both trend test and control-high pairwise comparison in the alternative study
L2464: ## Reviewer's findings:
L2465: The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 2A and 2B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons were noted in tumor data for male and female rats.
L2466: ## 3. Mouse Study
L2467: Two separate experiments, one in male mice and one in female mice were conducted. As indicated in Table 3, in the experiment there were three treated groups, one vehicle control group, and one positive control group. One hundred and twenty-five TgRasH2 (CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic) mice of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 25 mice per group. ALN-AT3SC was administered weekly by subcutaneous injection at doses of 0.05, 0.15, or 0.5 mg/kg/week to male rats, and 0.25, 0.08, 0.25 mg/kg/week to female rats for a total of 27 doses. In this review the three treated groups were referred to as the low, mid, and high dose groups (Group 2, 3, 4 for male rats, and Group 5, 6, and 7 for female rats), respectively. The mice in the vehicle control group (Group 1) were administrated once weekly by subcutaneous injection at 0.9% sodium chloride for injection, and handled for the same duration and in the same manner as the treated groups. The positive control group (Group 8) received a single intraperitoneal dose of MNU (N-Methyl-N-Nitrosurea) on Day 1 at 75 mg/kg.
L2468: **Table 3: Experimental Design in Mouse Study**
L2469: Group No.	No. of Toxicity Animals	Test Material	Dosage Level (mg/kg/week)
Male	Female	Male	Female	Male	Female
1	1	25	25	Vehicle control	0	0
2	5	25	25	ALN-AT3SC Low	0.05	0.025
3	6	25	25	ALN-AT3SC Mid	0.15	0.08
4	7	25	25	ALN-AT3SC High	0.5	0.25
8	8	25	25	Positive control (MNU)	75 mg/kg	75 mg/kg
L2470: The same clinical examinations, laboratory investigations and pathology procedures used in the rat study were performed in the mouse study.
L2471: ## 3.1. Sponsor's analyses
L2472: Because the mouse study was conducted by the same testing facility as the rat study, the sponsor used the same methodologies that were used for the analyses of the rat survival and tumor data.
L2473: ## 3.1.1. Survival analysis
L2474: ## Sponsor's findings:
L2475: The sponsor's analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy were 24 (96%), 25 (100%), 24 (96%), 24 (96%), and 10 (40%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and high dose groups, and the positive control group for male mice, respectively, and 24 (96%), 25 (100%), 25 (100%), 25 (100%), and 8 (32%) for female mice respectively. For both male and female mice, there were no statistically significant differences in the incidence of mortality amongst the groups.
L2476: ## 3.1.2. Tumor data analysis
L2477: ## Multiple testing adjustment:
L2478: The same multiple testing adjustment used in the rat study was used in the mouse study.
L2479: ## Sponsor's findings:
L2480: In the sponsor's report, for both male and female mice, the pairwise comparisons between the positive control group and the vehicle control group showed p-values less than 0.05 for many tumors, confirming animal expressed oncogenes and responded to carcinogenic insult as expected. No other statistically significant findings were noted in tumor data for male and female mice.
L2481: ## 3.2. Reviewer's analyses
L2482: Similar to the rat study, this reviewer independently performed survival and tumor data analyses of mouse data to verify sponsor's analyses. Data used in this reviewer's analyses were provided by the sponsor electronically.
L2483: For the analysis of both the survival data and the tumor data in mice, this reviewer used similar methodologies that were used for the analyses of the rat survival and tumor data.
L2484: ## 3.2.1. Survival analysis
L2485: ## Reviewer's findings:
L2486: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy were 24 (96%), 25 (100%), 24 (96%), 24 (96%), and 10 (40%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and high dose groups, and the positive control group for male mice, respectively, and 24 (96%), 25 (100%), 25 (100%), 25 (100%), and 8 (32%) for female mice respectively. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons was noted in mortality for both male and female mice.
L2487: ## 3.2.2. Tumor data analysis
L2488: ## Reviewer's findings:
L2489: The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 4A and 4B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively. For both male and female mice, the pairwise comparisons between the positive control group and the vehicle control group showed p-values less than 0.05 for many tumors, confirming animal expressed oncogenes and responded to carcinogenic insult as expected. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in tumor data was noted in the reviewer's analysis for both male and female mice.
L2490: ## 4. Summary
L2491: In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats and one in mice. These studies were to determine the carcinogenic potential of ALN-AT3SC, when given by subcutaneous injection once weekly for 104 consecutive weeks (total of a 104 doses) to rats, and for 26 weeks (total of 27 doses) to the TgRasH2 hemizygous mouse.
L2492: ## Rat Study:
L2493: Two separate experiments, one in male rats and one in female rats were conducted. In each of the experiments, there were three treated groups, and one vehicle control group. Two hundred and forty rats of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 60 rats per group. ALN-AT3SC was administered weekly by subcutaneous injection at doses of 0 (vehicle control), 0.03, 0.1 or 0.3 mg/kg/week to male rats and 0 (vehicle control), 0.1, 0.3 or 1 mg/kg/week to female rats, respectively. The rats in the vehicle control group (Group 1) were administrated with 0.9%
L2494: Sodium Chloride for injection, USP, and handled for the same duration and in the same manner as the treated groups.
L2495: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 20 (33%), 15 (25%), 15 (25%), and 18 (30%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and high dose groups for male rats, respectively, and 20 (33%), 27 (45%), 21 (35%), and 26 (43%) for female rats respectively. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in mortality was noted for both male and female rats.
L2496: In the reviewer's analysis, no statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons were noted in tumor data for male and female rats.
L2497: ## Mouse Study:
L2498: Two separate experiments, one in male mice and one in female mice were conducted. In each of the experiments there were three treated groups, one vehicle control group, and one positive control group. One hundred and twenty-five TgRasH2 (CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic) mice of each sex were assigned randomly in size of 25 mice per group. ALN-AT3SC was administered weekly by subcutaneous injection at doses of 0.05, 0.15, or 0.5 mg/kg/week to male rats, and 0.025, 0.08, 0.25 mg/kg/week to female rats for a total of 27 doses. The mice in the vehicle control group (Group 1) were administered once weekly by subcutaneous injection at 0.9% sodium chloride for injection, and handled for the same duration and in the same manner as the treated groups. The positive control group (Group 8) received a single intraperitoneal dose of MNU (N-Methyl-N-Nitrosurea) on Day 1 at 75 mg/kg.
L2499: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy were 24 (96%), 25 (100%), 24 (96%), 24 (96%), and 10 (40%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and high dose groups, and the positive control group for male mice, respectively, and 24 (96%), 25 (100%), 25 (100%), 25 (100%), and 8 (32%) for female mice respectively. No statistically significant finding was noted in mortality for both male and female mice.
L2500: In the reviewer's analysis, for both male and female mice, the pairwise comparisons between the positive control group and the vehicle control group showed p-values less than 0.05 for many tumors, confirming animal expressed oncogenes and responded to carcinogenic insult as expected. No statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in tumor data was noted in the reviewer's analysis for both male and female mice.

L2571: Diagram illustrating the mechanism of action of Fitusiran. The diagram shows the coagulation cascade pathways. The Intrinsic system (Hemophilia A) involves Factor VIII (FVIII) converting to FVIIIa, which activates Factor X (FX) to FXa. The Extrinsic system involves Factor VII (FVII) converting to FVIIa, which activates Factor X (FX) to FXa. Factor X (FX) is also activated by Factor VIIa (FVIIa) and Factor Va (FVa). FXa converts Prothrombin to Thrombin. Thrombin converts Fibrinogen to Fibrin, leading to a Blood clot. Fitusiran is shown inhibiting the activation of Factor X (FX) to FXa.
L2572: Bar chart showing Mean (±SEM) AT Activity (%) Relative to Baseline by Fitusiran Dose Group (TDR14767). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 100. The X-axis shows various dose groups: SAD 0.03 mg/kg (N=3), MAD 0.015 mg/kg (N=3), MAD 0.045 mg/kg (N=6), MAD 0.075 mg/kg (N=3), MD 0.225 mg/kg (N=3), MD 0.45 mg/kg (N=3), MD 0.9 mg/kg (N=3), MD 1.8 mg/kg (N=3), MD 80 mg (N=6), MDI 50 mg (N=6), and MDI 80 mg (N=11). AT activity decreases as the dose increases, starting around 80% for SAD and MAD 0.015 mg/kg, dropping to around 45% for MAD 0.045 mg/kg, and further decreasing to around 15% for MDI 80 mg.
L2573: Line graph showing the Median (90% Prediction Interval) Dose-AT Activity Relationship for Fitusiran. The Y-axis is Predicted Steady-State AT Activity (%), ranging from 0 to 100. The X-axis is Monthly Dose (mg), ranging from 0 to 140. A solid blue line represents the Median AT activity response, and a shaded blue area represents the 90% prediction interval. Red symbols represent mean steady-state AT activity observations calculated using data points between day 29 after first dose and day 57 after last dose for each individual participant who was part of the long term extension study, LTE14762. AT activity decreases sharply as the dose increases, stabilizing around 15% for doses greater than 80 mg.
L2574: Bar chart showing Mean (±SEM) Annualized Bleed Rate (ABR) by AT Quartile in Phase 1 Study TDR14767. The Y-axis is ABR Estimate, Mean (± SEM), ranging from 0 to 45. The X-axis shows AT Lowering quartiles: <25%, 25-<50%, 50-<75%, and >=75%. The ABR is highest for AT Lowering <25% (approx. 30.5) and decreases significantly for higher AT Lowering quartiles (approx. 14.5 for 25-<50%, 12.5 for 50-<75%, and 7.5 for >=75%).
L2575: Flowchart illustrating the Final Revised AT-Based Fitusiran Dose Regimens (LTE15174).
Initial Monitoring and Escalation:
Monthly AT monitoring after any dose change (SS = steady state).
If Patients with 2 SS AT levels >35\%, Dose escalate to dose of 80 mg QM.
If Patients with 2 SS AT levels >35\%, Change patients* to dose of 50 mg Q2M*.
If Patients with more than 1 AT level <15\%, Discontinue fitusiran.
Cohort for patients needing doses <50 mg Q2M:
Restart patients at 20mg Q2M*.
If Patients with 2 SS AT levels >35\%, Dose escalate to dose of 20mg QM.
If Patients with more than 1 AT level <15\%, Discontinue fitusiran.
*Start of dosing after descalation from higher dose to occur only after centrally measured AT activity level \ge 2\%.

L2579: Figure 9 is a flowchart illustrating the Study Design, EFC14768. The study begins with a Screening phase (Day -60 to -1), where subjects are assessed for Inhibitor HA/HB On-Demand BPA use. Subjects are then randomized (R) into two arms.

Forest Plot visualization showing Rate Ratios (RR) and 95% Confidence Intervals (CI) for various subgroups. The horizontal line at RR=1 represents no difference. The plot shows that the overall RR is 0.182 (0.084, 0.393), indicating a significant reduction in bleeding rate for the treated group compared to the control group. Subgroups like Type A, <=10 bleeding events, and Non-US region show RR values below 0.20, suggesting a strong protective effect of fitusiran prophylaxis in these groups. Subgroups with small sample sizes (e.g., <18 age group, North America region) show wide CIs, indicating high uncertainty in the estimates.

L2583: Figure 13 illustrates the subject flow and fitusiran regimen across clinical studies, divided into Parent studies, Roll-over studies, and a Pooled safety population.
Parent studies
EFC14768 (Phase 3 RCT, Hem A & B, with inhibitor):
Enrolled (N=60) → Randomized (N=57) → Received treatment (N=60).
Received treatment (N=60) → Rolled over to LTE15174 (N=57).
From Randomized (N=57): Fitu 80mg (N=38) and Control (N=19).
From Received treatment (N=60): Fitu 80mg (N=41) and Control (N=19).
From Rolled over to LTE15174 (N=57): Fitu 80mg (N=27) and Control (N=14).
From Fitu 80mg (N=27): AT-DR (N=25) and AT-DR (N=13).
From Control (N=14): AT-DR (N=13) and AT-DR (N=3).
EFC14769 (Phase 3 RCT, Hem A & B, without inhibitor):
Enrolled (N=120) → Randomized (N=120) → Received treatment (N=119).
From Randomized (N=120): Fitu 80mg (N=80) and Control (N=40).
From Received treatment (N=119): Fitu 80mg (N=79) and Control (N=40).
From Fitu 80mg (N=79): Rolled over to LTE15174 (N=62) and Fitu 80mg (N=27).
From Control (N=40): Rolled over to LTE15174 (N=39) and Control (N=1).
From Rolled over to LTE15174 (N=101): Fitu 80mg (N=62) and Control (N=39).
From Fitu 80mg (N=27): AT-DR (N=27).
From Control (N=1): AT-DR (N=6).
From Fitu 80mg (N=62): AT-DR (N=54) and AT-DR (N=14).
From Control (N=39): AT-DR (N=14) and AT-DR (N=25).
EFC15110 (Phase 3, Switching study):
Enrolled (N=80) → Received treatment (N=69).
From Received treatment (N=69): Fitu 80mg (N=67) and AT-DR (N=2).
From Fitu 80mg (N=67): Rolled over to LTE15174 (N=50).
From AT-DR (N=2): Rolled over to LTE15174 (N=2).
From Rolled over to LTE15174 (N=52): Fitu 80mg (N=50) and AT-DR (N=2).
From Fitu 80mg (N=50): AT-DR (N=47) and AT-DR (N=10).
From AT-DR (N=2): AT-DR (N=1).
TDR14174 (Phase 1 study):
Enrolled (N=42) → Received treatment (N=42).
From Received treatment (N=42): Fitu (N=42).
From Fitu (N=42): Rolled over to LTE14762 (N=34).
From Rolled over to LTE14762 (N=34): Fitu (N=34).
From Fitu (N=34): AT-DR (N=18).
Roll-over studies
LTE15174 (Phase 3 roll-over study):
Enrolled (N=281) → Rolled over (N=227) and China De Novo (N=54).
From Rolled over (N=227): Fitu 80mg (N=180).
From China De Novo (N=54): Fitu 80mg (N=54).
From Fitu 80mg (N=180): AT-DR (N=166), AT-DR (N=37), and AT-DR (N=10).
From Fitu 80mg (N=54): AT-DR (N=54).
LTE14762 (Phase 1/2 roll-over study):
Enrolled (N=34) → Rolled over (N=34).
From Rolled over (N=34): Fitu (N=34).
From Fitu (N=34): AT-DR (N=18).
Pooled safety population who received fitusiranReceived Fitu 80mg (N=270).
Received both Fitu 80mg and AT-DR (N=221).
Received AT-DR only (N=65).
These three groups combine to form the Received Fitu (N=335) and Received AT-DR (N=286) populations.

L2587: Scatter plot titled "Total (Pooled Hemophilia A and B)" showing Maximum Post-baseline Total Bilirubin (xULN) on the Y-axis (0 to 7) versus Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) on the X-axis (0 to 14). Data points are categorized by inhibitor status (Inhibitor: green triangles; Non-inhibitor: purple diamonds).
Key thresholds are marked by dashed lines:
A vertical dashed line is drawn at 3 xULN on the X-axis.
A horizontal dashed line is drawn at 2 xULN on the Y-axis.
Regions are labeled:
Cholestasis: Located in the upper left quadrant (high bilirubin, low ALT/AST).
Potential Hy's Law: Located in the upper right quadrant (high bilirubin, high ALT/AST).
Temple's Corollary: Located in the lower right quadrant (low bilirubin, high ALT/AST).
Two data points are circled in red, indicating potential Hy's Law cases.

A vertical dashed line marks 2x ULN for ALP. A single red circled data point is visible in the upper right quadrant, representing a potential cholestatic DILI case.

2015: IND Submission (6/26/2015)
2016: LTE14762 (9/18/2015)

2019: Revised BMG Guidelines (12/2017)
2020: Voluntary Dosing Pause (10/30/2020); AT-DR Implementation (12/10/2020)

2022: NDA data cut-off (6/14/23)

L2593: Figure 27 displays two graphs showing the Mean (\pmSD) Plasma Concentration-Time Profiles of fitusiran on Day 0 (After First Dose) in Hemophilia Subjects, Study TDR14767, Part D. The graphs compare two doses: MDI 50 mg (n=6) and MDI 80 mg (n=11).
(a) Linear Plot: The Y-axis represents Fitusiran Concentration (ng/mL) on a linear scale (0 to 150). The X-axis represents Time after Dose (h) (0 to 24). Both doses show a rapid increase in concentration, peaking around 6 hours. The MDI 80 mg group achieves a higher peak concentration (around 130 ng/mL) compared to the MDI 50 mg group (around 80 ng/mL). Concentrations decline thereafter.
(b) Semi-Log Plot: The Y-axis represents Fitusiran Concentration (ng/mL) on a semi-log scale (1 to 1000). The X-axis represents Time after Dose (h) (0 to 24). Both doses show similar concentration profiles, with concentrations remaining relatively stable between 100 ng/mL and 1000 ng/mL for the first 12 hours, followed by a gradual decline.
Legend (Dose): MDI 50 mg (n=6) and MDI 80 mg (n=11).
L2594: Figure 28 displays two graphs showing Mean (\pmSD) Plasma Concentration-Time Profiles of fitusiran on Day 56 (After Third QM dose) in Hemophilia Subjects, Study TDR14767, Part D. The X-axis for both graphs is Time after Dose (h), ranging from 0 to 24 hours. The Y-axis is Fitusiran Concentration (ng/mL).
The legend indicates two doses: MDI 50 mg (n=6) and MDI 80 mg (n=11).
(a) Linear Plot: The concentration rises rapidly, peaks around 4-6 hours, and then declines. The MDI 80 mg group shows a higher peak concentration (around 150 ng/mL) compared to the MDI 50 mg group (around 100 ng/mL).
(b) Semi-Log Plot: The concentration rises rapidly, peaks around 4-6 hours, and then declines. The MDI 80 mg group shows a higher peak concentration (around 100 ng/mL) compared to the MDI 50 mg group (around 80 ng/mL).

L2596: Figure 30 is a line graph showing Mean (±SEM) Plasma Antithrombin Activity Relative to Baseline versus Days since first dose (0 to 120). The Y-axis ranges from 0.0 to 1.1. Two dose groups are plotted: MDI 50 mg (N=6, triangles) and MDI 80 mg (N=11, inverted triangles). Both groups show a rapid decline in antithrombin activity from Day 0 (Baseline, approximately 1.0) to a trough around Day 30 (MDI 50 mg ~0.35, MDI 80 mg ~0.25). The 80 mg dose group exhibits a lower trough activity and a slower recovery phase compared to the 50 mg dose group. By Day 120, both groups have recovered to approximately 0.2 relative to baseline.
L2597: Line graph showing Mean (±SEM) Plasma Thrombin Generation Peak Height (nmol/L) over time (Days since first dose, 0 to 120) for five dose groups: MD 0.225 mg/kg (N=3), MD 0.45 mg/kg (N=3), MD 0.9 mg/kg (N=3), MD 1.8 mg/kg (N=3), and MD 80 mg (N=5). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 140 nmol/L. The graph illustrates the temporal profile of thrombin generation peak height for each dose group.
L2598: Jitter Box Plot of Thrombin Generation Peak Height (nmol/L) across five groups: Healthy Volunteers (N=4), AT Lowering <25% (N=4), AT Lowering 25-<50% (N=36), AT Lowering 50-<75% (N=39), and AT Lowering >=75% (N=32). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 240 nmol/L. The plot shows the distribution of peak thrombin values, with the box representing the interquartile range and the median indicated by a line inside the box. The Healthy Volunteers group shows the highest median peak height (around 120 nmol/L).

The graph shows that antithrombin activity generally declines over time, with some variability, and the ADA-induced groups generally maintain lower activity levels than the Not treatment-emergent ADA group.
L2601: Figure 35 is a schematic diagram illustrating the PopK-PD Modeling Strategy. The process starts with Rat Liver PK, which is scaled allometrically to Human Liver PK. Human Liver PK leads to Human Liver RISC PK. Human Liver RISC PK has a dashed feedback loop pointing back to Rat Liver PK. Human Liver RISC PK also inhibits AT Activity (indicated by a negative sign (-)). AT Activity is governed by synthesis rate (K_{syn}) and degradation rate (K_{deg}).
L2602: Figure 36 is a line graph showing the Predicted Steady-State AT Lowering (%) on the Y-axis (ranging from 0 to 100) versus Monthly Dose (mg) on the X-axis (ranging from 0 to 140). The curve shows a sharp decline in AT lowering from 1 mg to 20 mg, followed by a less steep decline up to 50 mg, and then an asymptotic plateau starting around 80 mg. The solid blue line represents the mean AT Lowering response, and the shaded area represents the 90% confidence interval.
L2603: Figure 37 displays three panels showing Model-Simulated AT Activity Profiles over 48 weeks for three dosing regimens: 50 mg Q2M, 50 mg QM, and 80 mg QM. The Y-axis represents AT activity [%] (0 to 130) and the X-axis represents Time (weeks). The legend indicates DOSE: 50 (red/orange line) and 80 (blue/cyan line). The colored lines represent the model simulated median AT activity. Black solid lines represent the model simulated 5th (lower) and 95th (upper) percentile AT activity. Black dashed lines represent the model simulated minimum (lower) and maximum (upper) AT activity. For all regimens, AT activity starts high (around 100-130%), drops sharply around week 4, and then fluctuates around a steady-state level. The 50 mg Q2M regimen shows the highest steady-state AT activity (around 35-40%). The 50 mg QM regimen shows a steady-state AT activity around 25-30%. The 80 mg QM regimen shows the lowest steady-state AT activity (around 15-20%).
L2604: Diagram illustrating the PopK-PD Model for Antithrombin (AT) lowering. The model consists of four compartments: Central Liver (V_2), Peripheral Liver (V_3), RISC (V_2 + V_3), and Antithrombin (AT).
Fitusiran enters the Central Liver compartment via uptake rate constant K_a.
Fitusiran moves between Central Liver and Peripheral Liver compartments via intercompartmental clearance CL_2.
Fitusiran moves from Peripheral Liver to RISC compartment via turnover rate Q.
Fitusiran leaves the RISC compartment via clearance CL_R.
Antithrombin (AT) is synthesized in the RISC compartment via zero-order synthesis rate K_{in}, modulated by the Inhibitory Fitusiran Effect.
Antithrombin (AT) is eliminated from the system via first-order degradation rate constant K_{out}.
The Inhibitory Fitusiran Effect is defined by the equation:
\text{Inhibitory Fitusiran Effect} = 1 - \frac{I_{max} \times C_{RISC}}{I_{C50} + C_{RISC}}
L2605: Figure 39 displays four plots assessing the goodness-of-fit of the Final PopK-PD Model for antithrombin (AT) activity data.
Top Left Plot (Individual Data): Shows Observed AT activity data [%] (Y-axis, 0 to 120) versus Individual Predicted AT activity data [%] (X-axis, 0 to 100). The data points (dots) are scattered around the solid line of identity (Y=X), indicating a good fit for individual data.
Top Right Plot (Population Data): Shows Observed AT activity data [%] (Y-axis, 0 to 100) versus Population Predicted AT activity data [%] (X-axis, 0 to 100). The data points (dots) are scattered around the solid line of identity (Y=X), indicating a good fit for population data.
Bottom Left Plot (Weighted Residual): Shows Weighted Residual (Y-axis, -10 to 20) versus Population Predicted AT activity data [%] (X-axis, 0 to 100). The residuals are randomly scattered around the dashed zero line, suggesting no systematic bias.
Bottom Right Plot (Conditional Weighted Residual): Shows Conditional Weighted Residual (Y-axis, -5 to 10) versus Population Predicted AT activity data [%] (X-axis, 0 to 100). The residuals are randomly scattered around the dashed zero line, suggesting no systematic bias.
L2606: Figure 40: Visual Predictive Checks for Final PopK-PD Model. A scatter plot showing AT activity [%] (Y-axis, 0 to 100) versus Time (hr) (X-axis, 0 to 60000). Grey closed circles represent observations. A black solid line represents the median of observations. Black dashed lines represent the 5th and 95th percentiles of observations. A grey shaded area represents the prediction intervals for the median, 5th, and 95th percentile. Red asterisks indicate time points where observations are outside the prediction intervals.

L2625: Line graph showing Summary Body Weights in Male Mice over 182 days. The Y-axis is Weight in Grams (22.00 to 40.00) and the X-axis is Day (-13 to 182). Five groups are plotted: Group 1 (Reference Item, squares), Group 2 (ALN-AT35C 0.05 mg/kg, circles), Group 3 (ALN-AT35C 0.15 mg/kg, triangles), Group 4 (ALN-AT35C 0.5 mg/kg, inverted triangles), and Group 5 (MNU (positive control) 75 mg/kg, diamonds). All groups show weight gain over time, with Group 1 and Group 5 generally maintaining the highest weights, and Group 4 showing the lowest weight gain.
L2626: Line graph showing body weight (Grams) versus Day for five groups of mice. The Y-axis ranges from 16.00 to 26.00 Grams. The X-axis ranges from Day -13 to Day 182. Group 1 (Reference Item, squares) shows the lowest weight, starting around 18.5g and ending around 20.5g. Groups 5 (ALN-AT35C 0.025 mg/kg, circles), 6 (ALN-AT35C 0.08 mg/kg, triangles), 7 (ALN-AT35C 0.25 mg/kg, diamonds), and 8 (MNU positive control 75 mg/kg, squares) show progressively higher weights, generally increasing from Day 7 to Day 140, and then stabilizing or slightly decreasing. Group 8 (MNU) shows the highest weight, starting around 20.5g and ending around 24.5g.
